14:Tc14,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: elizaOS Launch QA (Project 12)
- Parent tracker or epic: #13406; related #13377, #14358, #15603
- Coordination Discussion: #14292
- Suggested lane tag: codex-ios-cloud-launch

## Scope

Complete the first-party Cloud-only iOS launch contract as one integrated release lane: native Authorization Code + PKCE, Keychain delivery/ACK/recovery/revocation, deployed mobile-auth routes and registration preflight, valid AASA association, fail-closed onboarding recovery, and durable hosted-agent provisioning/backup/teardown behavior. Preserve browser, desktop, Android, and explicitly opted-in development runtime modes.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Native store builds default to Cloud without exposing local/remote choices; explicit development builds retain supported overrides.
- [ ] Mobile authorization uses S256 PKCE, claimed HTTPS callback, inactive-until-ACK credentials, Keychain-only secret storage, exact recovery, and exact-key revocation.
- [ ] Staging and production mobile config routes return the registered first-party contract rather than router 404.
- [ ] AASA origin and Apple CDN serve the exact release app ID and `/auth/callback` with the required headers; iOS claims only `applinks:eliza.app` and does not grant unimplemented shared-web-credential trust.
- [ ] Provisioning teardown and orphan recovery use durable, token-fenced cleanup leases and cannot release allocations before authoritative recount.
- [ ] Backup/restore paths enforce bounded materialization and metadata-first verification without worker heap exhaustion.
- [ ] Staging passes clean-install auth, provisioning, recovery, revocation, teardown, and a zero-restart soak before production rollout.
- [ ] Browser, desktop, and Android compatibility suites remain green.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: attach reviewed iOS clean-install before/after screenshots, MP4 walkthrough, and app visual-audit output.
- [ ] Logs: attach sanitized Simulator network/native logs, staging API logs, provisioning-worker logs, and restart/memory observations.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - this change does not alter prompts, model selection, providers, or agent action semantics.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: attach migration journal/execution proof, mobile grant/API-key lifecycle rows, cleanup outbox rows, and container teardown evidence.
- [ ] CI or local commands: attach package tests/typechecks, router/SDK audits, migration execution tests, app audit, native build, staging canary, and deployment preflight results.

## Claim / Status Notes

The `codex-ios-cloud-launch` lane is consolidating an already-coordinated worktree, rebasing it onto current `develop`, and will publish a draft PR before staging deployment. Shared production levers remain untouched until staging gates pass.

The independently reviewed AASA prerequisite is draft PR #16512. Its production trust document stays outside the `develop`-published Pages tree, and `webcredentials` was removed because this launch flow implements an OAuth callback, not shared web credentials.
15:T103f,## Problem

Eliza currently has no canonical live lane that proves a **managed dedicated** Cloud agent can be provisioned on staging, answer a real proof-token chat turn through both bridge and SSE paths, and be cleaned up without silently exercising the shared tier.

This is a correctness/coverage bug, not a latency optimization. Several existing lanes appear to cover this path but do not:

- `hetzner-e2e.yml` is the only current workflow that combines a real proof-token `message.send` turn with raw-server teardown. It is blocked by the rejected isolated `HCLOUD_TOKEN_CI` (HTTP 401; #15616). The broader `HCLOUD_APPS_TOKEN` must not replace that scoped credential.
- `cloud-provisioning-e2e.mjs` and `live-cloud-provision-smoke.ts` create agents without `alwaysOn`/an equivalent dedicated-tier request. Current tier selection therefore defaults them to `shared`, even though their comments call the result Hetzner-backed.
- `cloud-provisioning-e2e.mjs` proves only `/api/status|health|me`; it does not send a real chat turn or delete the fresh agent.
- `live-cloud-provision-smoke.ts` has proof-token bridge/SSE checks and cleanup, but no workflow invokes it. With the current shared default, its pairing-token assertion is incompatible with the created tier. SIWE cleanup also leaves its disposable organization behind.
- `app-live-e2e.yml` wires `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY`, while the repository has `ELIZACLOUD_API_KEY`. Recent cloud-login/provision/chat jobs report green with the sole Playwright test skipped because the key is empty (for example job `86628737358`). This is green-by-skip, not live evidence.
- `launch-hardening-8756.yml` and the mobile Cloud provisioning job are status-only and do not prove dedicated chat or full cleanup. Recent mobile success reused a shared agent and completed in about 1.3 seconds.

The staging Cloud bearer itself is healthy: the latest Hetzner E2E reached and passed the Cloud auth preflight before the independent Hetzner-token 401. Staging `/api/health` also serves the expected current commit. The missing piece is truthful harness wiring.

## Why this matters

Without a fail-closed dedicated lane, shared-runtime success and skipped tests can be mistaken for proof of the Worker → dedicated router → Headscale → agent path. That leaves the final acceptance in #15347 and the dedicated leg of #16079 unverified.

## Acceptance criteria

- Add one manual/scheduled staging workflow that uses the existing managed staging provisioner and the existing repository Cloud credential; do not require or substitute the broader apps-project Hetzner token.
- Create a unique canary explicitly as dedicated (`alwaysOn: true` or the current canonical equivalent) and require the returned tier/state to be dedicated, running, database-ready, heartbeat-fresh, and mesh-addressed.
- Fail closed if the credential is missing, a test skips, the response is a canned/fabricated fallback, or the actual tier/path differs from the declared dedicated path.
- Send nonce-bearing real turns through both central bridge and dedicated SSE. Require clean completion and proof match.
- Capture privacy-safe timings/trace/path fields only; never emit credentials, prompts, model output, raw provider bodies, agent IDs, or hostnames.
- In `finally`, delete only the unique agent created by the run and clean up the disposable user/organization. Cleanup failure must fail the lane or leave an explicit reaper artifact.
- Add a no-unbounded-autoscale/cost guard or an explicitly bounded canary-capacity contract.
- Fix the App Live secret-name drift and make a zero-executed-test/skip outcome red for the declared Cloud chat job.
- Correct stale “Hetzner-backed” comments/tests so shared and dedicated coverage cannot be confused.
- Link a successful exact-deploy-SHA run back to #15347 and #16079.

## Evidence

- Latest fail-closed raw Hetzner run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29184646484
- Prior manual credential recheck: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29164481453
- Related credential blocker: #15616
- Dedicated ingress acceptance tracker: #15347
- End-to-end telemetry tracker: #16079
16:T5e3,## Problem

`composeState` serializes embedding-gated providers ahead of every Stage-1 call. On a direct-Cerebras deployment (fast models, telemetry below), the context-assembly floor is **0.7–3.4s per turn before the routing model even starts**:

```
 total  ttreply  stage1  embed  compose  relconv  chatctx
  3351     3156    1176   1984     1600     1598      794
  6042     5853    1650   1012     2329     1963     1442
  7375     7212    1666   1447     2445     2131     1345
  4478     4232    1804   2559     1834     1833      538
  6322     6047    2010   1579     2248     1789     2243
```
(`InferenceTiming` spans; median composeState 1.2s across 20 live turns; the two ~15ms rows are reused-state turns, showing the ceiling when assembly is skipped.)

`model:TEXT_EMBEDDING` runs ×2 (query embedding for `relevant-conversations` + one more) and `PLATFORM_CHAT_CONTEXT` adds 0.3–3.4s. On a 1.2s-stage-1 backend this floor is 40–60% of time-to-reply.

## Ask

Make the embedding-gated providers non-blocking or degrading: kick off `relevant-conversations` (and its embeddings) in parallel with the fast providers and give it a soft deadline — when it misses, compose without it rather than serializing every turn behind it. `PLATFORM_CHAT_CONTEXT` deserves the same soft-deadline treatment.

Telemetry: captured on a live Discord agent, Cerebras direct (gemma-4-31b small / zai-glm-4.7 large), develop @ 3e47d12ba03.

17:Ta40,## The layer under #15205
With the CF wildcard + TLS fixed (#15205), the app's per-agent calls now correctly reach `https://<id>.staging.elizacloud.ai` on the STAGING worker (verified: valid `*.staging.elizacloud.ai` cert, request lands on the staging cloud-api worker, no longer the prod one). But the chat still dead-ends: every `/api/*` call returns **HTTP 404 from Cloudflare with no CORS header** → browser reports `No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin'`.

## Root cause (systemic, not per-agent)
The worker's `handleDedicatedAgentProxy` → `proxyToOrigin` forwards to `AGENT_ROUTER_ORIGIN_HOST = eliza-staging-1.elizacloud.ai` (the staging CP). The CP runs **nginx** (:80/:443) which routes `<id>.staging.elizacloud.ai` to the agent over the **headscale tailnet** via the agent's `headscale_ip`. But:
- **All 48 running staging agents have `headscale_ip` NULL/empty** (`SELECT (headscale_ip IS NULL OR headscale_ip='') , count(*) FROM agent_sandboxes WHERE status='running'` → `t | 48`).
- **`headscale nodes list` on the CP shows NO online agent-tagged node** — only `cp-staging-router` (tag:eliza-proxy, online) and two `robot-tstest`/`robot-tstest2` (tag:agent) **offline since 2026-06-16**.

So staging agent containers are provisioned + running + serving on their node bridge (`bridge_url` is set, e.g. `http://91.98.38.74:19006`), but they **never join the headscale mesh**, so the CP router has no tailnet address to reach them → 404. The agent's own CORS (`resolveCorsOrigin`, which allows any origin when `ELIZA_CLOUD_PROVISIONED=1`) is never reached because the request dies at the CP hop.

## Why it surfaced only now
Nobody ever got a staging agent reachable end-to-end before tonight (DB split #15160 → image boot #15196 → wildcard/TLS #15205 all had to land first). This is the next distinct gap in the same 'staging never fully mirrored prod' class, adjacent to the known prod-parity headscale gap.

## What to investigate
Why staging agent containers don't join headscale: is the tailscale-up / headscale pre-auth-key step injected at provision time (the staging worker HAS `HEADSCALE_PUBLIC_URL`/`HEADSCALE_API_URL`/`HEADSCALE_USER=tunnel`) actually running in the container, and is the provisioning worker recording `headscale_ip` back to `agent_sandboxes`? Either the join is failing/skipped or the IP is never persisted. Fixing it should light up all 48 agents at once.

## Scope
Systemic (48/48 agents), needs the headscale/tailnet provisioning path debugged — an infra workstream, not a config toggle. #15205's edge routing is done and verified; this is the CP→agent hop. [stan-cloud]18:T8b1,## What (admin action, exact)

If shaw prefers to land #12719 directly, that supersedes the `develop` half of this issue — but `main` protection is needed either way, and this issue is the 5-minute fallback that stops red merges **now** without waiting on the merge-queue workflow prerequisite.

19:T939,## Problem

`zai-glm-4.7` — the default `TEXT_LARGE` model on Cerebras since #16261 — is a hybrid reasoning model (streams a separate `reasoning` delta channel), but `isReasoningModel()` in `plugins/plugin-openai/models/text.ts` does not match it. Two consequences on a local Cerebras runtime (`ELIZA_PROVIDER=cerebras`, `OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL=zai-glm-4.7`):

1. The Cerebras-mode `reasoning_effort: "low"` default (added for exactly this failure on `gpt-oss-120b`) never applies to GLM, so its reasoning runs unbounded.
2. `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"` — set by core's planner loop (`packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts:1549`), Stage-1 formatting calls (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:8770`), and the app-model-tester — is silently ignored by plugin-openai, while plugin-elizacloud maps it to each Cerebras model's suppression value (`resolveCerebrasThinkingOffReasoningEffort`, `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts:366`).

With a capped `max_tokens` the model spends the entire budget on hidden reasoning and returns **empty visible content** with `finish_reason: "length"`.

## Reproduction (live Cerebras, wire-captured via a logging proxy)

`POST /api/model-tester/run {"test":"text-large"}` on a default local Cerebras setup, 8 runs. Every request went out as `{"model":"zai-glm-4.7","max_tokens":160}` with **no** `reasoning_effort`:

```
4x  finish_reason:"length"  reasoning_tokens:160/160  → "TEXT_LARGE returned empty text"
4x  finish_reason:"stop"    reasoning_tokens:62–157   → "pong"
```

~50% failure, purely dependent on how long GLM happens to reason. Direct curl confirms the mechanism: `reasoning_effort:"low"` bounds reasoning (~20–30 tokens), and `reasoning_effort:"none"` (accepted by gemma/GLM, rejected by gpt-oss) eliminates it entirely (`reasoning_tokens: 0`).

## Fix

PR incoming, mirroring plugin-elizacloud's settled semantics:

- add the GLM family to `isReasoningModel()` so the Cerebras `"low"` default applies;
- honor `eliza.thinking === "off"` in Cerebras mode by mapping it per model: `"none"` for gemma/GLM, `"low"` for gpt-oss; explicit caller `providerOptions.openai.reasoningEffort` still wins.

After the fix (same wire capture): 10/10 probes return content, `finish_reason:"stop"`, `reasoning_tokens: 0`.

- Suggested lane tag: codex-transactional-npm

## Scope

Replace the current single-job npm release script with a retry-safe, least-privilege workflow that stages and verifies the complete immutable package cohort before any public channel or GitHub release advances.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The npm workflow requires explicit `source_sha`, exact semver `version`, and `channel`; tag-push and `release:created` publication paths are removed.

- [ ] Publish jobs download the verified tarball cohort and never rebuild or mutate source/manifests.

- [ ] Finalization pushes only the exact planned `refs/tags/vX`; matching tags are idempotent and conflicting tags fail. `--follow-tags`, `-X theirs`, `git add -A`, and direct branch pushes are absent.

After #16156 removes the two external-Redis ingress stalls, the exact-head staging run still shows a separate cold authentication tail: two of five cold gateway requests spent about 3.2 seconds in the route auth interval before any organization limit, billing reservation, or provider call.

This is not model generation and is distinct from the monolithic Worker bootstrap tracked in #16155.

## Exact evidence

Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29171752918
Artifact: chat-latency-paired-staging-5710071096eff7a62c13ec1f7d9bd3fdfa10a110

All 320 requests returned HTTP 200 and clean streams. The two cold auth tails were:

| Case | Placement | Gateway headers | Route total | Auth | Mid | Reserve |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| GLM-4.7, none, 512 | remote-EWR | 5,247 ms | 5,016 ms | 3,209 ms | 1,128 ms | 605 ms |
| GLM-4.7, omit, 4096 | local-ORD | 5,218 ms | 4,000 ms | 3,204 ms | 481 ms | 185 ms |

The other three cold auth intervals were 3-4 ms. Warm auth was 4 ms p50 / 5 ms p95 with a 585 ms max. Across the five 30-second post-idle cases it was 100 ms p50 and 1,353 ms max.

The same gateway credential is reused across cases. Severe cold auth occurred under both local and remote placement, so geography alone does not explain it.

## Current path

The chat handler first calls resolveInferenceAuthContext. For an API key it:

1. reads the inference auth context from the shared cache;
2. on a hit, returns the fully authorized user/org/key context;
3. on a miss, runs requireAuthOrApiKeyWithOrg;
4. checks moderation authoritatively; and
5. writes the positive context back to cache.

Worker auto-cache configuration prefers the bound CACHE_KV namespace. Therefore the current evidence proves a cache-hit versus authoritative-path split, but does not yet identify whether the 3.2 seconds are KV read/miss behavior, database auth/org queries, moderation, the cache write, or cold dependency/connection setup. #16079 must name those boundaries before optimizing.

Relevant code:
- packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-auth-context.ts
- packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-auth-cache.ts
- packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/cache/client.ts
- packages/cloud/api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts

## Required work

- Add privacy-safe correlated timings for credential extraction, cache availability, KV hit/miss/invalid, authoritative key lookup, user/org lookup, moderation, and cache write.
- Record only bounded source/result enums and timings; never log presented keys, hashes, auth headers, prompts, or user-controlled identifiers.
- Reproduce cache hits and controlled cache misses in staging across fresh Worker versions/placements.
- Optimize the proven slow boundary. Candidates include a single authoritative join, parallel independent checks, Hyperdrive/connection placement, or a strongly consistent edge auth primitive. Choose from evidence.
- Preserve fail-closed invalid/revoked/inactive/suspended behavior and exact 401/403 taxonomy. Do not add an un-invalidated positive L1 cache or relax revocation to reduce latency.

## Acceptance

- One trace distinguishes cache hit, cache miss, cache unavailable, and every authoritative sub-hop.
- At least 30 fresh-version/cache-hit probes and 10 controlled staging cache-miss probes are retained with exact deployed SHA.
- Cache-hit auth p95 stays below 50 ms and has no requests above 250 ms.
- Controlled authoritative auth has no multi-second tail and improves by at least 50% at p90 versus the current evidence.
- API-key revoke/update/delete, user/org deactivate, moderation suspension, malformed cache entries, and cache outage retain existing fail-closed semantics and tests.
- No secret, credential hash, prompt, or unbounded user input enters logs or telemetry.

1e:T1350,## Problem

#16207 intentionally changed `NotificationService.start()` to fail when persisted notification state cannot be read instead of treating an unreadable durable inbox as a healthy empty inbox (the acceptance criterion in #16206).

That fail-closed contract is currently lost at the next boundary:

- `packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.ts` returns HTTP 200 with `{ notifications: [], unreadCount: 0 }` for `GET /api/notifications` whenever `runtime.getService(ServiceType.NOTIFICATION)` is absent.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.ts` has the same fallback for the Android UDS route.
- Neither route distinguishes an intentionally disabled/unregistered service from `pending`, `registering`, or `failed` service state. After a cache-read failure, `AgentRuntime._runServiceStart()` marks the service `failed` and leaves no instance, so both routes convert the failure back into a healthy empty inbox.
- `packages/ui/src/state/notifications/notification-store.ts` hydrates once. On a transport/5xx failure it logs, sets `hydrated: true`, preserves the live WebSocket subscription, but schedules no HTTP hydration retry. A transient startup race or recovered persistence layer can therefore leave persisted history absent for the whole UI session.
- The HTTP route comment says the UI “degrades gracefully and retries,” but no such retry currently exists.

This is a fail-closed integration/recovery gap. It is **not** the packaged App Live native-notification failure: that test injects a mock WebSocket `agent_event`, does not start core `NotificationService`, and the observed failing run predates #16207.

## Required behavior

1. Preserve #16207's fail-closed persistence semantics. Do not restore the blanket `hydrate()` catch that turns an unreadable inbox into empty state.
2. Give notification routes enough runtime lifecycle information to distinguish:
   - intentionally disabled/unregistered notification support;
   - `pending` / `registering` startup;
   - `failed` startup;
   - a registered service instance.
3. For `pending` / `registering`, return a typed retryable 503 response (for example `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_NOT_READY`) and a bounded `Retry-After` value instead of HTTP 200 empty.
4. For `failed`, return a typed 503 response (for example `NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_FAILED`) without leaking adapter/secrets/internal error details. Never represent failed persistence as an empty successful inbox.
5. Define the intentional disabled/headless contract explicitly. If that case remains HTTP 200 empty, it must be positively identified rather than inferred from a missing service instance.
6. Add bounded, jittered/background UI hydrate retry that:
   - honors `Retry-After` when present;
   - does not clear already received notifications;
   - keeps the live WebSocket subscription active;
   - avoids duplicate subscriptions/delivery;
   - stops after a documented cap and exposes/logs the terminal hydrate error rather than silently reading as healthy empty.
7. Add explicit, single-flight service recovery/backoff for transient notification hydration failures. Recovery may live at the runtime service boundary or in a dedicated route/service coordinator, but requests must not create a retry stampede. A recovered cache adapter must be able to rehydrate persisted history without restarting the entire agent.
8. Apply equivalent semantics to the Android UDS notification route.
9. Emit enough structured diagnostics/telemetry to distinguish disabled, not-ready, failed, retrying, recovered, and terminal-retry-exhausted states.

## Tests

- `packages/core/src/services/notification.test.ts`
  - unreadable cache still fails startup;
  - a transient cache-read failure can recover through the chosen explicit recovery path;
  - concurrent recovery requests are single-flight / bounded.
- `packages/agent/src/api/notification-routes.test.ts`
  - pending/registering GET is typed 503 + `Retry-After`, not 200 empty;
  - failed GET is typed 503, not 200 empty;
  - intentionally disabled/unregistered behavior is explicit;
  - registered service returns persisted rows.
- `packages/ui/src/state/notifications/notification-store.test.ts` (or the canonical store test file)
  - bounded retry and `Retry-After` handling with fake timers;
  - state received over WS is preserved while HTTP hydrate retries;
  - successful recovery merges/sets persisted history without duplicate delivery;
  - terminal exhaustion is observable and does not create an infinite timer/subscription loop.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/dispatch.test.ts`
  - lifecycle-state response parity with the HTTP route;
  - recovered service serves persisted rows.

## Non-goals

- Do not conflate this with the separate App Live packaged native-notification test failure.
- Do not mask cache/adapter failures as empty state.
- Do not retry every incoming request independently.

1f:Tc4e,## Problem

`develop` currently has no active branch ruleset or classic protection requiring PR checks. The lightweight `Develop PR` workflow and the evidence/security checks are advisory, so maintainers and agents can squash-merge before any result exists—or after a result is red.

This is no longer hypothetical. On 2026-07-13:

- #16215 opened at 02:19:20Z and merged at 02:19:55Z, 35 seconds later, before CI completed. Its evidence check subsequently failed because seven required evidence rows were absent. The body was repaired post-merge and the evidence rerun then passed.
- #16202 merged with only Auto Label checks on the PR. Its real validation is happening post-merge on push to `develop`.
- Both code changes appear safe after independent diff review, but safety was luck/review, not enforcement.

The obsolete merge-queue tracker #13386 already records that #14194 deleted ruleset 18511845 and left `develop` unprotected. #14051 still describes the old queue/`ci-ok` architecture and should not be treated as current enforcement documentation.

## Risk

A PR with a type error, broken build, incomplete deterministic lane classification, secret leak, stale base, or red evidence can be merged before GitHub reports it. Post-merge CI detects damage only after `develop` and downstream deploy triggers have advanced.

## Proposed safe shape

1. Add one stable hosted-runner aggregate check for the current lightweight PR gate. It must run with `if: always()` and fail unless the required component jobs reached allowed terminal states.
2. Include at minimum:
   - lint
   - typecheck
   - build
   - deterministic lane coverage
   - stale-base guard
   - PR title/evidence
   - security/gitleaks
   - changed-file coverage when applicable
3. Install an active `develop` ruleset requiring that stable aggregate, PR review flow, deletion protection, and non-fast-forward protection.
4. Do not restore the removed heavy merge queue or make scheduled/exhaustive/device/aesthetic lanes required.
5. Give emergency bypass only to explicitly named maintainers, audit every bypass, and never grant an automation-wide silent bypass.
6. Add a low-risk green canary and an intentionally red canary under active monitoring to prove enforcement before closing.

## Acceptance

- A PR cannot merge while the aggregate is pending, missing, cancelled, timed out, or failed.
- A path-skipped component is classified explicitly rather than making the aggregate disappear.
- A normal focused PR reaches a mergeable state without waiting for heavy post-merge suites.
- A red canary is visibly blocked; a green canary merges after the required aggregate succeeds.
- Ruleset JSON, exact canary PRs/runs, and rollback instructions are attached.
- #13386 is closed as obsolete or redirected here; #14051 is updated to distinguish throughput work from enforcement.

## Evidence

- #16215: merged before checks; post-merge evidence failure and later repaired rerun.
- #16202: merged before standard PR validation; post-merge push is the first full proof.
- #13386 maintainer audit: current `develop` rules query returned no rules and classic protection false.

24:Tabe,**Describe the bug**

The resumable local-inference downloader cannot recover when a process exits after a bundle file has been fully written to its `.part` path but before the atomic rename into the bundle directory.

On restart, the sidecar SHA still matches the current manifest, so `resumableStartByte` returns the complete file size. The downloader sends `Range: bytes=<size>-`; a compliant server answers `416 Range Not Satisfiable`. The job fails while leaving the same complete partial in place, so every retry repeats the same 416. A valid multi-GB transfer is permanently stuck until the staging file is deleted manually.

**To Reproduce**

1. Put the complete expected bytes at the bundle file staging path.
2. Put the manifest SHA256 in the adjacent `.expected` sidecar.
3. Start the bundle download against an HTTP endpoint that returns 416 for a range beginning at EOF.
4. Observe the download fail before the existing SHA gate can commit the staged file.

Fails on current `develop` (`16a038447`):

<details><summary>RED test output</summary>

```text
FAIL local inference downloader stale-content robustness > commits a complete .part after restart without requesting a range past EOF
Error: HTTP 416 from model hub for elizaos/eliza-1/text/eliza-1-2b-128k.gguf
```

</details>

**Expected behavior**

Before issuing a Range request, a SHA-pinned complete partial should be recognized by its manifest SHA, atomically moved into the bundle, and have its `.expected` sidecar removed. Incomplete matching partials must continue to use normal Range resume.

Acceptance criteria:

- A complete SHA-matching `.part` is committed without another model-file request.

2d:T592,Found while battery-testing every slash command through the GUI chat route (`POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/stream`).

**Repro:** in a conversation with enough history, send `/compact`. The deterministic command layer fires correctly (`thought: "Shortcut: cmd:compact"`), the COMPACT_CONVERSATION action succeeds, and its final text is the designed clean summary ("Compacted N older message(s); preserved the latest M."). But the **visible streamed reply** is the compactor's *internal* model call output — the raw rendered-ledger JSON:

```
```json
{ "state": { "facts": [ "…extracted facts from the conversation…" ] …
```

**Cause (as far as I traced):** the SSE route streams model tokens for the turn, and an action's *internal* `runtime.useModel` calls (here `buildActionCompactorModelCall`) stream into the same visible channel, so intermediate model output masquerades as the reply and the action's actual final text is buried. Any action that makes internal model calls on the GUI stream path has the same exposure. Discord/connector paths are unaffected (they deliver the discrete `reply` only).

This is the same family as the narration-envelope leaks (#8162): internal artifacts crossing into user-visible output. Expected behavior: mid-action internal model calls should not stream to the client; only the turn's actual reply should.

- [Anthropic: Adaptive thinking](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/adaptive-thinking)

- Define and document how existing numeric `ANTHROPIC_COT_BUDGET` / `anthropicThinkingBudgetTokens` settings interact with adaptive-only models. Do not silently describe `effort` as a hard token cap.

| Effective thinking budget | Reserved/provider cap |

On current `develop` (`7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c`):

- [`packages/cloud/api/agents/[id]/a2a/route.ts:277-293`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/api/agents/%5Bid%5D/a2a/route.ts#L277-L293) resolves the Anthropic thinking budget, derives `maxOutputTokens = effectiveThinkingBudget + 500`, and prices the reservation with it.
- [`route.ts:323-332`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/7f0a2247ecebf2c586af72e0cfdaa1ead1f0df2c/packages/cloud/api/agents/%5Bid%5D/a2a/route.ts#L323-L332) calls `streamText` without `maxOutputTokens`.

The provider can therefore consume more output than the amount admitted by the upfront reservation. Final reconciliation may report an uncollected overage only after upstream inference has already been purchased.

This is the live `agents/[id]/a2a` route, not the exported-but-unwired shared dispatcher tracked historically in #12979.

## Related work

- #16081 establishes the broader invariant that billing admission must use the exact provider-facing budget.
- #16099 currently changes only `/v1/chat/completions`; it does not cover this A2A route.

## Scope

- Derive one admitted/provider-facing output cap for A2A chat.
- Pass the identical value to `estimateRequestCost` and `streamText.maxOutputTokens`.
- Preserve character settings, environment defaults, and `ANTHROPIC_COT_BUDGET_MAX` clamping.
- Keep reserve-before-provider ordering and fail before provider dispatch when credits are insufficient.

## Acceptance

- [ ] The exact value used to price/reserve A2A chat is also sent as `streamText.maxOutputTokens`.
- [ ] Character override, environment default, and environment-capped budget cases are covered.
- [ ] Insufficient-credit admission performs no provider call.
- [ ] Final usage cannot exceed the admitted ceiling because the provider option was omitted.
- [ ] The existing creator-markup and exactly-once reconciliation behavior remains intact.

## Regression tests

Extend `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/agent-a2a-billing.test.ts` with a non-null thinking budget and assert both:

1. `estimateRequestCost(..., admittedMaxOutputTokens)`; and
2. `streamText({ ..., maxOutputTokens: admittedMaxOutputTokens })`.

The test must drive the mounted live route, not a copied helper or the unwired shared A2A dispatcher.

## Scope

Package-dist assembly permits non-canonical local state to affect published output in two ways:

1. `prepare-package-dist.mjs` recursively discovers manifests. A tracked nested manifest such as `plugins/plugin-sql/src/package.json` can repeat a canonical workspace name with a different version and impersonate the workspace root during dependency rewriting.
2. `copy-package-assets.mjs` can copy generated Python cache, coverage, virtual-environment, and package-build residue into publish directories.

Resolve dependency versions through the repository's shared canonical workspace-discovery seam, reject duplicate canonical workspace identities, and exclude generic Python-generated residue while retaining source assets. This scope does not add generated packaging-runtime output rules or change application/runtime behavior.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A nested manifest repeating a canonical workspace name cannot override the canonical workspace version.
- [ ] Duplicate names among canonical workspace roots fail with the conflicting identity and paths.
- [ ] Root workspace inclusions and negations remain the only source of workspace membership.
- [ ] Malformed selected workspace manifests fail rather than disappearing from dependency rewriting.
- [ ] `__pycache__`, `.pyc`/`.pyo`, Python test/type/lint caches, virtual environments, coverage residue, and `*.egg-info` directories are not copied.
- [ ] Ordinary Python source remains present.
- [ ] Focused tests cover the nested collision, duplicate and malformed canonical manifests, workspace negation, cache exclusions, and retained source.
- [ ] Representative agent, app-core, and UI builds and package dry-runs succeed.
- [ ] No package manifests, lockfiles, or workflows change.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - no rendered UI source or user flow changes.
- [ ] Logs: focused tests, shared workspace-seam tests, representative builds, and package dry-runs.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or agent-loop behavior changes.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: generated publish manifests, canonical-versus-emitted dependency versions, package summaries, and scans showing excluded residue.

3a:T47c,## Problem

Once routing is fast, **output tokens are the remaining time-to-reply driver** on chat turns (~3ms/token at the provider): a paragraph-length reply costs 3-6s regardless of how fast the runtime is. Live examples (Cerebras direct, glm-4.7): short replies 1.9-2.4s total; explanation-length replies 4.5-7.4s total with Stage-1 model spans of 4.2-6.8s that are almost entirely visible output generation.

Today the only brevity control is prose in `character.system`, which models honor inconsistently and which can't vary by surface (a Discord group reply wants 1-2 sentences; a DM or docs question can run longer).

## Ask

A structural reply-length budget: per-agent (and ideally per-channel-type) `maxReplyTokens`-style knob that flows into the Stage-1/synthesis calls as a real `max_tokens`-bounded budget plus a rendered length instruction — so operators can pin group-chat turns terse without rewriting personas, and the budget is enforced by the provider rather than requested politely.

Captured on a live Discord agent, Cerebras direct, develop @ 3e47d12ba03.

3b:T55f,## Problem

On Cerebras reasoning models (zai-glm-4.7, gpt-oss-120b), hidden reasoning tokens dominate tail latency — and the `InferenceTiming` telemetry can't see them. Live Stage-1 latencies on the SAME model/prompt-shape ranged **1.2s → 12.8s**:

```
 stage1(ms):  1176  1000  1650  1666   821  12791  6790  4689  2010  1804  10005  1293  4222
```

Direct-API measurement of the discrepancy (same prompt, zai-glm-4.7):
- default: **813ms, 400 reasoning tokens** (of 407 completion tokens)
- `reasoning_effort: "none"`: **158ms, 0 reasoning tokens**

#16299 wires thinking-off suppression for calls that carry `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"`, which covers Stage-1 and the evaluator — but the variance above shows bursts still landing in production, and without reasoning-token counts in the telemetry we can't tell suppressed-but-queued apart from unsuppressed.

## Ask

1. Surface `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` (Cerebras/OpenAI both return it) in `InferenceTiming` spans so reasoning bursts are attributable.
2. Audit which model calls in the turn pipeline do NOT carry `thinking: "off"` (planner iterations, callback rewrites, failure-reply helpers) and bound them on reasoning models the same way.

3c:T1152,## Summary

Every Eliza Cloud email magic link should also contain a six-digit, single-use companion code. A user on a constrained or cross-device client such as LP3 can type the code on the existing login page while the magic link remains available and unchanged.

This is blocked on an additive Steward auth contract. Eliza is only the Steward client/proxy and does not own challenge storage or email rendering.

Signed: **[sol-orch]**

## Recon findings

- Eliza login calls `@stwd/sdk` `StewardAuth.signInWithEmail()` from `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/login/steward-login-section.tsx`.
- `/steward/*` is a signed upstream proxy in `packages/cloud/api/src/steward/embedded.ts`.
- The link callback is `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/auth/email-callback-page.tsx`, which calls `verifyEmailCallback()` and syncs the Steward session cookie.
- Steward owns `POST /auth/email/send`, `POST /auth/email/verify`, token persistence, tenant email templates, and the mail provider.
- Steward already has six-digit email OTP endpoints, but they intentionally return a short-lived `emailGrant` for passkey registration. They do not log the user in.
- Steward also has device-code approval/poll primitives, but those are a separate protocol and are not aliases for an email magic-link challenge.

Calling both existing send methods is not acceptable. It produces independent challenges, may produce two messages, cannot invalidate both credentials when either succeeds, and would broaden the passkey grant's security contract.

## Required upstream dependency

Add an additive Steward combined challenge:

1. `POST /auth/email/send` creates one challenge and sends one email containing the existing link plus a six-digit code.
2. Link-token hash and code verifier resolve to one central, purpose-bound challenge record.
3. The central record is atomically consumed, so a successful link invalidates the code and a successful code invalidates the link, including concurrent attempts.
4. Add `POST /auth/email/code/verify`, then expose an SDK method returning the normal `StewardAuthResult | StewardMfaRequiredResult`.
5. Leave `sendEmailOtp`, `verifyEmailOtp`, and `emailGrant` behavior unchanged.

Security requirements:

- server-side cryptographic random generation,
- short shared TTL, recommended 10 minutes,
- hash/keyed verifier at rest, no plaintext token/code,
- bound to normalized email, tenant, and `email-login` purpose,
- atomic single use and cross-invalidation,
- per-IP plus per-challenge/target rate limits,
- maximum five failed attempts and hard lock,
- constant-time verifier compare where applicable,
- no code, email, raw token, or full challenge alias in logs,
- no account-enumeration differences,
- normal tenant, SSO, allowed-method, and MFA gates.

## Eliza acceptance criteria after Steward SDK release

- Existing link callback remains unchanged and valid.
- The `email-sent` state includes optional copy: `Enter the 6-digit code from your email`.
- Accessible input uses `inputMode="numeric"`, `autoComplete="one-time-code"`, numeric paste sanitization, `maxLength={6}`, and an explicit label.
- Code success uses the existing `handleSuccess()` session/cookie flow.
- Explicit loading, invalid, expired, locked/too-many-attempts, and transport-error states.
- Resend has a visible cooldown and requests a fresh combined challenge.
- Existing `packages/ui` tokens and Lucide icons only, with LP3 touch sizing.
- Focused backend, SDK, email-template/provider, UI, callback regression, and LP3 viewport tests.

3e:T8c7,## Summary

With `LOG_FILE=true`, the logger opens `output.log`, `prompts.log`, and `chat.log`, but `output.log` stays at 0 bytes forever. `prompts.log`/`chat.log` (via `logPrompt`/`logChatIn` etc.) work fine.

## Root cause

`stripAnsi` in `packages/logger/src/logger.ts` (line 376 on current `develop`) builds an invalid regex — the last alternative has one escape level too many:

```ts
const ESC = "\x1b";
const BEL = "\x07";
// throws SyntaxError: Invalid regular expression: Unterminated group
new RegExp(
  `${ESC}(?:\\[[\\x20-\\x3F]*[\\x40-\\x7E]|\\].*?(?:${BEL}|${ESC}\\\\|\\\\(B))`,
  "g",
);
```

`\\\\(B` cooks to the regex source `\\(B` — an escaped *backslash* followed by an **unclosed group** `(B` — so the outer `(?:` never closes and `new RegExp` throws on **every** call. The intended pattern is clearly `\(B` (the `ESC ( B` charset-designation sequence), i.e. one escape level less.

`writeLogEntryToFile` is the only caller of `stripAnsi`, and its bare `catch {}` swallows the `SyntaxError`, so every entry write dies silently: the console/Adze path and the in-memory ring buffer still work, the three files still get opened by `ensureFileLog`, and `output.log` never receives a byte. As far as I can tell this has never worked. The prompt/chat writers don't call `stripAnsi`, which is why they're unaffected.

## Repro

1. `LOG_FILE=true` + run any agent → `output.log` is created but stays 0 bytes while the console logs normally.
2. Or just paste the `new RegExp` snippet above into `bun repl` / `node` — it throws.

## Fix

```diff
     const re = new RegExp(
-      `${ESC}(?:\\[[\\x20-\\x3F]*[\\x40-\\x7E]|\\].*?(?:${BEL}|${ESC}\\\\|\\\\(B))`,
+      `${ESC}(?:\\[[\\x20-\\x3F]*[\\x40-\\x7E]|\\].*?(?:${BEL}|${ESC}\\\\|\\(B))`,
       "g",
     );
```

Verified locally by applying this to the built bundle: the regex compiles, SGR sequences are stripped correctly (`"\x1b[36mInfo\x1b[39m hi"` → `"Info hi"`), and `output.log` immediately starts receiving timestamped entries.

Worth considering separately: `writeLogEntryToFile`'s silent `catch {}` hid a 100%-reproducible failure for the sink's entire lifetime — a one-time stderr warning on the first swallowed error would have surfaced this instantly.
3f:Td5a,## Problem

The browser PCM recorder drops every frame until the VAD detector begins buffering. `DEFAULT_LOCAL_ASR_AUTO_STOP.startGraceMs` is 250 ms, and frames below the speech thresholds are also discarded before the first detected speech frame.

Current code:
- detector: `packages/ui/src/voice/local-asr-capture.ts:189-269`
- recorder buffer gate: `packages/ui/src/voice/local-asr-capture.ts:519-529`

On an iOS/WebKit AudioContext running at 48 kHz, the current 4096-frame ScriptProcessor callback is about 85 ms. A user who starts speaking as soon as the mic becomes live can therefore lose multiple leading callbacks—the first syllable or a short first word—before full-WAV batch transcription.

An abandoned branch proposed a 200 ms bounded PCM pre-roll in commit https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/commit/76ad66201ba2c2c53e624b9bdd61e51a80e3f9ea. Do not cherry-pick that commit wholesale: it also shortened the existing 12-second utterance cap to four seconds without device evidence.

## Scope

- Keep a bounded rolling PCM buffer while auto-stop VAD has not begun buffering.
- When real speech is first detected, prepend the retained pre-roll exactly once before the first speech frame.
- Bound the ring in milliseconds using the actual AudioContext sample rate (target default: 200 ms).
- Clear it on stop, cancel, and teardown; memory must not grow with idle time.
- Apply through the shared WAV recorder so both local-inference and cloud batch ASR benefit.
- Keep the current `maxSpeechMs: 12_000` policy. A shorter cap requires separate product approval and real-device evidence.

## Non-goals

No streaming-ASR/realtime-WebSocket changes, provider/route changes, WAV-header normalization, or auto-send/UI redesign.

## Acceptance criteria

- Recorder-level tests drive fake AudioContext callbacks with identifiable pre-grace, pre-threshold, and speech samples, then decode the returned WAV and prove:
  - only the newest bounded pre-roll is retained;
  - it precedes the first detected speech frame;
  - it is flushed exactly once;
  - older frames are discarded.
- Coverage at both 16 kHz and 48 kHz proves duration-based rather than chunk-count-based bounding.
- No-speech input does not promote pre-roll into a valid utterance.
- Capture without auto-stop remains byte-for-byte behaviorally unchanged.
- Echo-gate, trailing-silence, duplicate-stop, cancel, and empty-capture behavior remain green.
- A longer than four-second spoken phrase is retained, proving the 12-second cap was not shortened.
- `bun run --cwd packages/ui test`, relevant voice tests, typecheck/Biome, and `bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app` pass.

## Required real evidence

On current installed builds, test iOS PWA and desktop Chrome with phrases whose first word starts immediately after mic activation, including short/plosive openers such as “Turn…”, “Set…”, and “Play…”.

Attach and manually review:

- before/after raw WAVs and transcripts for the same phrase set;
- evidence that the leading word is present after the change;
- a 5–8 second utterance proving it is not cut at four seconds;
- screen recording with audio;
- browser network/console logs and matching backend STT logs;
- WAV sample rate, duration, RIFF/data sizes, and first voiced-sample timing.

The collision guard only covers similes matching a real parent NAME, not similes claimed by two parents.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)43:Ta5d,## Summary

The `eliza-code-acp` first-use workspace provisioning path needs a deliberately designed cross-process build protocol. A proposed `O_EXCL` lock implementation was rejected during review and was **not** integrated because it still allowed concurrent/partial builds and broke paths containing spaces.

This is separate from the deterministic CI fix in #16167: mocked-native orchestrator tests now inject `ELIZA_ELIZAOS_ACP_COMMAND` and no longer enter real provisioning accidentally.

## Rejected implementation findings

- A hard-link/inode stale-reclaim check remained TOCTOU: two waiters could both validate stale lock A; waiter 1 could replace it with lock B; waiter 2 could then delete B using its already-authorized unlink.
- A wall-clock stale threshold could steal from a verified-live owner. `spawnSync()` may remain blocked after its timeout if the child traps `SIGTERM`, so age alone cannot prove ownership ended. PID reuse also makes liveness-only checks ambiguous.
- The mtime fast path could bypass a still-held lock as soon as a partial `dist/acp.js` appeared. A crash after touching/truncating the file could leave a permanently false-fresh artifact.
- The generated native command was a single unquoted string; `splitCommandLine()` breaks Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## Required design properties

- [ ] Deterministic multi-process exclusion with fencing or an equivalent proven primitive.
- [ ] No stealing from a verified-live owner based only on wall-clock age.
- [ ] Replacement-lock-safe cleanup; an old owner/waiter can never delete a successor's lock.
- [ ] Build to a temporary artifact, validate it, and publish atomically under the lock (or use an equivalent completion marker tied to the artifact).
- [ ] Crash/dead-owner recovery without PID-reuse ambiguity.
- [ ] One shared deadline across waiting and building, with bounded status/signal/stdout/stderr diagnostics.
- [ ] Structured `{ command, args }` propagation, or equivalently robust path handling, including workspaces/Bun binaries with spaces.

## Required tests

- [ ] Multiple processes reclaim one dead lock; exactly one fake build runs.
- [ ] A holder writes `partial` while keeping the lock; a waiter cannot return until the validated complete artifact is atomically published.
- [ ] A live owner beyond the nominal wait budget is never overlapped.
- [ ] Crash recovery and PID-reuse simulation.
- [ ] Workspace and Bun paths containing spaces.
- [ ] Failed build never leaves a fresh-looking executable artifact.

Do not solve this by increasing timeouts or reintroducing test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.
44:T6ea,## Summary

`lookup.ts` now resolves independent input/output pricing reads concurrently to remove serial billing-path latency (#16156). On a fresh isolate where both sides miss the completed-value cache, both tasks can reach the same provider catalog fetch before either result is cached.

This is not a billing correctness bug: invalid/missing prices still fail closed and the returned rates are validated. It is a low-priority duplicate-I/O/cold-latency opportunity.

## Observed shape

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/lookup.ts` starts the input and output lookups concurrently.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/ai-pricing/cache.ts` caches completed values, but does not retain an in-flight promise.
- Two same-key cold misses can therefore issue two identical external catalog reads.

## Suggested implementation

Add key-scoped single-flight caching for in-progress catalog resolutions:

- share one promise for concurrent requests for the same canonical provider/model/rate key;
- remove the promise on both fulfillment and rejection;
- keep the existing completed-value TTL cache and fail-closed validation;
- do not let a failed promise poison later retries;
- bound the in-flight map or prove its lifecycle is bounded by completion.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A deterministic test gates the provider/catalog fetch and proves two concurrent same-key cold lookups make exactly one upstream call.
- [ ] Different keys still resolve concurrently.
- [ ] A rejected in-flight fetch is observed by all waiters and a later call retries normally.
- [ ] Invalid/non-finite rates remain rejected; no fallback can under-bill.
- [ ] Typecheck, focused pricing tests, error-policy ratchet, and changed-file coverage pass.

Related: #16156.
45:T8c1,## Problem

`computeEffectiveMaxTokens` currently applies:

`Math.max(request.max_tokens, 4096)`

to every model classified as using reasoning tokens. Both pass-through and SDK paths send that raised value upstream.

A client requesting `max_tokens:512` therefore authorizes an upstream ceiling of 4096 without any response field or header indicating that the request was changed.

The controlled Gemma/GLM latency run discovered this because direct requests used 512 while the gateway silently sent 4096, invalidating the original comparison.

## Why this is delicate

The floor was intentionally introduced by #8189/#8486 to prevent reasoning models from consuming a low budget entirely in hidden reasoning and returning an empty but billed answer. It must not simply be deleted.

Nevertheless, silently increasing an explicit caller ceiling is an API-contract and money-integrity problem: it can permit more generation, reservation, and spend than the caller requested.

## Required decision

Choose and document one safe contract:

1. Preserve an explicit caller cap; only apply a default floor when the field is absent.
2. Reject a reasoning-enabled request whose cap is too small with a clear 4xx.
3. Permit a low cap when reasoning is explicitly disabled.
4. Introduce explicit answer-vs-reasoning budget semantics.

Whichever contract is selected, expose requested and effective values in bounded telemetry and ensure billing admission uses the same effective value sent upstream.

## Acceptance

- An explicit output ceiling is never silently increased.
- Low-budget reasoning requests cannot regress to misleading `finish_reason:"stop"` with empty billed content.
- GLM-4.7 with `reasoning_effort:"none"` can use a genuinely small budget.
- Streaming and non-streaming routes behave consistently.
- Reservations and final billing are tested against the exact provider-facing budget.
- The benchmark harness records both requested and effective budgets.
- Compatibility behavior is documented.

## References

- Original broad floor: #8189
- Launch-model detection extension: #8486
- Equivalent `/v1/messages` history: #12864 / #12865
- Cerebras Chat Completions:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/api-reference/chat-completions
46:Tcf6,**Describe the bug**

A clean agent boot can advertise a settled/healthy deferred boot while startup work has failed. Three related paths are currently observable in a fresh packaged-runtime smoke:

- `AgentRuntime` service startup catches exceptions and returns `null` after logging, so the failure never reaches `runtime.reportError` or structured boot health.
- `@elizaos/plugin-inbox` runs its `inbox_migration` service against a fresh PGlite database before the required schema is ready and fails on `ALTER TABLE`.
- `@elizaos/plugin-scheduling` seeds boot tasks before its scheduled-task runner service is available.

This makes a broken initialization pipeline look healthy and violates the fail-fast/observable-boundary error policy.

**To Reproduce**

1. Build and start the real agent with `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`, configuration, HOME, and credential paths pointed at new temporary directories.
2. Load the default first-party plugin set including inbox and scheduling.
3. Wait for the deferred-boot health state to settle.
4. Inspect structured backend logs and the health response.

The logs report service/migration/seed failures while the health surface reports a settled boot.

**Expected behavior**

- A service startup exception is reported through `runtime.reportError` with service/plugin context and is reflected in structured boot failure state.
- Fresh PGlite initialization orders inbox schema work after its prerequisite schema exists; it completes without a suppressed `ALTER TABLE` failure.
- Scheduling boot seeding runs only after the scheduled-task runner service is registered and ready.
- A real isolated fresh-state boot completes without these errors, and injected service failures remain observably failed.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

- N/A - this is a headless agent boot/runtime correctness issue with no rendered UI surface.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no rendered UI surface.
- Backend logs: package smoke logs show the three failures; exact isolated before/after logs will be attached to the PR after manual review.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A - no frontend path changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or planner behavior changes.
- Domain artifacts: fresh PGlite schema/service registry state and the boot health payload will be captured in the PR evidence.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Reproduce in an isolated state/config/home without ambient credentials.
- [ ] Remove catch-and-null service startup behavior; typed failures are reported and visible at the runtime boundary.
- [ ] Make inbox fresh-schema initialization deterministic and successful on real PGlite.
- [ ] Make scheduling seed ordering depend on runner readiness structurally, not timing.
- [ ] Add real integration coverage for success and injected-failure paths.
- [ ] Run focused tests, error-policy ratchet, and full `bun run verify` after rebasing onto current `origin/develop`.

**Additional context**

This was exposed while hardening real Snap/Flatpak/Debian install-and-boot smoke coverage. The package verifier must not be asked to parse logs to compensate for a runtime health surface that claims success after startup failures.

55:T975,## Problem

`RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT` can report a different Claude/Codex model than `plugin-cli-inference` actually invokes.

PR #16310 added static `displayModel` metadata by reading `process.env` when the plugin module is constructed. The handlers, however, resolve model names from `runtime.getSetting()` for each agent and tier:

- Claude large tiers: `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_MODEL`
- Claude planner: `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_PLANNER_MODEL` then the large-model setting
- Codex large tiers: `ELIZA_CLI_CODEX_MODEL`
- Codex planner: `ELIZA_CLI_CODEX_PLANNER_MODEL` then the large-model setting

Core deliberately gives per-agent runtime settings precedence and does not read `process.env` from `getSetting()`. In a multi-agent process, or whenever a character setting differs from the host environment, the handler calls one model while its context provider claims another. The new unit tests only exercise environment-derived metadata and therefore do not cover the real runtime boundary.

## Required fix

- Make model-registration display metadata able to resolve an ordered list of runtime setting keys before a static provider default.
- Declare CLI-inference metadata per tier:
  - large tiers: the backend's large-model runtime key, then its canonical default;
  - planner: planner-model runtime key, then large-model runtime key, then the canonical default.
- Preserve serializable, handler-free registration metadata.
- Add integration coverage that constructs a real `AgentRuntime` with runtime values intentionally conflicting with `process.env`, registers the plugin models, invokes `RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT`, and proves the reported model matches the handler's effective model for both a large slot and the planner fallback chain.
- Keep inert/native-planner behavior unchanged.
- Replace change-narration comments with durable system rationale per the repository comment policy.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Per-agent/character settings override host environment values in model self-reporting.
- [ ] Planner metadata follows the same planner -> large -> default precedence as the handler.
- [ ] Claude and Codex backends are covered.
- [ ] Existing core and plugin-cli-inference tests pass.
- [ ] `bun run verify` passes on a branch rebased onto current `develop`.
- [ ] Exact-head evidence includes the provider output and handler-selected model from a real runtime, manually reviewed and attached inline to the PR.
56:Tdb8,## Summary
`app-staging.elizacloud.ai` is stuck on **"initializing agent"** for every new agent. Root cause: **every agent-create call to the staging cloud-api returns HTTP 500 `{"code":"internal_error"}`**. The UI provisions the agent, then polls the per-agent proxy passthrough (`GET /api/conversations`) which never serves (the agent was never created), so `runCloudManagedWarmup()` spins on `initializing-agent` until the warmup deadline.

The 500 is an **unhandled DB/infrastructure error** inside the create handler, forced to the generic `internal_error` envelope by `isInfrastructureError()` in `cloud-worker-errors.ts` (so the real SQLSTATE/SQL message is swallowed to avoid leaking raw SQL).

## Reproduction (staging, authenticated session)
```
POST /api/v1/eliza/agents {"agentName":"x","autoProvision":false}  → 500 internal_error (t≈2–8s)
POST /api/v1/eliza/agents {"agentName":"x","autoProvision":true,"dockerImage":"ghcr.io/elizaos/eliza-agent:latest"} → 500 internal_error (t≈7.6s)
```
Both shared and dedicated create paths 500 → it's the create **write**, not provisioning/worker-health.

## Reads are healthy — this is write/transaction-specific
```
GET /api/v1/eliza/agents    → 200 {"data":[]}   (t spiky 0.27–6.4s, but succeeds)
GET /api/v1/credits/balance → 200 {"balance":…} (t≈1.3s)
GET /api/health             → 200 environment:staging
```
- Bogus `dockerImage` → **403 agent_image_not_allowed** (handler reaches deep into `createAgent`/`assertAgentImageAllowed`; the write AFTER the allowlist gate is what fails).
- `getReadConnection()` and `getWriteConnection()` both resolve the **same** `getPrimaryDatabaseUrl()`/Hyperdrive binding, so this is NOT a code-level read/write DB split. The difference: create runs a **multi-statement interactive `dbWrite.transaction`** with a Postgres advisory lock + `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` + INSERT (`eliza-sandbox.ts` `createAgent`), while a passing read is a single pooled query.

## Ruled out
- **Schema drift** — deploy migrations job: `[db:migrate] pending migrations: 0; migrations complete`; deployed branch has migrations through `0176`.
- **General DB outage** — reads succeed.
- **Deployed branch code** — staging is currently deployed from `fix/cloud-cerebras-reasoning-effort` (PR #16087) via `workflow_dispatch`; that PR touches zero create/sandbox/db-client/managed-env files.

## Most likely cause
Interactive transaction / advisory-lock write over the **staging Hyperdrive binding `ef65eb94c74144a3a3848ad1945571fb`** failing (SQLSTATE swallowed to generic 500), while simple pooled reads pass. Same failure class as the previously-closed #15160 / #15203 staging-provisioning DB incidents.

## Needs (holder-gated — I don't have elizaOS CF account access)
1. `wrangler tail eliza-cloud-api-staging --env staging --format json` while issuing a create, to capture the swallowed Postgres SQLSTATE behind `[CloudApi] Unhandled error`.
2. Verify staging Hyperdrive binding `ef65eb94c74144a3a3848ad1945571fb` points at the intended **staging** Postgres and supports interactive transactions (re-point if stale/misconfigured — the #15160 surface).
3. Consider reverting staging to `develop` (currently on the cerebras feature branch) so QA tests real staging code.

Once the SQLSTATE is known: a code-level Hyperdrive-transaction incompatibility is a bounded fix; a binding/DB-target misconfig is an ops flip.

_Diagnosed by [sol-cloud]. No prod changes, no staging redeploy, no DB writes persisted._
57:T434,## Parent

## Problem
When mobile voice explicitly forces Eliza Cloud TTS, the renderer still POSTs to the on-device agent's `/api/tts/cloud` proxy. On constrained devices this adds another download through the phone-side Bun event loop plus base64 IPC remarshal, measured at roughly 5–6 seconds per reply even though the cloud worker completes in 1–2 seconds.

## Expected
For forced-cloud TTS with a cloud session token, call the configured Eliza Cloud worker's `/api/v1/voice/tts` endpoint directly. Derive the worker origin from boot configuration so staging/custom cloud environments are not hardcoded. Preserve the existing proxied path when cloud auth/config is unavailable.

## Acceptance criteria
- Forced-cloud TTS uses the cloud session bearer token and configured cloud API origin.
- Production is not hardcoded over staging/custom boot config.
- Existing non-forced/proxy behavior remains unchanged.
- Focused headless regression proves routing and fallback.

58:T840,## What

The shared vitest base config excludes two agent files from coverage reporting entirely:

```
packages/test/vitest/default.config.ts
  coverage.exclude:
    // Rolldown coverage still struggles with these inline type-import files.
    "eliza/packages/agent/src/api/server.ts",
    "eliza/packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts",
```

The enforced changed-file coverage gate (`.github/workflows/coverage-gate.yml` → `scripts/security/coverage-gate.awk`) fails any changed source file that is **missing from the merged LCOV**:

```
for (f in changed_map) {
  if (!(f in file_pct)) { printf "  MISSING: %s\n", f; missing_count++ }
```

So every PR that touches `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts` or `packages/agent/src/api/server.ts` fails the gate as `MISSING` — even with a changed test that executes the changed lines — because the config forbids those files from ever appearing in a coverage report. The two escapes cancel each other: the gate demands the file in LCOV, the config guarantees it never is.

(Note the exclude paths also carry a stale `eliza/` prefix from the old wrapper-repo layout, so they may not even match under the current roots — in which case the *comment's* Rolldown problem is unhandled instead, and the file's absence in practice comes from coverage collection failing on it. Either way the outcome for a PR author is the same: structural `MISSING`.)

## Live example

#16402 is a one-line boot-seeding fix in `eliza.ts` with a changed unit test (`packages/agent/src/runtime/__tests__/model-env-seed.test.ts`) that imports the module and drives `configureLocalEmbeddingPlugin` for real — and the file still cannot appear in LCOV.

## Options

1. Fix the underlying coverage-collection failure on those files and drop the excludes (best).
2. Teach the gate an explicit, visible allowlist for config-excluded files (fail-open with a printed warning instead of a hard `MISSING`).
3. At minimum, update the exclude paths to the current layout and document the trap where agent contributors will see it.

59:T714,## Problem

PGlite is instantiated **in-process on the main thread** (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/pglite/manager.ts` — plain `new PGlite`, no worker). Every WASM query execution blocks the event loop, which (a) serializes what look like concurrent provider awaits in `composeState` and (b) inflates every in-flight span — during the #16393 investigation, provider embedding calls measuring 230–320ms raw showed as 500–2500ms because their continuations couldn't run while WASM ground through queries.

#16405 removes the worst single query (unindexed vector full-scan → HNSW/KNN), but *all* PGlite work still executes on the loop: memory reads, entity resolution, the per-turn 2000-row hash-memory BM25 scan in relevant-conversations, trajectory writes.

## Ask

Host PGlite in a `worker_threads` worker with an RPC seam, so WASM execution never blocks the runtime loop. Notes from a feasibility pass:
- `@electric-sql/pglite` (0.4.6 vendored) ships a `worker` entry (`PGliteWorker` + `worker()` wrapper) — designed for web workers, but the leader/proxy protocol is transport-agnostic; a Node `worker_threads` adapter around the same message protocol is the likely shape.
- Insertion point: `plugins/plugin-sql/src/pglite/manager.ts` `createClient` (~:305–333). The drizzle pglite driver needs a client honoring the PGlite interface; `PGliteWorker` implements it.
- Care points: extension loading (`vector`, `fuzzystrmatch`) must happen worker-side; live-query/notification APIs if used; clean shutdown ordering (the manager already serializes close); benchmark a representative turn before/after.

This is the remaining structural half of the #16393 latency floor (the discarded-refetch half shipped in #16403, index half in #16405).

5d:T7a6,## Summary

`Provider.cacheStable` is part of the public `Provider` contract, but its value is never read when a provider's output is rendered into a prompt segment — so setting it has no effect.

## Where

Three spots drop the flag between the provider definition and the rendered prompt segment:

- `ContextProviderEvent` (`packages/core/src/types/context-object.ts`) has no `cacheStable` field.
- `appendStateProviderEvents` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts`) builds a `ContextProviderEvent` from `state.data.providers` but never copies `cacheStable` from the registered provider definition.
- `renderEvent`'s provider-event branch (`packages/core/src/runtime/context-renderer.ts:311`) hardcodes `stable: false`.

## Consequence

On the planner/evaluator stages, `buildStageChatMessages` buckets segments by their `stable` flag — `stable` content goes into the cached system message, `dynamic` into the uncached user message. With `stable` hardcoded `false` for every provider event, a provider that declares `cacheStable: true` still has its (genuinely stable) content routed into the uncached user message and repriced on every call.

## Why it matters

The flag is documented as functional. The plugin-starter scaffold (`packages/examples/_plugin`, the template new plugins are generated from) sets `cacheStable: true` on its example `HELLO_WORLD_PROVIDER`, and `registryPluginsProvider` sets it too. Any provider that sets it (and is rendered as a text segment rather than a native tool) is silently uncached.

Note: core's `ACTIONS`/`PROVIDERS` providers also set `cacheStable: true` but are intentionally in `MODEL_CONTEXT_PROVIDER_EXCLUSIONS` — they are rendered as native tools, not text segments — so they are correctly unaffected.

## Expected

`Provider.cacheStable` is threaded through to the rendered prompt segment's `stable` flag so `buildStageChatMessages` routes a stable provider's content into the cached system message.
5e:T17d0,## Summary

The Cloud chat gateway still adds seconds before a Cerebras request despite the provider itself being fast. A paired staging benchmark now isolates the dominant regression: chat crosses **three Railway-TCP Redis rate-limit gates** before provider forwarding. After 30 seconds idle, the two Hono middleware gates alone add **2.45–3.11s** outside the route timer, and the handler's org gate contributes to another **0.51–0.97s** phase. Direct Cerebras remains **0.09–0.37s**.

This is not reasoning-effort overhead and it is not slow Cerebras generation. The actual gateway-to-Cerebras response-header phase was **0.09–0.33s** in every post-idle case.

## Live evidence

Accepted paired run (staging gateway and direct provider, same prompts/models, paced below limits):

- Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29167994915
- Job: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29167994915/job/86584408510
- Artifact: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29167994915/artifacts/8252811951
- 320/320 HTTP 200; 0 transport failures; 0 SSE terminal failures
- 160 gateway + 160 direct samples; 30 warm pairs per case plus cold/post-idle
- Both paths entered Cloudflare at IAD; every gateway inference used the direct pass-through path

Post-idle gateway decomposition (`response headers - route total` exposes middleware before the route timer):

| Model/case | Outside route | Auth | Mid reads | Reserve | Provider-to-headers | Gateway headers |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Gemma / none | 2,993ms | 151ms | 664ms | 812ms | 333ms | 4,953ms |
| GLM / omit | 2,540ms | 158ms | 659ms | 844ms | 89ms | 4,290ms |
| GLM / none 4096 | 3,100ms | 44ms | 965ms | 755ms | 161ms | 5,025ms |
| Gemma / omit | 2,450ms | 1,827ms | 514ms | 614ms | 89ms | 5,494ms |
| GLM / none 512 | 3,107ms | 34ms | 862ms | 789ms | 156ms | 4,948ms |

The matched direct samples returned headers/TTFT in 86–365ms.

Warm gateway p50 was still 485ms vs direct headers around 81–88ms, with gateway outliers up to 5.84s. The hidden outside-route interval reached 4.66s.

### Independent live-tail confirmation on current staging

On commit `b2e36d3`, privacy-safe unauthenticated chat probes reproduced both modes without ever calling a model provider:

- successful Railway Redis check: 556–589ms wall
- SocketRedis timeout: 2.378s, 3.250s, and 3.459s wall
- exact log: `SocketRedis operation timed out after 1000ms`
- degraded response policy: `redis-unavailable` (the limiter fell open)

Across 679 successful Worker requests in the prior 30 minutes, p95 wall time was 3.40s and p99 was 8.49s. `/api/health`, which bypasses the lazy full-app import, stayed at 0–1ms; cold chat also pays initialization for the monolithic 580-route app.

## Code-level cause

Every chat call currently stacks:

1. `bootstrap-app.ts`: global Hono IP limiter (600/min), before the chat route timer.
2. `v1/chat/completions/route.ts`: Hono API-key limiter (200/min), also before the timer.
3. `handleChatCompletionsPOST`: per-org tier limiter (60/120/300 per minute).

All three use `REDIS_URL` in staging/production. `redis-factory.ts` resolves that to `SocketRedis` over `cloudflare:sockets`, opening request-scoped TCP/TLS connections to Railway. The committed Wrangler comments simultaneously state that raw external Redis from Workers is unreliable and can hang under load. `SocketRedis` has a 1,000ms operation timeout, so the measured two outer stalls plus one inner stall match the three connection/operation failure windows. Its error cleanup awaits an unbounded `close()` sequence, so the documented 1s ceiling is not actually a wall-clock ceiling; that is consistent with 3–4.66s outside-route outliers.

The existing `X-Eliza-Preforward-Ms` header starts inside `handleChatCompletionsPOST`, so it cannot see the largest two gates and currently labels provider-header wait as `total`. This is tracked more broadly in #16079.

## Required fix

- Replace the protective global and chat route Redis gates with Cloudflare's native Rate Limiting binding. It is machine-local/non-network-blocking and designed for protective limits.
- Keep the organization-tier limit on a globally strong backend. Cloudflare's native binding is location-local and eventually consistent, so silently substituting it would weaken the purchased org quota. Batch its lease reconciliation and use one request-scoped connection or a strong Cloudflare primitive such as a Durable Object.
- Preserve explicit 429 behavior and observable policy headers/logs. Missing/misconfigured production bindings must fail visibly, not silently disable protection.
- Bound SocketRedis teardown so an operation timeout remains a real wall-clock ceiling for non-chat consumers.
- Extend correlated telemetry across global limiter, route limiter, auth, org tier lookup/limit, pool selection, balance gate/reservation, provider fetch-to-headers, first byte/token, stream completion, and deferred settlement.
- Keep production untouched until staging proves the change.

## Acceptance criteria

- Unit/integration tests cover allow, deny, binding failure/missing binding, batched lease reconciliation, and no duplicate counter increments.
- Wrangler dry-run and generated binding types pass for staging and production config.
- A real staging paired run repeats the same five Cerebras cases with at least 30 warm pairs/case plus 30-second post-idle probes, paced below limits.
- Post-idle paired excess outside the route timer (`gateway headers - route total - matched direct headers`) has p90 below 100ms, and no Redis TCP timeout appears in the two protective chat ingress limiters.
- Gateway provider-to-headers remains within 2x direct after subtracting deliberate auth/billing work; all remaining deltas are named by truthful telemetry.
- Billing reservations/deferred settlement, auth failures, 402s, and 429s retain their existing correctness semantics.

Related: #9899 (original pre-forward work, now shown incomplete for middleware), #15513 (Smart Placement), #16079 (truthful E2E telemetry), #16137 (paired live harness).
5f:T107b,## Confirmed bug

elizaOS constructs a stable prompt-cache routing key, and `plugin-elizacloud` emits it, but the Cloud chat gateway silently drops it before Cerebras.

Current path:

1. `packages/core/src/runtime/provider-cache-plan.ts` builds `v5:<prefixHash>` and places it in Cerebras provider options as both `promptCacheKey` and `prompt_cache_key`.
2. `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts` resolves the key and sends both top-level forms in the native `/chat/completions` body.
3. `packages/cloud/api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts` defines a separate `ChatRequest` that contains neither field.
4. The direct pass-through `upstreamBody` copies temperature, top-p, penalties, stop, and max tokens, but not `prompt_cache_key`.
5. The AI-SDK streaming and non-streaming paths also construct no cache-key provider options.

The key therefore disappears at the Cloud gateway on both the simple pass-through path and the structured/tool-call SDK path.

## Existing implementation showing intended support

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/types.ts` already declares `OpenAIChatRequest.prompt_cache_key`.

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/providers/vercel-ai-gateway.ts::mergeGatewayProviderOptions` already reads that field and maps it into Cerebras/eliza provider options.

The Cloud chat route’s duplicate request shape has diverged from that shared contract.

## Impact

Cerebras now accepts an optional `prompt_cache_key` routing hint. Requests sharing a key are routed to the same prompt cache on a best-effort basis, improving cache-hit rate and Time to First Token for repeated stable prefixes.

This is particularly relevant to long-context agent/planner calls and GLM-4.7. Cerebras’s current supported-model list includes GLM-4.7, GPT-OSS 120B, and Qwen 3 235B; Gemma should not be claimed as benefiting without live evidence.

Automatic prefix caching still works without the field, so this is not an outage. The bug loses an explicit affinity hint under load and makes the already-implemented client cache plan ineffective through Eliza Cloud.

## Fix

- Accept canonical `prompt_cache_key`.
- Accept `promptCacheKey` only as a backward-compatible client alias.
- Canonicalize to one provider-facing `prompt_cache_key`; do not send both forms upstream.
- Validate a nonempty string with maximum length 1024.
- Never log the raw key.
- Preserve the key through:
  - Cerebras pass-through streaming.
  - AI-SDK streaming.
  - AI-SDK non-streaming.
- Merge cache provider options without overwriting Anthropic reasoning, web-search, or other provider options.
- Keep requests without a key byte-for-byte unchanged.
- Verify that the platform Cerebras account has `prompt_cache_key` routing enabled before production rollout; if unavailable, use an explicit capability/rollout decision rather than silently dropping caller intent.

## Acceptance

- A plugin-generated `v5:<prefixHash>` reaches the mocked Cerebras fetch as exactly `prompt_cache_key`.
- Both snake-case input and camel-case compatibility input are covered.
- Pass-through and structured/tool-call SDK paths have fetch-boundary tests.
- Oversized, empty, and non-string values have explicit tested behavior.
- No raw key appears in logs, telemetry, or errors.
- Existing reasoning, tools, streaming usage, billing, and passthrough qualification tests remain green.
- Live staging proof uses two or more GLM-4.7 calls with a stable prefix/key and records:
  - `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`
  - first-token latency
  - same-key versus rotated-key behavior
- Lack of a cache hit is reported honestly and checked against account enablement, TTL, 128-token block size, and prefix stability.
- No quality or billing behavior changes; Cerebras documents cached tokens as normally priced.

## References

- Cerebras Prompt Caching:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/capabilities/prompt-caching
- Cerebras changelog, April 22, 2026:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/support/change-log
- Cerebras cache/latency metrics:
  https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/api-reference/metrics/retrieve-metrics
- Analogous cache-plan loss fixed for OpenRouter/Anthropic: #15776 / #15777
- Related latency measurement umbrella: #15428
