## Evidence plan

Attach the forced-WebSocket-loss switching proof, live create/edit Codex run, finalized trajectories, full app audit verdicts, screenshots, and MP4 walkthroughs to the PR.65:T430,## Problem

`CliAuthSessionsService.getAndClearApiKey()` does not currently satisfy its documented exactly-once plaintext retrieval contract under concurrency.

Two poll requests can both read the same authenticated session with `consumed_at = null`, both load and decrypt the API key, and only afterward both call the unconditional `markConsumed()`. Both callers can therefore receive the plaintext credential.

This is separate from the concurrent completion/key-mint race tracked in #16240.

## Required acceptance

- Make plaintext retrieval a true single-winner operation on the primary database.
- A losing concurrent poll must receive no plaintext.
- Do not permanently consume the session if key lookup or decryption fails; define a fail-closed retry-safe transaction/state boundary.
- Preserve expiry, authentication status, and existing ownership/auth checks.
- Add a deterministic concurrent-poll integration test that proves exactly one plaintext response.

6d:T10fe,## Summary

- Add `@elizaos/eliza-hub` as a self-contained workspace package with an Eliza-themed Forgejo deployment, dark/light themes, a local Compose stack, and a portable standalone layout.
- Add Merge Steward: an agent-native merge queue and coordination service with claims, validation budgets, queue simulation, merge trains, Eliza Work/Kanban APIs, OIDC identity, audited overrides, dry-run-first execution, and production evidence gates.
- Add isolated Forgejo Actions runner configuration, Postgres migrations, OpenAPI, backups and restore drills, observability, Terraform/Cloudflare/Hetzner deployment scaffolding, and operator documentation.
- Align the package with elizaOS conventions: `@elizaos/*` names, Node 24 images/runners, monorepo scripts and lockfile, identical `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, MIT metadata, and Poppins OFL attribution.
- Extend the shared coverage gate so subprocess entrypoints require changed tests without falsely treating parent-process LCOV as child-process coverage.

## Why

This gives the team one public folder to inspect, test, extract, and deploy while keeping runtime databases, credentials, runner state, mirrored repositories, Terraform state, backups, and production evidence out of source control. Forgejo remains the Git source of truth; Merge Steward adds agent-scale controls that are not native GitHub or Forgejo concepts.

The package is intentionally honest about readiness: it is a working local demo and production-oriented staging seed, not a GitHub API drop-in or an approved production cutover. Live agent merges remain disabled unless explicit identity, persistence, runner, repository-protection, and private-evidence gates pass.

6f:T16bb,## Summary

Overhauls the Milady chat surface across web, iOS, Android, and desktop, and lands a **native chat-transcript renderer** (SwiftUI on iOS, Views on Android, an AppKit spike on macOS) driven by one shared, versioned contract. Renamed `ContinuousChatOverlay → ChatOverlay` throughout.

Three themes:

### 1. Liquid-glass chat surface (real OS material where physics allows)
- The chat sheet floats as liquid glass: a light fill over a heavy `backdrop-filter` (blur + saturate + brightness) so the field behind glows *through* the panel, plus the directional specular rim.
- **Native glass on Capacitor**: the `GlassBridge` plugin (iOS 26 `UIGlassEffect`, Android 12+ Material) was never registered — fixed. The default ember field moves into a **native layer behind a transparent webview** (`setBackdrop`), and the inset sheet anchors a real `UIGlassEffect` panel over it (verified live via lldb: `<UIGlassEffect: …>` + an `EmberBackdropView`/`CAGradientLayer` below the `WKWebView`). The panel's corner radius tracks the sheet's animated corners.
- Maximized chat is a flat neutral `#212121` reading surface (the sheen/gradient read as artifacts full-screen); the exit is a pure cover-layer crossfade (no discrete style swap at the corners); the maximized composer capsule is glassmorphic.
- Desktop: opt-in `NSVisualEffectView` window glass behind the chat-overlay window (`ELIZA_DESKTOP_NATIVE_GLASS=1`).

### 2. Gesture correctness (the pull continuum)
- **maximize → pull-to-half** now settles at a real detent (was: bounced tall / collapsed / stranded a stub). Root causes fixed: an invisible stale grabber captured the restore drag; the restore bookkeeping raced the maximize-release hysteresis; `maximizeFromPull` never settled the height spring (stranded the GPU layer + drag latch); restore releases free-rested instead of completing.
- One **constant pointer-locked drag handle** across pill ↔ input ↔ chat (rendered outside the scale transform, y tracks the panel's visual top).
- Restore drag morphs live (shape springs home, handle fades in under the finger) instead of popping at release.
- Regression e2e: `test:chat-restore-e2e`, `test:chat-restore-continuum-e2e`.

### 3. Native chat-transcript renderer (new)
Everything in the chat renders to **native widgets** — text/markdown, code, choice, follow-ups, form (fields/select/checkbox + validation), workflow, checklist, task card, background swatches, permission card, secret request, tool-call rows, thinking disclosure, failure/retry.

- **One contract** (`eliza.native-transcript/v1`): frames serialized through the *same* `parseSegments` the DOM uses; a typed action envelope back (`message | navigate | prefill | background | retry`). A golden fixture is conformance-tested by every decoder.
- **iOS** SwiftUI, **Android** Views, **macOS** AppKit spike — all one contract.
- Security by construction: secrets are display-only (never transit chat/bridge), permission grant requires a real OS request (no fabrication), navigate/prefill/background stay local intents (never leaked to the agent as chat text).
- Harness demo: `ELIZA_NATIVE_TRANSCRIPT_DEMO=1 bun run --cwd packages/app ios:chat-harness:preview` (full-screen native, DOM composer reserved at the bottom; `eliza:native-transcript-split=1` for side-by-side review).

## Red-team

A 4-lens adversarial red-team (security / iOS correctness / DOM-parity / bridge-lifecycle) + verification judge returned **NO-GO on 5 HIGH findings**; **all fixed** and re-verified:
- iOS scroll no longer yanks a reader who scrolled up (at-bottom guard, DOM/Android parity).
- iOS widget `@State` keyed by stable segment/tool identity, not array offset (locked choice / form draft no longer adopts the wrong view's state).
- Android row fingerprint excludes streaming-volatile fields (widget drafts/locks survive token growth).
- `navigate` routes through the real `dispatchNavigateViewEvent` shape the shell reads.
- Retry is a typed `{kind:retry}` envelope gated to retryable failure kinds (no fabricated chat string).
Plus MEDs: iOS markdown link runs stripped; Android background emits shared preset ids; Android rejects non-v1 schema; Android form no longer trims; harness hides the native list on mid-`show()` unmount; sim-check fails on a native reject.

## Validation

**iOS on-simulator** — all 14 widget types rendered natively and interactive: choice (selection state), form (text input + required validation + submit producing the exact DOM-parity `[form:submit …] {json}`), permission (no grant button), secret (display-only), follow-ups, workflow (step glyphs), tool calls (running/success + duration), thinking, checklist, task, background, code, generated-UI card, failure (gated Retry). Bridge round-trip proven (native tap → `transcriptAction` → script advance → new frame → native re-render).

**Android on-emulator** — native choice + form rendered as native Views (EditText/Spinner/CheckBox/Button); bridge round-trip confirmed in logcat.

**Automated** — 558 UI vitest; native conformance: swiftc -typecheck (0 errors, full module), decode-check, 65-check widget logic script, javac (0), JVM contract 6/6; chat-sheet e2e 296/296; restore e2es green; iOS mirror byte-identical.

## Known follow-ups (non-blocking)
- Native conformance gates (Swift/JVM) are not yet wired into CI (only the TS `spec.test.ts` runs there). Add a macOS Swift lane + JVM lane on fixture/model changes.
- Deep-nesting decode depth cap (hardening; latent, native not production-wired).
- The maximized composer is still CSS glass (an in-page sheet over DOM pixels can't be lensed by an under-webview native panel); a true-native maximized input needs the dual-webview or native-composer architecture.

# Risks

Medium. Snap, Debian, and Flatpak jobs now stop when an installed launcher fails, reports a different version, or lacks usable help. This can expose previously hidden packaging defects and intentionally block publication. The Flatpak metadata also replaces three dead screenshot URLs with current public 1440×900 application baselines so validation can reach the package build and launcher smoke.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one process-level verifier for installed elizaOS launchers and uses it in all five Snap, Debian, and Flatpak smoke steps. The verifier runs the actual installed command for both `--version` and `--help`, requires a zero exit, requires the exact release version, and requires a Commander `Usage:` section.

Contract tests exercise real child executables and parse the workflow YAML to prevent masked launcher checks from returning. Flatpak AppStream metadata now points at existing reviewed application screenshots rather than deleted documentation assets.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking release-pipeline hardening).

# Documentation changes needed?

The Flatpak submission README now documents its repository-hosted screenshot source of truth.

```bash
bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/verify-packaged-cli.test.ts
node packages/scripts/verify-packaged-cli.mjs \
  --expected "$(node -p "require('./packages/agent/package.json').version")" \
  -- node packages/agent/dist/bin.js
```

71:T18a2,## Realtime voice: integrated Phase-1 slice (server + client + live-provider evidence)

One vertical slice, per Definition of Done: server, browser client, and the evidence rig that proved them against **live Deepgram Flux + Cartesia Sonic**, in a single reviewable unit. Nothing here is a seam, a stub, or UI ahead of capability.

### What this is
- **Server** (`packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/**`, `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/voice-session/**`): mint route (short-lived scoped JWT, jti, aud-bound), consent-nonce precondition (server-enforced, SEC-21), WS handler with hello-first headerless auth (WebView 113 cannot set WS headers), revoke-to-silence (SEC-6: revocation severs live provider sockets), server-derived usage metering (SEC-15: client never trusted for cost), interruption pipeline under a single voiceTurnId (cancel TTS -> abort SSE -> flush downlink -> `interrupted`), uplink re-framing to Flux's 2560B/80ms frames.
- **Client** (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-*.ts`): mint -> hello-first WSS -> full event handling; mic capture with runtime-verified AudioWorklet + ScriptProcessor fallback (WebView 113); streaming PCM playback (no decodeAudioData full-clip barrier); barge-in flushes local playback before server ack; re-mint on revoked/expired token (no reconnect with a dead token); maps onto the existing unified voice status from #15924 (no second state system).
- **Evidence harness** (`packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness`): one command drives baseline / barge-in / error-auth scenarios and fails loudly if any stage or artifact is missing. `--target=real` boots the REAL server code (real jwt/consent/session/registry/metering/reframer/adapters; only the socket transport is a documented node shim in place of Workers `WebSocketPair`).

### Flag
`VOICE_REALTIME_WS_ENABLED` (default off) is consumed at the WS route: mint returns 503 `voice_realtime_disabled` when off; the evidence run exercises it ON. Real runtime consumer, not a dead flag.

### Live-provider evidence (run against the REAL server, this branch)
Evidence pack (WS transcript with tokens redacted, input/output WAVs, both-side logs, stage timings, domain rows, SHA-256 index, MP4): `evidence/voice-e2e/2026-07-10T15-37-44-748Z-real-server/` (artifacts attached below; full pack retained outside repo, hashes in the index).

| scenario | result |
| --- | --- |
| baseline (real speech in -> transcript -> LLM -> real audio out) | PASS |
| barge-in (interrupt mid-speech) | PASS, **0 post-interrupt downlink frames** |
| error-auth (bad provider key) | PASS, typed `stt_transport_error`, no ready, no audio |

Measured stage deltas (baseline, live providers, monotonic clock; missing stages report `not_reached`, never zero):
- mint -> ready: **10.5ms**
- stt_final -> llm_first_text: 834ms
- llm_first_text -> tts_first_frame: 1273ms
- **stt_final -> tts_first_frame: 2107ms** (see "Latency" below)
- transcript verbatim: "What is the weather like in Denver today?"; 1.8MB real Cartesia audio captured.

### Real bugs found ONLY by the live boot (fixed in this branch, with regression tests)
1. `end_audio` advisory frame was treated as fatal `control_unknown_type` and killed the turn.
2. Empty-transcript finalization sent Cartesia a live 400 ("No valid transcripts passed") -> tts_error, zero audio. Finalization now holds one phrase back so the terminal phrase closes the context with `continue:false`.
3. Harness `stop()` hung on half-open sockets under bun canary drain-exit.
4. Harness error-auth gate accepted only one error shape; the real adapter emits `transport_error`.

### Latency (honest)
2107ms stt_final -> first audio frame is above our 1.2s target. The measured split shows two roughly equal legs (LLM first-token 834ms, TTS first-byte 1273ms). Optimization is scoped as immediate follow-up on this same evidence rig (candidates: eager-EOT speculative LLM start, TTS warm-context reuse, first-phrase fast-path). The rig measures every run, so improvements land with numbers, not adjectives.

### Tests
124 green under bun canary (CI's bun): 31 cloud-api session, 33 cloud-shared voice-session, 60 ui client. Full WS lifecycle, barge-in zero-post-cancel, revoke <=500ms, JWT expiry/revocation/claim-mismatch, metering enforcement, hello-first, malformed/oversized frames, PCM golden vectors, playback ordering, visibility-suspend.

### Known pre-existing failure (not this branch)
`bun run verify` fails on `audit:type-safety-ratchet` on **clean develop** (dd2aac161d4) with identical feed-package violations; receipt below. This branch's surfaces typecheck clean.

### Security acceptance (from the voice threat model)
Consent nonce server-enforced before mint; revoke-to-silence severs provider sockets; metering server-derived; `mip_opt_out` note: NOT yet on develop's flux adapter (verified absent) — separate single-boundary PR follows; token never in URL or logs (redacted in evidence transcript, hand-reviewed).

72:T143f,# Relates to

Implements the disabled-by-default Cloud server portion of #16420. The native iOS shell is tracked in #16640; the renderer consumer and live staging walkthrough remain separate integration slices.

# Summary

- Add a first-party Authorization Code + PKCE lifecycle for the fixed `ai.elizaos.app` client and exact `https://eliza.app/auth/callback` redirect.
- Mint a short-lived hash-only authorization code, create one inactive encrypted credential on exchange, and activate it transactionally only after the native client proves durable Keychain receipt.
- Add crash/retry-safe exchange, acknowledgement, self-revocation, signed-in account recovery, expiry cleanup, app-deactivation revocation, and additive database migrations.
- Keep mobile credentials primary-consistent and outside positive auth caches; prevent them from managing generic credentials or delegated access.
- Apply route-isolated admission/ingress limits and reject mobile credentials in WebSocket query strings. Ordinary Cloud/browser WebSocket tokens retain their existing behavior.
- Regenerate the canonical Hono router and public SDK wrappers.

# Rollout and compatibility

- The feature fails closed unless `ELIZA_MOBILE_APP_AUTH_ENABLED` is exactly `true`, the environment is exactly `staging` or `production`, and `ELIZA_MOBILE_APP_AUTH_APP_ID` is a live approved app UUID whose allowed origins include the fixed callback.
- Neither flag is configured by this PR. No staging or production deployment/enablement was performed from this branch.
- Migrations are additive. Existing API keys, sessions, browser auth, Android, desktop, local, and remote-host flows retain their existing contracts.
- Mobile credentials use headers only. The server returns `mobile_credential_transport_forbidden` for `eliza_mobile_*` URL query tokens so a long-lived secret is never forwarded through the generic WebSocket channel.
- The credential is finite-lived (30 days), attributable to the configured app, individually recoverable/revocable, and tombstoned with encrypted reveal material erased.

# Reviewer start points

- Protocol and transactional lifecycle: `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/mobile-app-auth.ts`
- Persistence invariants: `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/mobile-app-auth-grants.ts`

- Real persistence/concurrency/adversarial coverage: `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/mobile-app-auth.integration.test.ts`
- Header-only dedicated-agent boundary: `packages/cloud/api/src/dedicated-agent-proxy.ts`

73:T15a1,Closes #16460

## Summary

This is a cohesive interaction and visual-stability pass across the home notification shade, continuous chat sheet, startup/auth surfaces, and local development runtime.

- Gives the notification shade shared momentum physics, endpoint resistance, stable detents, coordinated card/count/control fades, clean stack expansion/collapse, desktop clear-all confirmation, and isolated chat-vs-home gestures.
- Makes notification boot/reconnect snapshots authoritative while preserving concurrent arrivals, and tags replayed WebSocket events so one-shot UI effects are not repeated.
- Reworks chat actions, inline editing, replies, playback state, timestamps, transcription, long-draft scrolling, search, upload, and pending attachments so controls stay scoped to the correct message without unintended row or sheet movement.
- Makes message-search jumps deterministic by revealing already-loaded history before fetching and routing the jump through the canonical message scroller; the target lands in view with a neutral white highlight.
- Keeps the startup logo/name static while only status text shimmers, hides desktop pager arrows on mobile, reuses canonical cloud/device login launch surfaces, and runs the local Vite server under Node so HTTP upgrade/WebSocket proxying remains connected.

## Root causes

Notification drag physics, settle state, opacity, mounting, and glass presentation were driven by partially independent transitions. Chat action/reply/transcription controls participated in message and composer layout. Search fetched before revealing bounded history while bottom-follow could override imperative scrolling. Snapshot/replay handling could conflate reconnect state with new arrivals. The pre-React startup lockup and mounted shell used different geometry, and Bun-hosted Vite did not reliably preserve proxy upgrades.

## User impact

Notification pulls and stacks now track, settle, expand, collapse, and clear as one coherent surface. Chat controls stay minimal and stable. Older search hits reliably scroll into view. Voice/transcription and attachment workflows no longer unexpectedly open or collapse the sheet. Startup/auth transitions no longer morph or fork into duplicate UI, and mobile keeps swipe navigation without desktop arrows.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Makes view switching work when WebSocket delivery is delayed or unavailable.
- Reconciles the active view after reconnect or resume.
- Prevents internal action results from leaking into public chat, streaming, and shortcut responses.
- Completes generated-view creation, validation, loading, editing, and atomic reload rollback.
- Makes view-registry and ACP task failures observable instead of presenting fabricated healthy states.
- Adds real lifecycle and live end-to-end coverage for the repaired workflows.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and reliability improvements.

# Documentation changes needed?

The generated-plugin scaffold contract and plugin-agent-orchestrator package guidance were updated with the new behavior. No additional user-facing documentation is required.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with the two walkthrough videos: WebSocket-withheld switching and live-model view creation/editing. Then compare the exact-base desktop/mobile screenshots with the post-sync captures and inspect the live proof summary.

## Detailed testing steps

1. Send a view-navigation request while `NAVIGATE_VIEW` WebSocket frames are withheld.
2. Confirm the HTTP action response still navigates and active-view state reconciles after recovery.
3. Request a new view through the real live-model and coding-agent path.
4. Confirm the generated plugin is built, validated, registered, and rendered.
5. Edit that view and confirm its visible marker and bundle hash change.
6. Inspect the linked trajectory inputs/outputs, service receipts, network/state proof, and generated-view artifacts.

# Risks

**Low-medium.**
- UI: the realtime path is dead code unless `VITE_VOICE_REALTIME_WS=1` is baked at build time AND the server mint succeeds. Production builds keep the flag at `0`, so prod behavior is byte-identical on the voice path.
- Workflow: `.github/workflows/cloud-cf-deploy.yml` changes (staging-only voice env plumbing + `VITE_VOICE_REALTIME_WS` gated to `0` on production/main). **Workflow-file change: flagged for orchestrator review per fleet law.**
- Worker: `wrangler.toml` staging vars for the voice session service (staging section only) + a `binaryType` fix in the WS route that only executes when the voice WS feature flag is on.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Closes the staging gap where api-staging has the realtime voice server live (mint/consent/WS routes answering 200) but the app-staging bundle contains **no client path to reach it**. #16062 merged the voice-session client *library* (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-*`) but not the chat-UI wiring, so nothing in the develop-built bundle imports `createVoiceSessionClient`, and the deploy workflow never set the build-time flag.

Six commits cherry-picked from `feat/voice-standalone-backend` (the branch behind the working 2026-07-10 staging build), plus one rebase-hygiene commit:

1. `50d27a94acf` feat(ui): wire realtime voice-session client into the existing chat mic UI — `useRealtimeVoiceSession` / `useRealtimeVoiceMint` / `useContinuousVoiceSession` hooks + ChatView/chat-view-hooks integration. Realtime is an ADDITIVE enhancement of the existing continuous-mic toggle; batch path unchanged when unavailable.
2. `fbc40396a27` fix(ui): route forced realtime voice through mic controls.
3. `1065f619b3e` feat(voice): force-arm override (`VITE_VOICE_REALTIME_FORCE`, default OFF everywhere; set to "0" explicitly in the workflow).
4. `f0b9708743b` chore(cloud): enable realtime voice on staging deploy — workflow secrets plumbing + staging wrangler vars + `VITE_VOICE_REALTIME_WS` = 1 on staging, 0 on production/main.
5. `549b8c8890d` fix(cloud): staging voice auth fallback (dedicated secrets preferred, ElizaCloud API key fallback so the Worker is not deployed half-configured).
6. `c4aa4eeffa8` fix(cloud): set voice WS server `binaryType` to arraybuffer so audio uplink works.
7. `731ab66cda0` fix(ui): biome format + drop an unused `continuous` destructure that the develop rebase made dead.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (staging-gated) + fixes.

- Rollback = revert this PR or flip the workflow env to `0`.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `6ef46e6bc977facb0a3ac656dc521f4f76bd1f31`, the latest `origin/develop` at push time; zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync: 573/573 Turbo tasks and 28/28 dist-path consumer configurations.

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes release tooling and the manifest preparation order used by the release workflow. Failure modes are intentionally stricter: malformed workspace metadata, ambiguous registry responses, changed manifests, conflicting integrity, moved Git refs, or inconsistent retry evidence now halt the release. The new unit and real-process integration suites cover successful publication, failure before mutation, partial publication, exact-integrity resume, registry restart, atomic Git rejection, ref movement, and idempotent retries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This adds the fail-closed, immutable release primitives requested by #16276:

- A canonical, deterministic release contract records the source/expected commit, exact version and channel, explicit package cohort and directories, dependency graph and order, manifest identity, entrypoint metadata, and the real packed tarball path, size, SHA-512 digest, SRI integrity, and npm shasum. `2.0.3-beta.8`, `.9`, and `.10` remain permanently reserved.
- One shared workspace resolver now serves release scripts and app-core discovery. Invalid manifests, non-file manifests, duplicate package names, and unexpected filesystem failures surface instead of disappearing.
- Candidate creation requires a clean exact Git HEAD, executes one caller-supplied build exactly once, verifies manifests did not change, invokes `npm pack --json --ignore-scripts` once per package, validates the packed package identity and entrypoints, and records immutable real `.tgz` artifacts. Candidate state uses canonical JSON, ordered phase transitions, atomic replacement, and an exclusive writer lock.
- Registry inspection treats only HTTP 404 as missing. Authentication, authorization, throttling, server, transport, redirect, and malformed-response failures are distinct hard errors. Publication preflights the complete cohort before mutation, skips only exact-integrity matches, resumes missing packages from the recorded tarballs, promotes the requested channel only after full verification, and binds retries to the recorded registry.
- Git publication pushes only the explicitly named branch and tag in one atomic operation with an exact old-branch lease. Remote movement, non-fast-forward updates, conflicting tags, retry evidence changes, and reserved tags fail without mutation; matching annotated or lightweight tags dereference to the expected commit and no-op.
- `replace-workspace-versions.js`, `restore-workspace-refs.js`, and `release-set-public-access.mjs` now delegate to shared transactional manifest primitives. Rewrites use exact target versions, preserve byte-exact originals in an integrity journal, roll back on write/journal failure, and refuse restoration over a changed manifest.
- The only release workflow behavior change is the required access-before-reference-journal ordering, so the journal snapshot and packed manifests describe the same final bytes. The broader `release.yaml` orchestration remains outside this primitive-focused issue.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and non-breaking release-infrastructure feature.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was required and is included in `.github/workflows/README.md`, covering the immutable artifact/state contract, exact resume rules, reserved versions, registry/Git safety properties, commands, and the deliberate orchestration boundary.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with these executable specifications:

1. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-verdaccio.integration.test.ts` for a real local registry failure and integrity-matched resume.
2. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-git.integration.test.ts` for real bare-Git atomic ref publication and rejection.
3. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-contract.test.ts` for cohort, dependency graph, exact-version, reserved-version, deterministic serialization, and state-machine invariants.
4. `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-candidate.test.ts` and `release-manifests.test.ts` for immutable `.tgz` creation and transactional manifest behavior.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun run install:light

bun test --coverage \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/workspaces-lib.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-contract.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-candidate.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-manifests.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-registry.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-verdaccio.integration.test.ts \
  packages/scripts/__tests__/release-git.integration.test.ts

bun run verify
bun run audit:error-policy-ratchet
bun run audit:publish-graph
bun run audit:test-realness
actionlint .github/workflows/release.yaml
```

Observed results:

- Focused suite: **55 pass, 0 fail, 163 assertions** across seven files; overall focused line coverage **91.26%**.
- Real Verdaccio: publication was interrupted by terminating the registry before package two, leaving one exact-integrity match and one missing version; restarting the same registry/storage resumed only the missing tarball, verified both packages, promoted `beta`, removed the staging tag, made the next run a no-op, and rejected a changed registry URL.
- Real bare Git: an update hook rejected the tag and the atomic push changed neither branch nor tag; retry after removing the hook moved exactly the named branch and tag while leaving an unrelated local tag unpublished. Moved branch state, conflicting tags, changed retry evidence, and reserved tags all failed without mutation.
- Full verification: **573 successful / 573 total** Turbo tasks; build/typecheck model, dependency graph, secret-leak, script, and test-realness audits passed; **28/28** dist-path consumers typechecked.
- No public npm package, dist-tag, Git branch, or Git tag was created or modified.

78:Tddf,## Stack

This is a reviewable hardening commit stacked on #16192 at exact base `47b5f2054145b8d4102e7471b8f26645c7802574`. It targets `fix/voice-realtime-ui-staging-wiring` so it can be reviewed and merged into the parent branch without overwriting Solace's work.

## What this closes

- obtains a fresh one-use consent nonce for every initial mint and reconnect;
- cancels provisional microphone and playback setup, including stalled AudioWorklet loading, without leaking a hot mic or AudioContext;
- serializes reconnects and prevents stale sockets, stops, and rapid identity changes from resurrecting or clobbering a newer lifecycle;
- makes actual composer clicks cancel pending realtime starts while preserving speaking-time barge-in;
- keeps manual and continuous voice on one mic owner, with batch fallback unchanged when realtime is unavailable;
- surfaces autoplay unlock state and preserves low-latency streaming PCM playback;
- correlates mint/turn telemetry without exposing tokens, prompts, transcripts, or raw identifiers in the always-on HUD;
- fails staging deploys closed when Deepgram, Cartesia, or bridge auth is absent;
- keeps production realtime explicitly off, blocks repository-key fallback there, and makes a future enable require dedicated auth plus complete provider/runtime configuration;
- preserves the parent branch's Worker `ArrayBuffer` transport fix and browser/UI evidence.

- Worker + workflow contracts: **11/11**
- independent final review: **SHIP**, including the replay conflict resolutions and playback abort delta

- Biome, actionlint, and diff checks: pass

## What's in here

**Settings panel** (`packages/ui`, ai-model section):
- Three catalog-driven groups: Small / Large (provider → role-filtered model → per-model effort, prefilled from `GET /api/models/config` with source notes — "set by environment" etc., plus a shared-`OPENAI_REASONING_EFFORT` warning) and Coding sub-agent (codex/claude/opencode/eliza-code segmented backend, per-backend model list, codex efforts clamped to the pinned acp set, `costHint` + not-API-callable badges, default-backend switch).
- Chat saves arm an explicit **restart confirmation**, POST through a typed `updateModelsConfig` client verb (2xx/400/409 normalized into an applied/invalid/busy union), then poll status until running. Coding saves are restart-free ("applies to the next coding task").
- Typed 400s render inline with `context.supported`; 409 shows the active operation id; catalog load has distinct loading / designed-empty / error(+retry) states — a pre-stage-1 runtime yields a readable error, not a TypeError (visible in the settings-e2e screenshot below).
- All controls use the agent-addressable settings rows; `updateModelsConfig` registered in the view-action-ratchet registry.

**Slash commands** (`plugins/plugin-commands` + chat controller):
- `/model show` — current config with per-key source attribution (authorized).
- `/model small|large <model> [effort]`, `/model coding <backend> <model> [effort]` — validated writes through the same loopback route the panel uses; **owner-only**; chat writes announce the restart, coding writes say "no restart needed"; route 400s (incl. the codex-acp ultra/max pin gate) surface verbatim.
- Pre-existing `/model local|cloud` and bare per-room `/model <name>` behaviors preserved byte-identically (regression-pinned) — and `local|cloud` now carries the **same owner-only gate** (adversarial review found the global runtime switch had *no auth at all*; a connector guest could flip the inference backend).
- `dynamicChoices: "models"` in the chat composer no longer returns `[]` — completions come from the `/api/models` catalog, per-subcommand (chat providers' models vs coding backend models).

**Deterministic-command fix** (`packages/agent`) — found live, pre-existing:
- Document augmentation rewrote `message.content.text` into the contextual-documents envelope **before** the command action's `validate()` re-read it, so any slash command embedding-similar to stored docs (`/help`, `/model` on an agent with model docs) silently fell through to LLM improvisation. The pre-LLM gate matched the original text; validate saw the envelope. Command turns now skip augmentation before any retrieval work (they're instructions, not document questions). Live-proven: `/help`, `/model`, `/models` flipped from LLM-improvised to `Shortcut: cmd:*` deterministic replies.

## Evidence — live against the running agent

<details><summary>Deterministic slash battery (GUI chat route, before → after the augmentation fix)</summary>

```
BEFORE (thought field of the turn):
/help => LLM        /model show => LLM      /models => LLM      /whoami => Shortcut: cmd:whoami

AFTER:
/help       => Shortcut: cmd:help    (real command list)
/model show => Shortcut: cmd:model   ("Model configuration: **small** OPENAI_SMALL_MODEL = gemma-4-31b (config.env) ...")
/models     => Shortcut: cmd:models
/model      => Shortcut: cmd:model
```
</details>

<details><summary>Owner write + rejection through the slash path (live)</summary>

```
/model coding codex gpt-5.6-luna high
→ "Coding model for codex set to gpt-5.6-luna at high effort — applies to new coding sessions, no restart needed."
→ eliza.json: ELIZA_CODEX_MODEL_POWERFUL=gpt-5.6-luna, ELIZA_CODEX_EFFORT=high

/model coding codex gpt-5.6-terra xhigh   (restore)
→ persisted: gpt-5.6-terra xhigh

/model coding codex gpt-5.6-terra ultra
→ "Couldn't update the model config: Effort \"ultra\" is valid for gpt-5.6-terra but not parseable by the pinned codex-acp adapter (supported until the pin is bumped: low, medium, high, xhigh)"
```
</details>

<details><summary>Auth: fail-closed proven live</summary>

A non-owner (webhook user) sent `/model coding codex gpt-5.4 low` on Discord: the persisted config stayed untouched (`gpt-5.6-terra xhigh`). Note the message was *plain text* (webhooks cannot invoke native Discord slash commands); with a leading bot-mention the deterministic layer can't see the command and the planner **hallucinated a success reply** while the write path held — that planner-honesty gap + the mention-stripping gap are filed as a follow-up issue, they are not introduced here. The new `/model` subcommands also surface as real native Discord commands via the existing `catalog-commands` bridge (execute-time role gate applies).
</details>

# What this fixes

The Android lane had several independent false-proof paths:

- `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` executes each nonblank script line in a separate `sh -c`, so multiline Bash state, traps, PIDs, continuations, and `pipefail` were lost;
- a cold native `appUrlOpen` could arrive while the startup splash listener was mounted but still restoring reset/session state; reading `gatewayUrl` falsely acknowledged the command immediately before restore cleared the newly selected remote;
- hosted emulators expose a hardware keyboard, suppressing the real software IME even after the probe requested it, and the verifier sampled UIAutomator only once;
- Android toybox did not finalize `screenrecord` through the previous `pkill -INT` path, leaving an `ftyp/free/mdat` file with no `moov` box;
- failed verifier commands and non-finalized recordings could still look like uploaded proof.

The branch moves emulator work into committed Bash processes, queues and drains early native deep links, exposes renderer readiness, acknowledges/replays CONNECT delivery within a bounded window, and ensures only the correct startup owner can claim a deep link. It also forces the emulator software-IME policy, polls the actual IME hierarchy, terminates `screenrecord` by PID with SIGINT, waits for encoder/transport exit, and rejects incomplete ISO-BMFF output.

# Final remote-loopback correction

The final hosted run exposed one more device-only ambiguity: the remote-mac harness deliberately forwards the host through `http://127.0.0.1:31337`, while the Android client previously treated every loopback-shaped URL as the bundled native agent. That hijacked the forwarded request and returned `local_agent_unavailable` even though the remote coordinator was healthy.

The exact head now resolves Android routing from persisted runtime mode, durable Cloud-session precedence, and build defaults before deciding whether loopback HTTP belongs to the native bridge. IPC remains unambiguously native. The mounted-App regression drives a real `CONNECT_EVENT` through deferred first-run adoption and asserts the observable `adopt → complete → retry` outcome sequence, remote mode, shell state, and success notice.

Final local gates: 10 changed UI test files pass 143/143; the enforced changed-file coverage gate remains above 50% for every executable target (81.05% mean for the final routing slice); test-realness reports zero regressions; and `bun run verify` passes 573/573 Turbo tasks plus all audits and 28/28 dist-path consumers.

3. `packages/app/scripts/lib/android-capture.mjs` and `packages/app/scripts/android-assistant-verify.mjs`;

7e:T15e6,# Relates to

Relates to #16277.

Dependency #16276 is already merged through #16285. This branch is rebased directly onto that prerequisite at `42838644ed2c871916a1151db145825a1234497d`.

# Outcome

Replaces the legacy single-job release script with an explicit three-stage transaction:

1. A credential-free candidate job verifies the exact repository/ref/SHA, builds and packs the reviewed cohort once, and records immutable plan, cohort, manifest, entrypoint, and tarball integrity.
2. A least-privilege npm job authenticates the planned publisher, binds the normalized registry before mutation, stages missing immutable versions under a cohort-derived candidate tag, verifies the full cohort, promotes the public channel, and removes the staging tags.
3. A finalizer re-reads npm without credentials, binds the Git push destination, pushes only the exact planned annotated tag, and creates or verifies a GitHub Release whose repository, tag, target commit, and prerelease identity match the plan.

Every mutation command requires the complete release identity plus the canonical plan digest. Exclusive state locks and monotonic `registry-bound` / `git-bound` transitions make interruption retries idempotent without permitting a target, publisher, candidate-tag, ref, or artifact swap.

# Pack-once invariant

The obsolete `verify-npm-pack-dist.mjs` helper is removed because its only caller was the legacy workflow and it rebuilt plus dry-packed packages a second time. Validation is not reduced: candidate construction now performs the single real `npm pack`, checks the actual `.tgz` bytes, verifies every declared `main` / `module` / `types` / `bin` / concrete `exports` target is present, records SHA-512 hex and SRI, and revalidates the same immutable tarballs before every side effect. Removing the legacy helper is what preserves the issue's build/test/pack-once contract and clears the script-orphan audit.

# Risk and rollout

Risk is high because this is public release infrastructure. The workflow remains manual/reusable, uses one global non-cancelling concurrency group, reserves failed beta versions, separates trusted tooling from candidate source, and never pushes a branch. This PR did not contact public npm for publication or create/update any public Git tag or GitHub Release.

# Risks

Medium. This changes when a successful inference-auth cache population write completes on Cloudflare Workers. The API key, user/organization, and moderation checks still finish authoritatively before authorization returns; invalid, revoked/inactive, suspended, unavailable-cache, and malformed-input paths remain fail-closed. Worker requests register the positive write with `executionCtx.waitUntil`; non-Worker/test callers continue to await it inline. Cache invalidation coverage protects lifecycle transitions.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds bounded, privacy-safe, trace-correlated timings for credential extraction, cache availability/read/write, API-key lookup, user/org lookup, moderation, and total auth resolution.
- Makes inference-auth cache availability and outcomes explicit instead of conflating unavailable/malformed data with a healthy miss.
- Uses a focused inference API-key resolver that preserves the existing errors, validation order, and usage accounting while allowing controlled probes and cache-failure recovery to measure authoritative repositories.
- Defers only the post-authorization positive KV population write to the Worker lifetime, with a second correlated completion record for the actual backend outcome and duration.
- Invalidates positive auth entries across API-key lifecycle and moderation changes.
- Adds an exact-version staging probe and GitHub workflow that deploys a uniquely named workers.dev-only diagnostic Worker, creates an isolated auth fixture, captures 30 retained hits + 10 controlled misses + fail-closed guards, sanitizes Tail logs, enforces thresholds, and always removes the Worker and fixture.
- Proves Worker Tail readiness with a real authenticated trace before retaining samples. The readiness boundary retries only the observed transient workers.dev propagation 404, fails immediately on other HTTP statuses, and emits only sanitized Wrangler stderr on exhaustion.
- Adds a narrow Gitleaks exception for the deterministic public `unit-probe-token:<32 lowercase hex>` test fixture in its single test file; the rest of that file and the full branch range remain scanned.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and performance improvement for the cloud inference-auth hot path, plus diagnostic/test infrastructure.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public documentation change is needed. The new workflow and probe are self-describing operational diagnostics, and the runtime contract remains unchanged.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

2. Download the [sanitized report and correlated Tail logs](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29322590272/artifacts/8306610038), artifact `inference-auth-staging-460efacc78d640c1269234a17f4ab25c32f431bd`.
3. Inspect `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-auth-context.ts`, then `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-api-key-auth.ts`, `packages/scripts/cloud/inference-auth-latency.mjs`, and `.github/workflows/chat-latency-live.yml`.

## Detailed testing steps

The final exact-version run produced:

| Auth interval | n | p50 | p90 | p95 | max |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Exact-version cache hit | 30 | 2 ms | 3 ms | 3 ms | 6 ms |
| Controlled cache miss | 10 | 271 ms | 281 ms | 406 ms | 406 ms |

The controlled-miss p90 is **91.2% lower** than the issue's 3,204 ms evidence, with no multi-second auth tail. Controlled-miss stage p90s: key 57 ms, user/org 102 ms, moderation 53 ms, KV read 73 ms. Deferred KV writes completed at p90 254 ms; 10/10 were observed as `written`. The single 890 ms deferred-write maximum remained outside the response path.

Worker Tail readiness required six authenticated probes before capture. Only after that trace appeared did the workflow retain the 43 measured requests, eliminating the process-live/websocket-not-ready race that an earlier diagnostic run exposed.

Fail-closed guards in the same run:

- Invalid key: HTTP 401, authoritative `rejected`, write `not_run`.
- Suspended key: HTTP 403, authoritative `suspended`, write `not_run`.
- Forged controlled-probe request: HTTP 400, cached authorization with probe `off`; the request could not force an authoritative miss.

The [pre-optimization exact-version run](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29314229862) measured controlled-miss p90 679 ms and KV-write p90 277 ms, isolating the positive KV write as the dominant controllable boundary.

81:T2914,## Summary

- run the scheduled iOS Local lane through the canonical composition: fresh simulator app install, OS-delivered auth callback, then the strict full-Bun local-model probe
- write/read Capacitor preferences inside the simulator domain, repair the Xcode 16.4 simulator Swift-library lookup for `simctl` children, and pre-approve the app's URL scheme without replacing unrelated LaunchServices entries
- share one strict app-core full-Bun probe with the renderer, preserve the exact model input/output receipt, and render an explicit terminal pass/fail state only after the terminal receipt is persisted
- split platform entrypoint seams into source modules with honest source-path tests; changed-source coverage passes without a classifier or policy exemption
- hard-gate the redacted scheme/auth/model receipts, exact head and model hashes, unified logs, JPG, and H.264 MP4

Addresses #13577. Scoped contribution to #13562.

## Root cause and repair

The hosted lane exposed three independent simulator boundaries in sequence:

1. A host-path `defaults write` could exit successfully without producing simulator-readable Capacitor Preferences. The lane now executes `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults ...`, supports Capacitor's prefixed key plus its raw alias, and requires a native readback before launch.
2. Xcode 16.4 with the iOS 18.5 simulator could launch-abort while resolving `/usr/lib/swift/libswiftWebKit.dylib`. The workflow supplies Apple's simulator-runtime Cryptex fallback only to `simctl` child processes; it does not change the production deployment target.
3. A fresh simulator presented an untappable `Open in “Eliza”?` sheet for `simctl openurl`, leaving auth at `phase=requested`. The orchestrator now preserves the existing LaunchServices dictionary, writes and reads back `com.apple.CoreSimulator.CoreSimulatorBridge-->elizaos = ai.elizaos.app`, emits a redacted receipt, and reboots once when that mapping changes.

Mutation-sensitive contracts prove unapproved=`confirmation-blocked`, approved=`deliver-to-app`, wrong-bundle repair, idempotence, simulator-native preference readback, and the complete strict non-streaming + streaming model contract.

The target [iOS Local (full-Bun) Simulator Smoke job 87030207735](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29316042667/job/87030207735) passed every step, including the composed auth + full-Bun E2E.

82:T1aa1,Closes #16442. One of the five cards that replaced the closed monolith #16200 (rebuilt narrowly on `develop`, not cherry-picked).

## Problem

Draft #16200 mounted a separate `ShellControllerProvider` in **each** desktop webview, so every window instantiated its own `useShellController` engine — duplicated chat sessions, fighting microphone/audio owners, and divergent conversation state. On current `develop` the same hazard exists: the `chat-overlay` (bottom-bar) and `tray-popover` windows each fall through to the full `App`, each mounting its own engine + mic.

## Design — one authoritative owner per process

- **Election.** Each window runs one `ShellControllerCoordinator`. They share a presence table and elect a single owner deterministically by `(priority, windowId)` — `main` (0) < `chat-overlay` (1) < `surface` (2) < `tray-popover` (3). Only the owner mounts the engine (the sole mic/audio owner). Followers render the owner's snapshot and forward commands. A joining window waits a short discovery grace before it may claim, so it hears an existing owner rather than briefly flashing a second engine. Lone/main windows own immediately, so **web, mobile, and single-window desktop behave exactly as before** (no transport ⇒ no bus traffic).
- **Handoff / crash / restart.** Owner leaves (clean `bye`) or crashes (heartbeat TTL prune) ⇒ the lowest-ranked live window is promoted; a promoted owner starts a strictly higher `epoch` so a dead owner's late snapshots are dropped. A restarted `main` window reclaims ownership.
- **Mic / audio ownership.** Exactly one window ever opens a mic or plays TTS (the owner). A follower's "start voice" is a *command*, never a local capture.

## Typed IPC contract

- **Commands** (follower → owner): a discriminated union (`send`, `startRecording`, `toggleHandsFree`, `speak`, `recheckMicPermission`, `navConversation`, …) — no `any`, no boolean-only dispatch. Idempotent: the owner dedupes by `commandId`, so a redelivered command re-acks but applies once. Replies (`ack`) are correlated by `commandId`; a failure (owner gone / timeout / version-mismatch) rejects visibly and surfaces a notice — never a silent no-op.
- **Snapshots** (owner → followers): ordered by `(epoch, seq)`; stale/reordered snapshots are dropped. The snapshot is the serialisable read-projection of the controller (the non-serialisable `AnalyserNode` and the imperative methods are excluded).
- **Presence + version.** Every envelope carries `protocolVersion`; an incompatible peer still competes for ownership (so no duplicate engine spawns across a version boundary) but a follower of an incompatible owner degrades to a visible `version-mismatch` state instead of rendering an envelope it can't interpret. All IPC input is validated at the boundary (`parseShellSyncEnvelope`).
- **Transport.** Production relays through the Electrobun native main process: a renderer publishes via the `shellControllerRelay` RPC; the main process fans it out to every window as a `shellControllerSync` push. The relay is a dumb pipe (the coordinator owns all semantics, and filters its own echo).

## Files changed

New renderer module `packages/ui/src/components/shell/shell-controller-sync/`: `protocol.ts`, `coordinator.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `transport.ts` (+ in-memory bus), `follower-controller.ts`, `apply-command.ts`, `electrobun-transport.ts`, `useShellControllerSync.ts`, plus `__tests__/`. Provider split in `ShellControllerContext.tsx` (owner mounts the engine; follower never does). Native relay: `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/shell-sync-relay.ts` + wiring in `index.ts` / `rpc-handlers.ts` / `rpc-schema.ts`.

## Tests — 62 passing (real behaviour, no mock of the unit)

Coordinator over a **real in-memory bus** (several coordinators, one bus): two windows electing one owner; follower renders the owner's snapshot; two followers racing start/stop/send (owner applies each once); idempotent redelivery; command timeout / no-owner rejection; crash re-election + epoch fencing of zombie snapshots; clean handoff; main-restart reclaim; version-mismatch degrade; join-grace. Plus protocol/snapshot/transport/apply-command/follower-controller units, a jsdom two-window `useShellControllerSync` integration (single-owner + snapshot + command via real hooks), the provider split, and the native relay routing. Log: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-2/16442-controller-tests.txt

## What still needs a real desktop pass (draft)

I could not boot the Electrobun multi-window desktop in this environment, so the **native RPC round-trip and the multi-window visual capture are unverified end-to-end**. A desktop-capable pass must: (1) open two windows (main + tray-popover / detached surface) and confirm exactly ONE mic opens and one conversation state (owner drives, follower renders); (2) close the owner and confirm handoff (engine tears down in the old owner, stands up in the promoted window); (3) capture before/after screenshots + a walkthrough video for the visual evidence rows below; (4) confirm the `shellControllerRelay`/`shellControllerSync` messages traverse the native relay. The renderer transport is unit-tested against a fake bridge and the relay routing is unit-tested, but the live native hop is the remaining gap.

84:T1a40,"## Problem\n\nNothing deterministic stood between a sub-agent's \"done\" claim and the task being declared done:\n\n- **No residuals check existed anywhere** — no `git status --porcelain`, no unpushed-commit check; \"tests pass / no leftovers\" was decided by an LLM reading self-reported envelopes and regex-mined transcript text.\n- `validateTask` wrote `status:\"done\"` **directly**, bypassing the lifecycle transition table; `humanOverride` could promote a task from ANY status with a fabricated default evidence string, via plain `POST /tasks/:id/validate`.\n- Operator paths (`retryTurn`/`rerunFromEvent`/`restartTask`/`reopenTask`) also bypassed the table, contradicting the table module's \"single legal way\" contract.\n- The user-facing completion relay never consulted verification state — the user could be told \"done\" while verification was still running or failing.\n- Criteria-free tasks skipped verification entirely; a dead duplicate of the goal-contract module diverged from the live one.\n\n## Fix\n\n- New `completion-residuals.ts` — deterministic gate run **before any model spend** in `autoVerifyCompletion` and before honoring `passed:true` in `validateTask`: real `git status --porcelain`, `git rev-list @{u}..HEAD` (when an upstream exists), envelope legs (`testResults[].exitCode !== 0`, non-empty `residualRisks`). Statuses: `clean | residuals | unverifiable`. Fail-closed (`unverifiable`) when the task **expects a repo** (`session.repo` or `task.boundRepo`); for unbound tasks the git legs run opportunistically when the workdir is a real git worktree and skip otherwise (every ACP session has a scratch workdir — a voice/Q&A task must not be blocked by a non-git scratch dir; caught by merged-tree scenario verification). Default ON; `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_RESIDUALS_GATE=0` disables.\n- Blocked completions re-engage the worker through the existing `reEngageOrEscalate` loop with a correction enumerating the exact residuals, bounded by `MAX_AUTO_VERIFY_ATTEMPTS`, parking `waiting_on_user` at cap. The snapshot is persisted on task metadata + validation events so the UI shows what blocked (or was overridden at) completion.\n- **All durable status writes now route through the transition table**: new `human_override_passed/failed` triggers (non-terminal states only), `restarted` made total; `retryTurn`/`rerunFromEvent` via `retrying`, `restartTask`/`reopenTask` via `restarted`/`reopened`. The table's \"no other legal way\" doc is now true.\n- `humanOverride` hardened: requires a non-empty evidence string (route returns 400 without it), illegal from terminal states, and records the residuals snapshot it overrode.\n- User-facing completion relay frames replies by the durable task's verification state: `validating` → \"verification still running\"; re-engaged after a failed verify → provisional; unchanged when done/record-less.\n- Deleted dead `goal-contract.ts` duplicate (zero production consumers; `acceptance-criteria.ts` is the live implementation).\n\n## Evidence\n\n- `completion-residuals` tests: **19 passed** — real temp repos with a real bare upstream: dirty tree, unpushed commits, clean+pushed, bound vs unbound × git vs non-git matrix. # Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Introduces the **one typed composer-bridge contract** (`eliza.native-composer/v1`) so an iOS / Android / desktop shell can push content INTO the chat composer and drive it, and the renderer can report the composer's state back — without coupling to the #16200 UI monolith.

- **`contract.ts`** — two versioned discriminated-union logs: `ComposerOperation` (native → renderer: `text.insert`/`text.set`, `attachment.add`/`remove`, `reply.set`/`clear`, `mention.add`, `send`, `cancel`, `focus.set`, `voice.handoff`) and `ComposerEvent` (renderer → native: `draft.changed`, `send.result`, `focus.changed`, `voice.state`). Applying an op returns a typed **`DispatchResult`** (`applied` / `duplicate` / `deferred` / `rejected{reason}`) — **never a bare boolean**. Attachment sources are media-store vocabulary only (`inline` / `data-url` / `remote` / `stored`) — **there is no file-id variant, so a second file store is structurally unrepresentable**.
- **`decode.ts`** — strict boundary decoder. Every field of an untrusted op/attachment is validated; a malformed frame yields a typed `{ok:false,error}` (error-policy **J3**), never a throw on per-op input and never a fabricated default. Structurally rejects any second-store shape (an unknown attachment `source`, e.g. `file-id`, is refused).
- **`attachments.ts`** — normalizes a native attachment into the **existing content-addressed store's** vocabulary: `inline`/`data-url` → a `data:` URL the existing outgoing pipeline (`api/media-runtime.ts` → `persistAttachmentUrlIfInline`) persists to `${STATE_DIR}/media/<sha256>` on send; `remote` → an http(s) URL flagged for the existing server-side SSRF-guarded rehost; `stored` → an `/api/media/<hash>` passthrough. First-line private-host guard; the server's DNS-pinned SSRF guard (`packages/core/src/network`) remains authoritative. **No new store, no file IDs.**
- **`reduce.ts`** — the state machine / behavioral spec: `opId` idempotency (duplicate native callback = no-op), offline-send deferral + reconnect replay, cancellation (`send` vs `draft` scope), and permission/limit enforcement.
- **`client.ts`** — the renderer-side bridge client: decode → reduce → emit events; `serialize()`/`hydrate()` preserve the draft + idempotency ledger + offline queue across app backgrounding, window reload, and reconnect. Malformed native input degrades to `rejected: "invalid-input"` — one bad frame never tears down a live composer.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking; additive new module + subpath export).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project-docs change required; the module is self-documenting (prose headers + JSDoc), matching the sibling `native-transcript` contract.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/ui/src/native-composer/contract.ts` (the shape), then the four test files that pin the whole acceptance case matrix.

## Detailed testing steps

```
bunx vitest run src/native-composer/   # (cwd packages/ui) -> 5 files, 56 tests passed
bunx tsgo --noEmit -p packages/ui      # -> exit 0, no diagnostics
```

Case matrix covered (per-file vitest, deterministic, no mocks standing in for the thing under test):

- **decode.test.ts** — field-level rejection of every op type; attachment-source validation incl. **structural rejection of a `file-id` second-store shape**; mixed-batch stream decode keeps good ops + collects malformed with index/reason.
- **attachments.test.ts** — inline/data-url/remote/stored normalization; **oversized** cap; malformed mime; non-base64; **private-host SSRF block** (localhost/127/10/192.168/169.254/internal); non-http scheme; non-content-addressed stored URL; asserts no `fileId` on the produced attachment.
- **reduce.test.ts** — text/mention/reply edits + oversized rejection; attachment add/remove; **permission-denied** (attach & voice capabilities absent); attachment count cap; send empty/happy/**in-flight concurrency**/**offline-deferred + flush replay**; **cancel** (send vs draft); **duplicate-opId idempotency**; draft-revision preservation; `resolveSend` success clears / failure keeps.
- **client.test.ts** — malformed raw input → typed rejection (never throws); event emission (draft/focus/voice/send.result); **serialize→hydrate reload preserves idempotency + deferred offline send**; batch replay de-dupes after reconnect.
- **index.test.ts** — the `@elizaos/ui/native-composer` subpath re-exports the schema/decoder/reducer/client, and the client factory works when imported from the barrel.

## Web-verified vs device-gated (explicit)

- **Web-verified here:** the contract, boundary decoder, attachment normalizer, reducer state machine, and renderer-side client — the full case matrix, as fast per-file vitest (results above). The attachment routing reuses the existing content-addressed media store + SSRF guard; no second store or file IDs are introduced (asserted in `attachments.test.ts`).
- **Store + SSRF routing is proven end-to-end by an existing real test** — the normalizer emits exactly the URL vocabulary the current outgoing pipeline consumes, and `packages/agent/src/api/media-runtime.test.ts` already drives that pipeline against a **real** temp-dir content-addressed store: an inline `data:` URL lands content-addressed at `/api/media/<sha256>.png`, and a link-local `169.254.169.254` host is **SSRF-blocked** (kept + flagged ephemeral). This PR deliberately adds no second store and no server code, so that real test is the routing proof for criterion #3; the two halves (normalizer vocabulary ⇄ existing store/SSRF pipeline) meet at the store URL and are each independently proven with real (non-mock) tests.
- **Device-gated remainder (NOT done here — needs a real iOS-sim / Android-emu / desktop pass):** the native SwiftUI / Android / Electrobun shells that *emit* these operations and *consume* these events. No native composer bridge is tracked on `develop` today (the earlier #16200 native work exists only in gitignored generated project dirs), so there was nothing to wire. This PR defines the contract so those shells **can** conform. I ran **no** simulator/emulator/desktop pass and claim **no** native verification.

## Known gaps / failures

- Native (iOS/Android/desktop) wiring is intentionally out of scope for this card, as above — the contract is the agent-completable core; native conformance is device-gated and belongs to a follow-up with a real device pass.
- Full workspace `bun run verify` / build lanes were not run locally (long-running; watchdog-bounded env); scoped vitest + `tsgo` were run instead and CI runs the full lanes.

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes GitHub credential ownership, startup hydration, device-flow routes, and client refresh behavior. The main risks are cross-agent credential disclosure, accepting a cancelled/superseded grant, losing an existing credential during reconnect, and stale Octokit/git clients. The tests exercise two-agent isolation, encrypted persistence, restart hydration, cancellation/supersession races, typed OAuth failures, reconnect preservation, and client refresh generation guards.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Stores GitHub OAuth/PAT credentials in the existing encrypted vault under agent-scoped keys and hydrates only the matching runtime without mutating `process.env`.
- Replaces placeholder device authorization behavior with typed start/poll/cancel/reconnect routes. Device codes stay server-side behind opaque local flow IDs; expiry, denial, cancellation, `slow_down`, upstream timeout, and newest-flow-wins behavior are explicit.
- Revalidates a flow after an in-flight GitHub token poll so cancellation or supersession cannot revive and persist that flow.
- Validates granted tokens through GitHub `/user`, commits to the vault before refreshing runtime clients, and preserves the current credential when reconnect fails.
- Removes app-core's host-global credential file/environment bootstrap and prevents orchestrator git children from inheriting ambient GitHub token variables. Authenticated git commands receive the selected agent credential through a per-command `extraheader`.
- Makes the guided credential the default agent GitHub identity while retaining explicit role/account precedence and PAT entry.
- Renders distinct loading, empty, unavailable/error, waiting, cancelled, denied, expired, success, and reconnect states.
- Updates the connector and package documentation, including the single-agent-only limitation of owner-provided environment configuration.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking behavior repair for agent isolation and guided GitHub OAuth).

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated in `packages/docs/connectors/github.md` and the affected plugin READMEs/package guides.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review the desktop/mobile GitHub card captures below, especially waiting, cancelled, expired, denied, unavailable/retry, success, and connected/reconnect.
2. With the elizaOS OAuth App client ID configured as `GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`, start Device Flow for agent A, approve it, and confirm agent A becomes connected.
3. In the same process, confirm agent B remains disconnected and cannot poll, read, overwrite, or clear agent A's flow/credential.
4. Perform a real GitHub operation as agent A, restart the runtime, and confirm the same identity is hydrated from the encrypted vault.
5. Reconnect agent A and deny/cancel the grant; confirm the existing identity remains active.

8b:T1b16,## Summary

Closes #16441. One of five cards replacing the closed monolith #16200 — this one
delivers the **unified structural intent vocabulary + routing authority** for
chat, voice, and transcription launches, independently rebuilt on `develop`
(nothing cherry-picked from #16200).

Today every launch surface is ad-hoc: iOS App Intents / Siri, Android app-actions
+ shortcuts, desktop deep links, tray/widget controls, and notification taps each
emit an `elizaos://…?action=<free-form string>&text=…&voice=1` deep link, and the
only consumer (`assistant-launch-payload.ts`) handles **chat send only**, keyed on
a free-form `action` string with a plain in-memory `Set` for dedupe. There is no
typed vocabulary, no voice/transcription launch path, no prerequisite handling,
and no dedupe across windows / restored sessions.

This PR adds `packages/ui/src/os-intent/` — the one place a launch is expressed as
a typed `OsIntent` and routed by **structural fields only** to the single shared
shell controller.

### Intent vocabulary (`contract.ts`)

Discriminated-union intents, each with a stable `intentId` (idempotency key),
`source`, optional `issuedAt`, declared prerequisites, target, and typed outcomes:

| Intent | Target | Auto-start | Prerequisites |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `open-chat` | chat | no | session |
| `send` | chat | no | session |
| `start-voice` (`converse`/`dictate`) | voice | **yes** | session, unlocked, foreground, microphone, voice-capture |
| `stop-voice` | voice | no | — (always allowed) |
| `start-transcription` | transcription | **yes** | unlocked, foreground, microphone, voice-capture |
| `stop-transcription` | transcription | no | — (always allowed) |
| `continue-conversation` | chat | no | session |

### Routing authority (`router.ts` + `dedupe.ts`)

`routeIntent(intent, context, dedupeStore)` returns a typed `IntentOutcome`
(`routed` / `duplicate` / `stale` / `blocked` / `consent-required` / `degraded`)
and, when routed, the `IntentControllerCommand[]` to apply. Evaluation order:

1. **Stale** — `issuedAt` older than `maxIntentAgeMs` → `stale` (never fires a
   launch the user has moved on from).
2. **Duplicate** — `intentId` already applied → `duplicate`. Only *routed* intents
   are recorded, so a `blocked` intent retried after the user grants permission
   still routes.
3. **Degraded** — device cannot honor it (voice unsupported / sandboxed) →
   `degraded` (visible unavailable state, never a silent no-op).
4. **Blocked** — recoverable prerequisite unmet (unauthenticated / auth-expired /
   locked / backgrounded / microphone-denied) → `blocked` with the missing
   prerequisites.
5. **Consent** — an auto-start (mic) intent without recorded consent →
   `consent-required` (explicit, reversible, consent-aware).
6. **Routed** — emit commands + record `intentId`.

Routing is **structural only** — it switches on `intent.type` and reads the
context; it never inspects prompt or transcript text. The free-form native
`action` string is mapped to a typed intent once, at the decode boundary.

**Idempotent dedupe across every redelivery path** — deep-link retries, re-tapped
notifications, multiple windows, and restored/crashed-then-reopened sessions all
carry the same `intentId`. `IntentDedupeStore` is a pure, clock-injected,
TTL-pruned store with `snapshot()` / seeding so a restored session rehydrates the
already-applied set and does not re-fire.

### Boundary decoder (`decode.ts`)

`decodeOsIntent(raw)` and `decodeDeepLinkIntent(url)` validate untrusted intent
payloads (deep links, notifications, native bridges) into a typed `OsIntent` or an
explicit typed failure (`{ ok: false, error }`) — never a fabricated-valid
default, never a throw on a single bad frame (error-policy J3). Matches the typed
transcript-event decoder style landed by sibling #16440.

### One engine (`apply-command.ts`)

`applyOsIntentCommand(controller, command)` runs a routed command against the one
live `ShellController` — the same engine the shared cross-window controller
(#16442) owns. The `IntentControllerCommand` union is the subset of #16442's
`ShellControllerCommand` that intents produce, kept structurally identical so no
intent ever spins up a second chat/voice session. Adapters
(`fromAssistantLaunchPayload`, `fromDeepLinkUrl`) let the existing entry points
conform.

## Test evidence

Comprehensive per-file vitest (jsdom/node, deterministic) covering the full case
matrix from the card: invalid/stale intents, locked device, missing permissions,
auth expiry, concurrency (interleaved routing sharing one dedupe store),
background/foreground, crash recovery (snapshot → rehydrate → no re-fire), and
duplicate-start prevention across deep-links / notifications / windows / retries /
restored sessions. Run: `bun run --cwd packages/ui test os-intent`.

## Scope / what is device-gated

- **Web/desktop-verifiable (this PR):** the vocabulary, decoder, dedupe store,
  routing authority, and command executor — all covered by fast vitest.
- **Device-gated (NOT verified here — no device pass run):** wiring the vocabulary
  into the live app-shell launch consumer and the per-platform native invocation
  (iOS App Intents / Siri, Android app-actions + Quick Settings tiles, desktop
  deep-link/tray/hotkey) requires real iOS-sim / Android-emu / packaged-desktop
  passes. The vocabulary + adapters are defined so those surfaces can conform;
  the deep-link `action` strings the native spine already emits (`ask`, `chat`,
  `voice`, `smart-reply`) map onto typed intents in `decode.ts`. I did **not** run
  device verification and do not claim it.

## Evidence

Non-visual TypeScript library module (`packages/ui/src/os-intent/*.ts`) + vitest.
No React component, view, route, or agent/model surface is touched.

<!-- evidence-row:before-screenshots -->
- [x] N/A - no UI surface changed; pure `.ts` intent-routing library + vitest (no `.tsx`, no view/route).
<!-- evidence-row:after-screenshots -->
- [x] N/A - no UI surface changed; pure `.ts` intent-routing library + vitest (no `.tsx`, no view/route).
<!-- evidence-row:walkthrough-video -->
- [x] N/A - no user-exercisable UI flow; module is not yet wired into the live launch consumer (device-gated, see Scope).
<!-- evidence-row:backend-logs -->
- [x] N/A - no server code path; this is a client-side UI library module with no backend route.
<!-- evidence-row:frontend-logs -->
- [x] N/A - no rendered surface; behavior is proven by deterministic vitest over the full case matrix instead.
<!-- evidence-row:llm-trajectory -->
- [x] N/A - no agent/action/provider/prompt/model behavior changes; routing is structural and never invokes a model.
<!-- evidence-row:domain-artifacts -->
- [x] N/A - produces no persisted domain artifacts; outputs are typed in-memory outcomes/commands asserted in tests.

8c:T1d70,# Model configuration API — catalog + validated config routes + spawn wiring (stage 1)

Lets an operator set the **small / large / coding sub-agent** models (and per-model reasoning effort) through one validated API, with the config actually reaching the running system: chat-brain writes restart the runtime through the operation manager; coding writes apply to the **next spawn with no restart**.

## What's in here

**`GET /api/models` → `catalog`** (additive field) — provider→model catalog with per-model effort lists, defaults, roles, and cost hints:
- **codex**: static table for gpt-5.6-sol/terra/luna, gpt-5.5/5.4/5.4-mini, spark (`apiSupported:false`), live-merged with the operator's `$CODEX_HOME/models_cache.json` (server view wins per id; corrupt/absent cache degrades to the static table — error-policy:J4)
- **claude chat/coding**: fable-5 + opus-4-8/4-7/4-6 + sonnet + haiku with per-model effort gates (xhigh/max only where the API accepts them)
- **cerebras / elizacloud**: gemma-4-31b, zai-glm-4.7, gpt-oss-120b — all three carry the `low/medium/high` `reasoning_effort` knob (**live-probed 2026-07-12**: glm modulates 90→1337 reasoning chars low→high, gemma 663→1133)

**`GET/POST /api/models/config`** — validated writes + source-attributed reads:
- POST validates target/backend/provider/model/role/effort against the catalog and writes the dual config seam (`config.env` + `config.env.vars` + `process.env`)
- chat targets request a runtime restart via the operation manager; coding targets return `restart:false`
- GET reports every key with its resolution source (`config.env` / `process.env` / `default`)
- **codex-acp pin gate**: `max`/`ultra` are valid for gpt-5.6-terra per the live catalog, but the pinned `@zed-industries/codex-acp@0.14.0` config parser cannot parse them — writing them would fail the whole `config.toml` parse and drop the model pin. The route rejects them with a typed 400 naming the pin; the bridge carries a matching two-tier guard.

**Spawn wiring (the part that makes the keys real):**
- `coding-account-bridge`: per-account `CODEX_HOME/config.toml` now resolves model as explicit `ELIZA_CODEX_MODEL` pin > app-configured `ELIZA_CODEX_MODEL_POWERFUL` > operator machine config > `gpt-5.6-terra` default, and writes a validated `model_reasoning_effort` from `ELIZA_CODEX_EFFORT`. Before this the app-configured key was **write-only** — the machine `~/.codex/config.toml` silently won every spawn (caught by the live spawn proof below).
- `acp-service.buildEnv`: claude spawns read config-env `ELIZA_CLAUDE_EFFORT` → `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` (validated) and fall back to `ELIZA_CLAUDE_MODEL_POWERFUL` → `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` when the spawn carries no explicit model. Both are config-env reads: a UI/API save applies to the next spawn without restart.
- coding defaults: codex powerful `gpt-5.6-terra` (mirrored in `CODING_MODEL_DEFAULTS`), claude powerful `claude-opus-4-8`; plugin-anthropic default bumped opus-4-7 → opus-4-8.
