8d:T1870,## What

Implements the core of #16440: **one typed, append-only transcript-event contract
(`eliza.native-transcript/v1`)** meant to be rendered identically by the iOS,
Android, desktop, and web shells, plus the web/DOM renderer, a language-neutral
golden fixture, and a full conformance test matrix.

Rebuilt fresh from `develop`. Does **not** cherry-pick the closed monolith #16200,
and deliberately does **not** reintroduce its banned anti-pattern of bucketing
state by `text.length` — every reduction/render decision here is made from
**structural fields only** (the `type` discriminant, `phase`/`status`/`final`
flags, stable ids, and the monotonic `seq`).

## Contract (`packages/ui/src/native-transcript/`)

- `contract.ts` — discriminated-union `TranscriptEvent` (8 types: `stt.partial`,
  `stt.final`, `agent.text`, `tool.state`, `tts.audio`, `cancel`, `error`,
  `reconnect`) + the reduced `TranscriptViewModel` (ordered `TranscriptItem[]` +
  transient `speaking`/`connection`). Each event is a self-contained **snapshot**
  (not a delta), so a bridge that dupes/reorders/drops a frame never buffers.
- `decode.ts` — strict boundary decoder. Every field validated; a malformed frame
  yields a typed `{ ok: false, error }` (never a fabricated default, never a throw
  on per-event input) — error-policy **J3**. `decodeTranscriptStream` keeps good
  events and collects rejected ones with index + reason.
- `reduce.ts` — the pure reducer that IS the behavioral spec: **dedupe** (unique
  `seq` → repeat is a no-op), **stable ordering** (item renders at its first-seen
  `seq`), **late-event** guard (per-item `revSeq`; a late partial after a final is
  dropped), and the **cancellation boundary** (`cancel` marks in-flight items
  cancelled and bumps their `revSeq` to the cancel `seq`, so pre-boundary
  stragglers drop while a post-boundary continuation still applies).
- `TranscriptEventView.tsx` — web/DOM renderer. Reuses the chat `parseSegments`
  pipeline for agent prose+code; renders each row from `item.kind`/`item.status`;
  `dir="auto"` bidi-resolves Unicode/RTL; the error row is a distinct
  `role="alert"` state (J4). No text/length inspection anywhere. Exposed as the
  `@elizaos/ui/native-transcript` subpath.

## Case-matrix conformance

`fixtures/native-transcript-golden.json` is the **language-neutral** golden
fixture (schema-tagged `{ schema, scenarios[] }`) so the iOS/Android native
decoders can load the exact same bytes and assert the same reduced model. The
conformance test decodes + reduces every scenario and deep-equals `expectView`.

Cases covered (golden fixture + focused unit tests): malformed, duplicated,
reordered, concurrent (interleaved turns), empty, Unicode/RTL, long, offline
(reconnect lost/restored), permission-denied (error), and cancellation boundary.

## Test results (web-verified, per-file vitest)

```
bunx vitest run src/native-transcript/    → 4 files, 42 tests passed
  contract.conformance.test.ts  18 passed   (golden matrix: decode + reduce)
  decode.test.ts                            (field-level rejections, stream guards)
  reduce.test.ts                            (dedupe no-op, late guard, terminal turn, long text)
  TranscriptEventView.test.tsx              (jsdom DOM: roles/status/dir/code/alert/speaking)
bunx tsgo --noEmit -p packages/ui          → EXIT 0 (no diagnostics)
```

## Web-verified vs device-gated (explicit)

- **Web-verified here:** contract, decoder, reducer, web renderer, and the full
  conformance matrix — all as fast per-file vitest (results above). Evidence
  screenshots/video are a lightweight standalone render of the component
  (`react-dom/server` → the real `parseSegments`+`CodeBlock`), since the renderer
  is not yet wired into a routed page (that integration is a separate UI task).
- **Device-gated remainder (NOT done here — needs a real iOS-sim / Android-emu
  pass):** the SwiftUI and Android-Views decoders that consume
  `native-transcript-golden.json`. No native transcript decoder is tracked on
  `develop` today (the earlier #16200 native work exists only in gitignored
  generated project dirs), so there was nothing to wire. This PR defines the
  contract + golden fixture so those decoders **can** conform; the fixture is the
  cross-language source of truth. I ran **no** iOS simulator or Android emulator
  and claim **no** native verification.

message with a lifted `js` code block + copy button, tool status row, RTL Arabic
+ emoji turn, a distinct permission-denied error alert, and the live speaking
indicator).

8e:T168f,## What & why

Next slice of the account-observability roadmap (after #16355). Users running a large credential pool get a proper **command-center table** instead of a stack of cards, so health, usage, resets, priority, and lease activity are legible at a glance and sortable.

Renders as a **desktop enhancement** (`min-width:1024px`, the same query `SettingsView` uses for the settings rail from #16354) once a provider pool has **>= 4 accounts**. Narrow/mobile widths and small pools keep the existing `AccountCard` stack (which carries the richer per-card affordances). The card view is untouched.

### Columns
account (label + email) · **health** badge (ok / needs-reauth / rate-limited / invalid, with `healthDetail` tooltip + rate-limit countdown) · session/weekly **usage** bars · **resets** (relative) · **priority** · **enabled** toggle · **last-used** (relative) · **leases** (feature-gated, see below) · **actions**.

### Row states & actions
- **Sortable** by health / usage / last-used / priority. Default surfaces worst health first (the reason to open the table). `aria-sort` lives on the header `<th>` cell.
- **needs-reauth / invalid** → a repair CTA that reuses the **existing #16314 credential-repair flow** (`replaceAccountId` path via `onReauthenticate`).
- **rate-limited** → a live "resets in …" countdown in the health cell.
- **disabled** rows are dimmed.
- Full card affordance parity preserved in the actions cell: **test**, **refresh usage**, **reorder** (priority up/down — targets the priority neighbour regardless of the current table sort), reauthenticate, and remove (confirm dialog). Every row action is an optional prop and hides when its handler is absent.
- Loading is upstream (the table only mounts with data). Empty/degenerate pool → an in-table empty row; the surface's overall empty state remains the existing add-account CTA in `AccountManagementPanel`.

### Feature-detection (does NOT depend on #16355 merging)
#16355 adds an `observability` field (`activeLeaseCount`, `lastLeaseAt`, `lastSelectedAt`, `servedLastRequest`) to each account. This PR feature-detects it: the **Leases column is hidden entirely** when no row carries observability, so it degrades gracefully on older hosts and pre-#16355 payloads — no column of lying zeros, no crash. The client types are augmented with the **optional** shape only.

### Design gate (#16367)
packages/ui tokens + lucide-react only, **no new deps**. The new files introduce **zero** `no-focus-ring-gate` / `no-widget-chrome-gate` offenders (verified against the source-scanning suites; the only account-surface offenders on `develop` are the pre-existing ones #16367 already targets, none in the new files).

90:Ta3c,## What
Two additions that let a user talk to the agent from a Discord **group DM** — a context where a bot can never read the message stream, only receive interactions:

1. **`/ask <message>`** — routes free text through the real message service and returns the agent's reply as the interaction response (chunked across `editReply` + `followUp` for >2000 chars). Works everywhere (servers, bot DMs, group DMs); degrades cleanly when the runtime/message service isn't ready or the message is empty.
2. **Opt-in group-DM / user-install commands** — `transformCommandToDiscordApi` sets `contexts: [guild, bot-DM, private-channel]` + `integrationTypes: [guild, user]` on non-guild-only commands when `DISCORD_USER_INSTALL` is enabled. Guild-only commands stay pinned to the guild context; an explicit `contexts` array still wins.

## Why off by default
Discord **rejects** user-install command registration unless the application is configured as user-installable in the developer portal. So `DISCORD_USER_INSTALL` defaults to `false` (current behavior: guild + bot-DM), and operators opt in after enabling User Install in the portal.

## Tests (10; full plugin suite 315/315)
- Transform opt-in matrix: default omits contexts; `userInstall` adds `[0,1,2]` + `[0,1]`; guild-only stays `[0]`; explicit `contexts` respected.
- `/ask` handler: routes text into the message service, DM channel-type off-guild, empty-message reject (no runtime touch), no-service graceful degrade, long-answer chunking across editReply + followUps.

91:T103b,## What

Makes the workspace-native **eliza-code ACP** first-use provisioning path (`ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` in `plugin-agent-orchestrator`) crash-safe and multi-process safe, per the design properties in #16169.

Previously `ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` built `dist/acp.js` with a naive missing-or-stale check and no cross-process coordination:
- concurrent first spawns could interleave writes and publish a corrupt executable,
- a crash after `bun build` truncated the artifact left a partial `acp.js` whose mtime looked fresh forever,
- the launch command was an unquoted string that `splitCommandLine` broke on Bun/output paths containing spaces.

## How

New `acp-provisioning.ts` implements the deterministic protocol required by the issue. `ensureWorkspaceElizaCodeAcp` becomes a thin adapter over it, preserving the existing string-command contract (byte-identical for quote/space-free paths).

- **Fenced mutual exclusion** — lock created with `open(..., "wx")` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL)`; record carries `{pid, fence, startedAtMs}`. Ownership is the fence token, never age or PID alone.
- **No stealing from a verified-live owner** — a waiter reclaims only when the owner PID is not alive; a dead owner is reclaimed immediately (crash recovery within the outer deadline).
- **PID-reuse disambiguation** — a "live" PID older than an absolute lifetime ceiling is treated as reused and reclaimed, resolving the ambiguity the issue calls out.
- **Single-winner reclaim** — an `O_EXCL` reclaim-intent gate ensures exactly one reclaimer replaces a stale lock and runs the build at a time (the shared `bun run build` canonical output is not safe to run concurrently). The intent records its holder PID so a crashed holder is cleared immediately, not after the ceiling.
- **Gap-free lock replacement** — reclaim installs the new lock via a temp+atomic-`rename`, so `paths.lock` is never momentarily absent (no window for a third `open("wx")` acquirer).
- **Atomic publish + completion marker** — build writes to a private per-fence temp, is validated (non-empty, marker present), then `rename`d into place; a `dist/.acp.done` marker records the published size+mtime. Freshness is keyed on the marker, so a bare/partial `acp.js` is never treated as fresh and a failed/invalid build never leaves a fresh-looking artifact.
- **Space/quote-safe command** — `formatAcpCommand` quotes only when needed and picks the quote char the path lacks, so paths with spaces or quotes round-trip through `splitCommandLine` (which has no escape grammar). Quote-free paths stay bare (unchanged behavior).

## Tests

`acp-provisioning.test.ts` (24 tests) simulates crash-at-each-step via injectable `build` / `isPidAlive` / `now` hooks and covers every required scenario:
- multiple processes reclaim one dead lock, exactly one build runs;
- a partial/unmarked artifact is never fresh; a failed/invalid build leaves no fresh artifact;
- a verified-live owner is never overlapped; crash + PID-reuse recovery;
- malformed-lock recovery (crash mid-claim) with a mid-claim grace window;
- single-winner reclaim: a held gate blocks a second reclaimer; a dead-holder gate is cleared immediately; an aged-out gate is recovered;
- gap-free lock install; overlapping builds never corrupt/partially publish (atomic-publish invariant);
- space- and quote-containing paths round-trip through the command.

**Evidence (VPS, bun canary):**
- `acp-provisioning.test.ts` + `eliza-code-acp-install.test.ts` + existing `acp-service.test.ts`: **82 passed**.
- Coverage on `acp-provisioning.ts`: **~85% lines / ~80% branch / 95% funcs** (>= 50% bar).
- `tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors. Biome: clean. `error-policy-ratchet` vs `origin/develop`: `emptyCatch 0->0`, no new fallback-slop.
- Codex review (`gpt-5.5`) against the commit: no findings after iterating through the P1/P2 races it surfaced (successor race, PID reuse, shared canonical build output, intent-timeout-vs-deadline, error-policy annotations) — all fixed in this branch.

Does not increase timeouts and does not reintroduce test dependence on a prebuilt `packages/examples/code/dist/acp.js`.

Closes #16169

93:T2408,Fixes #15901, #15902, #15903 — the three onboarding defects isolated on a physical Pixel 6a during the #14358 Android close-out (evidence + logcat timelines: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/14358#issuecomment-4930960082). They share the client handoff surface and are fixed coherently here. **Do not merge without review — reviewer sign-off requested.**

## Root causes and fixes

### #15901 — handoff hard-fails during the proxy-route 404 window
**Root cause.** Control-plane `running` precedes actual routability: the runtime proxy can 404 the dedicated subdomain for minutes after the record flips (structural window, see the CP-side findings annotated on #15901 — the router only routes once `web_ui_port` + mesh IP register, and recovery is reactive). The client treated `status==="running" && url` as ready and did a **one-shot** import; the thrown 404 landed `phase: failed` seconds into a 10-minute budget → "Setup paused / Retry" for an agent that was actually coming up.
**Fix.**
- `resolveReadyBase` (`packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts`) now probes the dedicated base itself (`GET /api/health`) and only reports ready once the proxy actually routes — 404/408/425/429/5xx or a network throw means "running but not yet routable", so the readiness poll keeps going inside its budget and the UI stays honestly in "Setting up…".
- `runConversationHandoff` (`packages/ui/src/cloud/handoff/conversation-handoff.ts`) retries transient step failures (`HandoffTransientError`: retryable HTTP statuses + network-layer throws, classified in `cloud-handoff-supervisor.ts`) by re-entering the readiness loop under **one shared deadline** — a step failure never re-arms a fresh budget, non-transient errors (401/403/…) still fail immediately, and an exhausted budget reports `timed-out`/`failed` honestly (user stays on the working shared adapter, Retry stays armed).
- CP-side "expose routable on the detail DTO" was investigated and deliberately not bundled (spans router daemon + CF Worker + SDK mirror); full findings + file map annotated on #15901.

### #15902 — stale `eliza:cloud-handoff-pending` marker pins "Setting up… / shared" forever
**Root cause.** The home provisioning tile rendered "Setting up…" for ANY shared-adapter session, forever, with nothing pending; a leftover marker from a previous (failed) onboarding also suppressed the fresh upgrade path in `bindCloudAgent`, and the marker was only reconciled on the boot-poll 404 path.
**Fix.**
- The widget (`agent-provisioning.tsx`) tracks a shared-adapter session only while a pending-handoff marker **for that agent** exists (the marker load TTL-clears expired entries); no marker + no live phase → render real state (nothing). A phase-event cache (`events/index.ts` + `useCloudHandoffPhase`) covers late mounts so an in-flight `migrating` is still visible.
- `bindCloudAgent` (`first-run-finish.ts`) clears a mismatched leftover marker, resumes a matching interrupted migration **at the landing** (`resumePendingCloudHandoff`, previously only reachable via the boot-poll 404 path), and never mints a second dedicated target for a reused agent that already owns one (`bridgeUrl` set).
- The dedicated-base boot landings in `startup-phase-poll.ts` also reconcile (a marker is provably stale once the active server is off the shared base).
- The Android spec's `resetKeys` now include `eliza:cloud-handoff-pending`.

### #15903 — `eliza:first-run-complete` never persisted on the returning-account landing
**Root cause.** Durable completion was persisted only through the conductor's React completion-callback chain; landing paths that bypass or outrun it (returning-account reuse, the pre-navigate pairing handoff that unloads the page) left the flag unset, so the next launch could re-enter first-run for a healthy agent.
**Fix.** Every successful cloud landing persists the flag **headlessly at the landing itself** in `bindCloudAgent` — fresh provision, returning-account reuse, in-process pairing, and before the `navigate` pairing hand-off — unified with (and idempotent to) the conductor's own `setFirstRunComplete(true)` persist.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds W1's `LanePlannerService` to `plugin-agent-orchestrator`:

- pure deterministic decomposition and overlap rejection
- structured `LaneSpec` values with scope, forbidden paths, open-PR/sibling collision annotations, routing-compatible difficulty tag, acceptance criteria seed, and initial prompt
- sibling scope unioning into every lane's forbidden paths
- optional defensive `TEXT_SMALL` refinement behind a separate setting
- one minted wave id stamped into ACP session and durable task metadata
- one existing `runCreateLegacy`/Smithers path per lane, launched concurrently
- an injected collision-provider adapter that reuses workspace-service plumbing without adding a GitHub client, task store, or runner
- exact legacy fallback for planner failures and unscoped single-task plans

## What kind of change is this?

Feature, non-breaking and default OFF.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is required for this independently landable W1. The setting is exported in code and defaults OFF while the broader #16443 orchestration stack is assembled.

# Risk

Medium. This replaces a machine-derived 392-row checked-in smoke corpus with a deterministic 20-row synthetic fixture and tightens training/release boundaries discovered while proving that fixture. The synthetic corpus exercises mechanics only and is explicitly excluded from model-quality and release evidence.

# What changed

- The builder serializes only the canonical formatter/privacy-filter result; raw source records cannot cross the output boundary.
- The tracked corpus is 20 source-controlled synthetic rows: 16 train, 2 validation, and 2 test, spanning direct, multi-turn, native-boundary, tool-call, and legacy formats.
- Manifest revision 3 records exact generator, formatter, privacy filter, source, split hashes, counts, and bytes. `--check` proves byte-for-byte regeneration and fails closed when Git provenance is unavailable.
- Privacy handling and emitted-byte tests cover credentials, contacts, numeric latitude/longitude, nested GeoJSON coordinates, case variants, and duplicate longitude aliases.
- `train_local.py` rejects malformed JSON, incompatible rows, invalid tool arguments, non-finite/negative controls, and partial preflight success. Its checkpoint scan covers actual shard patterns, nested tensors, and empty scans.
- Release evaluation no longer auto-selects the synthetic smoke corpus. Missing held-out data is publish-blocking, and results carry corpus path, hash, and record count.
- RL/Atropos paths share runtime state-root precedence, including `ELIZA_TRAJECTORY_DIR`; dead literal fields and swallowed curriculum persistence failures are removed.
- The training workflow runs on pull requests, keeps PR-controlled code off persistent self-hosted runners, and executes builder/privacy/validator/low-VRAM/Stage-2/scenario/evaluation suites.
- Tracked pytest output, mutable curriculum state, stale sidecars, and obsolete release-queue evidence are removed.

# Documentation

The training and corpus READMEs describe the synthetic-only contract, exact split counts, reproducibility, low-VRAM preflight limits, real Stage 2 requirement, state paths, and the release-evidence prohibition.

96:Te15,## Summary

- ships the localhost-only, bearer-gated account-pool lease broker because the prerequisite broker branch was not present on `develop`
- attributes each lease with an opaque id, timestamp, model, and SHA-256 session-key prefix while keeping raw session keys and credentials out of observability responses
- exposes active lease counts, last lease/report status, provider last-selection state, and a bounded ten-entry failover history through broker health and `/api/accounts`
- derives failovers only when a 401/403, 429/quota, or transient report is followed by a different-account lease for the same provider/session within 60 seconds
- preserves configured account pins, avoids stale parallel successes clearing newer failure state, and leaves UI labels/selection semantics unchanged

W3 of #16443. Adds a default-off `WaveSupervisor` to `plugin-agent-orchestrator` for wave-scoped lane lifecycle management.

- reads the canonical `metadata.waveId` contract, with defensive aliases for manually stamped tasks
- refills terminal lanes while the wave goal remains unmet through an injected `WaveRefillPlanner` interface
- ships a no-op planner fallback so W3 can land before W1; W1 can register `ORCHESTRATOR_LANE_PLANNER` with the same interface without changing W3
- detects failed-lane uncommitted work via `git status`, preserves lifecycle-registered workspaces, and includes the preserved path/files in replacement metadata and brief
- re-checks active lane scopes against sibling scopes and matching-repository open PR changed files, warning once per collision pair in the originating room
- layers a per-wave concurrency reservation/cap over the existing durable admission queue, including head-of-line avoidance across waves
- persists compact wave status in existing task metadata and surfaces it through `ACTIVE_SUB_AGENTS`
- gates all behavior behind `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_WAVE_SUPERVISOR` (default OFF)

## Redundancy check and design choice

I read the existing watchdog, reflexion/respawn path, task service/store/types, admission queue, Smithers integration, active-sub-agent provider, workspace lifecycle/registry, and swarm coordinator before implementation.

This is a **new service rather than an extension of `TaskWatchdogService`**. The watchdog is session-liveness detection and reminder delivery; wave supervision owns a different aggregate lifecycle plus admission control and planner orchestration. Combining them would couple default-on watchdog behavior to a default-off feature and expand the watchdog's responsibilities substantially. W3 does reuse its proven structure: pure detectors, one tick owner, in-memory one-time warning keys, target resolution through the task service, no new store, and timer-free policy tests.

Existing mechanisms reused:

- task durability and metadata: `OrchestratorTaskService` / existing store
- dispatch backpressure: existing admission queue and its durable admission record
- salvage ownership: workspace lifecycle plus shared registry, without adopting caller-owned paths
- status visibility: existing `ACTIVE_SUB_AGENTS` provider
- retry context remains owned by existing reflexion/respawn logic

## Configuration

- `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_WAVE_SUPERVISOR=true` enables the service
- `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_WAVE_CONCURRENCY=<n>` sets the default per-wave cap (default 4)
- `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_WAVE_INTERVAL_MS=<ms>` sets tick cadence (minimum 5000, default 60000)
- task metadata `waveConcurrencyCap` overrides the default for that wave

# Risk

**Medium.** This changes protective counters on every Cloud API/chat request and hardens a shared Redis socket client. Missing or throwing production rate-limit bindings fail visibly with 503. Cloudflare counters are deliberately used only for the global flood backstop and chat API-key gate; exact organization/purchased-quota accounting remains Redis-backed and authoritative.

# Background

## Root cause

Paired staging/direct evidence showed Cerebras responding in roughly 89–333 ms while the Cloud gateway could spend 2.45–3.11 seconds before the handler after idle. Privacy-safe unauthenticated probes reproduced two sequential Railway TCP Redis checks at 2.38–3.46 seconds without calling a model.

The two outer protective limiters were paying cross-cloud TCP setup before every chat turn. Independent review also found that a timed-out SocketRedis operation could resume after its 50 ms teardown bound and touch a successor connection's mutable reader/parser.

## What this PR changes

- moves the global 600/min flood backstop and chat 200/min gate to Cloudflare Rate Limiting bindings;
- preserves the exact organization inference limiter on Redis;
- hashes API-key/anonymous identifiers before using them as counter keys;
- batches carried Redis lease increments and TTL lookup into one pipeline exchange;
- makes SocketRedis reader/writer/parser state connection-local and protects publication with an ownership epoch;
- bounds teardown while ensuring timeout losers cannot read from, publish over, or close successor connections;
- starts independent input/output pricing reads concurrently and evicts the short-lived balance hint on central credit mutations;
- preserves Cerebras `promptCacheKey` when `reasoningEffort` also contributes provider options, with streaming and non-streaming regressions;
- preserves the inner 200/min chat limiter's headers while Hono unwinds through the outer 600/min limiter, for both allowed and denied responses; and
- repairs/restores process-global Redis mocks used by the complete Cloud lane.

It does **not** change provider/model selection, billing rates, response streaming bytes, database schema, or production deployment state. Native rate-limit metadata is intentionally more truthful than before.

## Reviewer entry points

1. `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/middleware/rate-limit-hono-cloudflare.ts`
2. `packages/cloud/api/src/bootstrap-app.ts`
3. `packages/cloud/api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts`
4. `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/cache/socket-redis.ts`
5. `packages/cloud/api/wrangler.toml`

# Known limits and follow-ups

- Cold startup is not fixed: two of five cold gateway cases remained about 5.2 seconds, with roughly 3.2 seconds in auth. The monolithic 16 MiB/646-route Worker bootstrap is #16155; cold auth reconnect investigation is #16159.

99:Td0d,## Summary

W2 of #16443 adds deterministic remote-reality verification to the existing completion-validation pipeline in `plugin-agent-orchestrator`.

- verifies the claimed pull request exists and records open/closed/merged state
- fetches head-SHA check runs plus commit statuses, classifies required checks as green/pending/red, and synthesizes pending entries for required checks not yet reported
- compares the captured `WorkspaceChangeSet.changedFiles` with the PR's actual changed-file list in both directions
- appends a `GROUND TRUTH` section to the existing completion-evidence string without replacing its local evidence
- persists the structured verdict at `task.metadata.groundTruthVerdict` for W4
- supports evidence default ON, hard-fail default OFF, and explicit PR-required policy
- treats GitHub/API failures as inconclusive, never as false failures

## Settings

- `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_GROUND_TRUTH_EVIDENCE`: default ON; set `false`/`0` to disable the evidence section and verifier metadata write unless hard-fail is enabled
- `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_GROUND_TRUTH_HARD_FAIL`: default OFF; when enabled, a claimed-but-missing PR or red required check fails before any model call
- `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_GROUND_TRUTH_REQUIRE_PR`: default OFF; task metadata may also set `requirePullRequest: true` or `groundTruthPolicy.requirePullRequest: true`

## Architecture

Pure file comparison and check-rollup classification live in `ground-truth-verifier.ts`; remote calls are injected. GitHub IO stays in `workspace-github.ts` and reuses the authenticated `GitHubPatClient`/Octokit transport already owned by `CodingWorkspaceService`. PR extraction reuses `pull-request-link.ts`; claimed files reuse the captured workspace change set.

Closes #16457

## What changed

- `packages/shared/src/contracts/inbox-routes.ts`: strict schema now accepts the smallest typed top-level `accountId` selector; all other connector routing fields (channel, connectorSendAs, metadata) remain rejected at the contract edge. Contract tests extended.

Forged, disabled, cross-provider, ambiguous, owner-mismatched, and missing-required-account selectors all fail observably with typed errors and invoke NO connector. `Content.metadata` is never routing authority. Default/single-account behavior is explicit and deterministic.

9b:T1def,Relates to #16278

## Summary

- remove the startup-failing `release-all.yml` aggregate without changing `release.yaml`, the merged immutable release primitives, or the transactional npm work in #16277
- remove its unused `flatpak-publish.yml` child and the credential playbook for a Flathub repository that does not exist
- preserve the standalone OS manifest recovery operation by replacing its fail-open reusable/direct-push design with a manual-only, exact-SHA-bound, strict-checksum, pull-request boundary
- make the workflow catalog and OS secret map describe the real call graph, including distinct Snap credentials and the retained Snap/APT skip-as-success defect owned by #16279
- replace name/literal-only assertions with a real-tree YAML contract covering all local reusable calls, automatic versus manual manifest writers, recovery permissions/triggers/commands, the known automatic OS permission rejection, and actual orchestrated secret identities

## Root cause and correction

`release-all.yml` combined a second release coordinator, OS artifact publication, store dispatches, and an npm event-chain assumption in one entry point. Its entire recorded history is 13 `startup_failure` runs with zero jobs. Its `GITHUB_TOKEN`-created GitHub release also could not trigger `release.yaml`, because repository-token events do not recursively start another workflow except explicit dispatch events.

The first version of this PR incorrectly treated `update-os-release-manifest.yml` as behaviorally redundant. Exact-head review showed that it uniquely accepted a manifest/tag for already-published assets while the retained full OS workflow has only startup failures. This revision preserves that recovery behavior but removes its failure masking and direct protected-branch push: the operator must pin the current `origin/develop` SHA and release-tag commit SHA; every release asset is downloaded and checksummed; strict validation and checksum verification must pass; and the only mutation is a dedicated branch plus reviewable PR.

```text
update-os-release-manifest.yml
  trigger: workflow_dispatch only
  required identity: manifest path + release tag + current develop SHA + tag commit SHA
  data: exact GitHub release asset set
  checks: canonical checksum regeneration + strict manifest validation + checksum verification
  mutation: unique recovery branch + pull request to develop
  forbidden: workflow_call, release/push trigger, direct develop/main push
```

The automatic/manual writer inventory is exactly:

```text
automatic: elizaos-os-full-release.yml
manual recovery: update-os-release-manifest.yml
```

This is an authority inventory, not a health claim. The test detects that the automatic OS caller's read-only ceiling cannot satisfy the retained reusable children’s `contents`, `id-token`, `attestations`, and `pages` write requests. The catalog therefore says explicitly that the automatic path is startup-invalid and routes its repair to #16279.

### Secret/call graph correction

`release-orchestrator.yml` calls `publish-packages.yml` with PyPI, Snap, APT, and Flatpak enabled. That child reads `PYPI_API_TOKEN`, `SNAP_STORE_CREDENTIALS`, and `APT_REPO_TOKEN`. The separate `snap-publish.yml` reads `SNAPCRAFT_STORE_CREDENTIALS`. The checklist now records both paths and warns that missing orchestrated Snap/APT credentials can still skip the store mutation while the containing build succeeds; #16279 owns that completion repair.

9c:Tfc9,## Summary

Makes consumer metering a first-class part of the account-pool broker introduced by #16355.

- managed consumer API keys: create, label/update, enable/disable, rotate, one-time plaintext return, SHA-256 digest at rest
- opt-in `ELIZA_ACCOUNT_POOL_PUBLIC_CONSUMER_AUTH=true`; legacy localhost/direct callers remain keyless when disabled
- Anthropic-shaped 401/429 responses, caller `x-api-key` and bearer credentials stripped before upstream integration
- exact non-stream usage extraction and byte-identical SSE observation across arbitrary chunk boundaries
- durable daily JSONL request records plus atomic rolling totals
- cross-process filesystem locking and durable quota reservations
- per-consumer totals and time-window/model/day breakdown API for a future command-center consumers panel
- no OAuth refresh behavior added; the broker remains the sole refresh owner

## API / integration

Contract is documented in `packages/app-core/src/services/account-pool-consumer-metering.md`.

Admin routes, protected by the existing loopback + broker bearer gate:

- `GET|POST /internal/account-pool/v1/consumer-keys`
- `PATCH /internal/account-pool/v1/consumer-keys/:id`
- `POST /internal/account-pool/v1/consumer-keys/:id/rotate`
- `GET /internal/account-pool/v1/consumer-usage?consumerId=&startMs=&endMs=`

The protocol proxy calls the exported auth/admission helper before leasing, uses the returned stripped headers, tees streaming bytes through the observer transform, and records status/latency/usage after completion. The transform enqueues the exact input byte chunks and does not buffer or replay transport bytes.

## Compatibility / rollout

Public consumer auth is explicit opt-in. With the flag unset, the current localhost/legacy keyless behavior remains active.

The interim `pool-meter.js` service on `:18811` should keep `pool.shad0w.xyz` alive until this lands and the account-pool proxy integration deploys. Existing totals can be migrated by importing its JSONL records into the new normalized JSONL schema and rebuilding rolling totals. Do not cut the edge over before deployment verification.

No type errors referenced the changed metering or broker-route files in the captured output.

9d:Tf4f,Part of #16420

## What changed

- keeps the develop-published GitHub Pages AASA fallback deliberately inert
- stores the reviewed production association document outside the Pages public tree
- serves only the exact `https://eliza.app/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` path from a Cloudflare Worker
- binds the document to `25877RY2EH.ai.elizaos.app`, preserves every reviewed legacy link, and adds only `/auth/callback`
- adds only `applinks:eliza.app` to the App target; shared `webcredentials` trust is intentionally excluded because the launch flow does not implement that feature
- validates the exact App/Release target, bundle ID, Team ID, entitlements file, manifest shape, legacy route order, response bytes, headers, redirect behavior, body limits, timeouts, aborts, and partial transport failures
- publishes only from protected `main` through the non-self-approved `production` environment; a bad origin deploy rolls back (or removes a first broken Worker), while Apple CDN convergence is observed separately and never rolls back a healthy origin

## Root cause

The existing production trust document lived under `packages/homepage/public`, which is published directly from `develop`. Updating it with the release Team ID would bypass the protected production approval path. The earlier verifier also accepted extra app identities and did not preserve the exact legacy route set.

## User and platform impact

This is an iOS association prerequisite only. Android files and `assetlinks.json` are byte-unchanged. The GitHub Pages homepage and its public fallback AASA bytes are unchanged. Browser, desktop, and Android runtime behavior is not modified.

9e:Tfb0,The multi-account operator surface, requested for Discord/GUI/Telegram parity with the Settings account UI. Built in the plugin-commands catalog (reaches every connector through the existing bridges) following the `/model` loopback-route precedent.

## `/accounts` — manage the coding-account pool
- **bare `/accounts`** — per-provider report: strategy + each account's label, email, enabled/disabled, priority, session%/weekly% usage, reset countdown, health. Only providers that have linked accounts.
- **`/accounts use <provider> <account>`** — make an account active: enables it and disables its enabled siblings within that provider (scoped — no cross-provider bleed), then re-reads and lists the resulting states.
- **`/accounts enable|disable <provider> <account>`** — single toggle.
- **`/accounts strategy <provider> <priority|round-robin|least-used|quota-aware>`** — set the rotation strategy.
- **`/accounts refresh <provider> [account]`** — force a usage re-probe, then show fresh numbers.
- Provider wire ids **and** aliases (`claude`→anthropic-subscription, `codex`→openai-codex, `cerebras`→cerebras-api). Account matching by exact id, ≥6-char id prefix, exact label, or exact email — case-insensitive; **ambiguous or no match refuses with the candidate list, never guesses.**

## `/backend` — default coding backend
- **bare `/backend`** — the effective `ELIZA_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE` + the available backends.
- **`/backend codex|claude|opencode|eliza-code`** — persists through a new additive `defaultBackend`-only shape of `POST /api/models/config` (dual seam config.env + config.env.vars + process.env, restart-free, `eliza-code`→`elizaos` wire spelling). **Never `runtime.setSetting`** — that's in-memory only and reverts on restart.

## Auth
Both `requiresAuth` (reads authorized-only) + in-handler `isElevated` re-check on every write — matching `/model`. Adversarial review caught an earlier `requiresElevated`-at-definition version that made the connector bridge refuse the bare read before the handler ran; fixed and pinned with a gate-layer test.

```
  Claude Max (bot account 3) (…) — enabled, prio 1, session 0% / weekly 0%, ok
**openai-codex** (strategy: round-robin)
  ChatGPT Codex (bot account 2) (…) — enabled, prio 1, session 9% / weekly 36%, resets in 2h 37m, ok
  elizamakesmagic@… — enabled, prio 2, session 100% / weekly 21%, resets 8h 5m ago, needs-reauth
```

```

/backend                             → "Default coding backend: elizaos (config.env) … Switch with /backend <backend>."
/backend claude                      → persisted ELIZA_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE=claude (config.env, restart-free)
```
(The service.env `ELIZA_DEFAULT_AGENT_TYPE` pin that would have silently reverted writes was migrated to config.env in the same rollout, so /backend is authoritative.)
</details>

# Risks

Medium. This changes response telemetry headers and Cloudflare observability sampling on the public Cloud API, and touches the provider-dispatch boundary for chat/messages. Provider payloads, billing decisions, response bodies, and streaming bytes are unchanged. Regression coverage explicitly protects zero-buffer streaming, cancellation, error/refund paths, CORS credentials, and route-specific payment headers.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Resolves one safe application trace id from `X-Eliza-Trace-Id`, strict W3C v00 `traceparent`, or a generated UUID and records it in Worker request telemetry.
- Emits browser-readable `Server-Timing` plus `X-Eliza-Preforward-Ms` for monotonic `auth`, `middle`, `reserve`, `setup`, and `total` gateway phases.
- Freezes that timing at the actual `fetch` / `streamText` / `generateText` invocation after request and provider configuration is built, then logs and returns the same two-decimal snapshot.
- Preserves timing on provider failures, immutable streaming responses, and `/v1/responses` success/remapped-5xx compatibility responses without buffering the body.
- Merges telemetry CORS exposure after route handlers so existing x402/payment exposure headers are not overwritten.
- Finalizes in-memory request telemetry when a handler throws and records the trace id on both success and error paths.
- Explicitly enables complete Worker logs and 5% native trace sampling in Wrangler.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and observability improvements (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is required for this correctness slice. The full deployment topology and remaining end-to-end telemetry work are documented in #16079 and #16085.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/observability/http-telemetry.ts`, then the provider-dispatch observers in `/v1/chat/completions` and `/v1/messages`, followed by the gated-stream and real-bootstrap regressions.

It does not yet add browser marks, dedicated-agent/proxy propagation, provider request-id correlation, first-frame/reasoning/content/completion timings, Analytics Engine persistence, or the production benchmark harness.

a3:Te0a,## Summary

Adds a burn-efficiency account-pool strategy that spends paid Anthropic capacity before each account's weekly allowance rolls over unused.

The strategy is now `drain-soonest-reset`, and is the default for `anthropic-subscription` pools. This replaces the earlier subscription-expiry-centered design after the hawktom100 incident, where an account sat at 0% weekly usage all week and then rolled into a fresh window with its paid allowance wasted.

## Design

- Primary order: relevant weekly usage window `resetsAt`, soonest first.
- Tiebreak: most remaining headroom, lowest weekly utilization first.
- Final-days booster: when reset and utilization tie, prefer the account whose subscription ends soonest within 48 hours.
- Explicit per-account priority remains a hard operator override. Generated creation-order priority does not defeat drain ordering.
- The legacy `priority` strategy remains available as a manual pool strategy.
- Anthropic's all-model `seven_day` bucket is persisted as blended `weeklyPct` / `resetsAt`.
- Anthropic `limits` entries marked `weekly_scoped` / `weekly` are persisted by model display name. A requested model such as `claude-fable-5` uses the `Fable` bucket. Unknown models fall back to blended weekly usage.
- Session exhaustion and 429 cooldown temporarily exclude an account without changing its weekly drain rank. Weekly reset timestamps are deliberately not reused as hourly/session cooldown timestamps.
- Subscription `subscriptionEndsAt` persistence and the terminal `expired` health state from the original PR remain.

## Why

Subscription end date is not the day-to-day binding resource. The scarce resource is each account's asynchronous weekly allowance, including model-scoped weekly limits. Letting a window close with unused allowance destroys paid capacity. The selector therefore burns the soonest-refunding budget first while still respecting current session caps and hand-tuned operator priority.

a8:T198d,# Relates to

Config-collapse: today a deployment juggles per-lane model vars (`OPENAI_*`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_*`, `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_*`, `GOOGLE_*`, `CEREBRAS_*`, bare aliases…). The operator's mental model is two knobs: a small model and a large model — code should decide which tier uses which.

# Risks

Low-medium. The pair slots **below** every lane-specific var and **above** hardcoded defaults, so pair-unset is behavior-identical (pinned by tests per lane + boot-seeding). Family gating prevents cross-provider id poisoning. The diff was adversarially reviewed by a 29-agent workflow (4 lenses × refutation); all 6 confirmed defect classes are fixed in this revision (details below).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds `ELIZA_MODEL_SMALL` / `ELIZA_MODEL_LARGE` (env or runtime setting) with one core resolver, `readCanonicalModel(runtime, tier, family)`:

- **Precedence per lane**: lane-specific var (escape hatch) → canonical pair → bare alias → lane default. Per-family tier derivations (anthropic's opus planner, RH small-vs-nano forks) are untouched — only the base getters gained a leg.
- **Family qualification**: values may be `provider/model` (`anthropic/claude-opus-4-8`) — honored only by the matching family; unqualified values serve every lane (the single-provider case). Native slash ids (`meta-llama/…`, groq's `openai/gpt-oss-120b`) are recognized as ids, and can be family-pinned (`groq/openai/gpt-oss-120b`).
- **Lanes wired**: openai (incl. the Cerebras/EvoLink overlays — the pair outranks each overlay's baked default, not its explicit keys), anthropic, ollama, google-genai, elizacloud, cli-inference (claude-sdk: pair drives large=replies, small=planner+triage; codex-sdk).
- **Host boot**: `applyProviderModelEnvDefaults` derives GOOGLE_/GROQ_/CEREBRAS_ seeds from the pair (qualified values verbatim; unqualified pass the OpenAI-id-shape guard); `applyCloudConfigToEnv` gains pair legs reading config.env + process.env; `GETSETTING_ENV_ALLOWLIST` exposes both names.
- **Display**: `resolveProviderModelString` + the runtime-model-context provider report the pair only after every lane key and only unqualified (never report a model a family gate rejected).
- **Excluded by design**: embeddings (dimension pinning), coding `*_POWERFUL/FAST` (disjoint id namespace), effort vars (v2).

## Adversarial review (29 agents, 4 lenses, 25 raw → 16 confirmed → all fixed here)

- Cerebras LARGE seed missing its canonical leg (pair silently dead, TEXT_LARGE ran the small model) → leg added, pinned by test
- Unqualified openai-shaped pair values bypassed the groq/cerebras poisoning guard → `guardedCanonicalSeed` (qualified=verbatim, unqualified=guarded)
- EvoLink overlay's hardcoded default outranked the pair → rewired like Cerebras
- Display resolvers misreported (inversion + no family gate + trailing-slash) → canonical checked after the full lane-key walk, unqualified-only
- `applyCloudConfigToEnv` legs missed config.env → reads through `readEffectiveEnvValue`
- Slash-bearing native ids misparsed as qualifications → known-family-token rule
- Known scoped limitation (follow-up issue): per-agent (character-settings) pair values can be masked by boot-seeded env for the seeded lanes (google/groq/cerebras) — the hosted-agent bake migration owns that.

## What kind of change is this?

Features.

# Documentation changes needed?

Env-var reference gains the two names (self-documented at the resolver + allowlist comments).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)a9:T1a29,## Root cause

When mobile voice explicitly forces Eliza Cloud TTS (`provider === "eliza-cloud"`), the renderer still POSTed to the on-device agent's `/api/tts/cloud` proxy. On a dedicated/on-device (hybrid) runtime that proxy runs on the constrained device itself, so every reply pays an extra audio download through the phone-side Bun event loop **plus a base64 IPC re-marshal** — measured at ~5–6s/reply even though the cloud worker completes in 1–2s. The worker's provider-agnostic `POST /api/v1/voice/tts` route could serve the same `{ text }` → audio bytes directly.

Before this change, `useVoiceChat.speakElizaCloud` only bypassed the proxy for **shared-runtime** agents (`#15395`); a dedicated/on-device agent always went `resolveApiUrl("/api/tts/cloud")` → local proxy → cloud.

## The fix

`speakElizaCloud` now resolves its target through a **pure** `resolveForcedCloudTtsRoute(...)` helper (`packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.ts`), which picks one of three targets:

1. **shared-runtime** → the worker's `/api/v1/voice/tts` off the active base (unchanged, `#15395`).
2. **direct-cloud** (`#16116`, new) → when a **cloud session bearer** (`getCloudAuthToken()` — the canonical Steward JWT) **and** a **configured cloud origin** (`configuredCloudVoiceOrigin()`, derived from boot config `cloudApiBase`) are both present, POST straight to `<cloudOrigin>/api/v1/voice/tts` with `Authorization: Bearer <cloud session token>`, skipping the on-device proxy entirely.
3. **on-device-proxy** → when cloud auth/config is unavailable, the existing `/api/tts/cloud` path is preserved unchanged.

The cloud origin is read from boot config (**not** a hardcoded production URL), so staging/custom cloud environments route correctly. Only the URL + bearer change — the `{ text }` body and audio-bytes response are identical.

### Acceptance criteria
- [x] Forced-cloud TTS uses the cloud session bearer token and configured cloud API origin.
- [x] Production is not hardcoded over staging/custom boot config (origin derived from `cloudApiBase`).
- [x] Existing non-forced/proxy behavior remains unchanged (fallback to `/api/tts/cloud` when no cloud auth/config; other providers untouched).
- [x] Focused headless regression proves routing and fallback.

## Files
- `packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.ts` — `configuredCloudVoiceOrigin()`, `resolveForcedCloudTtsRoute()`, `ForcedCloudTtsRoute`.
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts` — `speakElizaCloud` routes via the resolver; direct path carries the cloud bearer.
- `packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.test.ts` — unit coverage (origin helper + resolver, all branches).
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.forced-cloud-tts.test.tsx` — hook-level regression driving the real hook/processQueue.

## How to verify
On a device running a local/on-device Eliza agent, connect Eliza Cloud (so a Steward session token exists) and set voice provider to `eliza-cloud`. Speak. The forced-cloud TTS request now hits `<cloudApiBase>/api/v1/voice/tts` directly with the cloud bearer — it no longer appears against the on-device `/api/tts/cloud` proxy (`ELIZA_TTS_DEBUG` logs `useVoiceChat:eliza-cloud-direct-worker`). Log out of Cloud (no session token) and the request falls back to `/api/tts/cloud`, unchanged.

# Risks

Medium. This deliberately makes the live gate stricter. A reported runtime or
service error, failed action, structured failure reply, errored/active native
trajectory, or missing required native artifact now fails the benchmark after
the review bundle is persisted.

Designed confirmation and clarification results remain valid terminal states.

error-free confirmation or carries a recognized missing/ambiguous-input code;
a structured service error still fails even if it also asks for confirmation.

meeting-corpus, and native screen-time cases instead of fabricating empty data
or mutating an external owner account.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Gives each selected case a typed terminal outcome: `completed`, explicit
  `capability_unavailable`, or typed `failed`.
- Captures runtime reports, failed action events, structured failure replies,
  native tool failures, trajectory aborts, and incomplete trajectories, then
  validates them fail-closed.
- Registers the production-owned knowledge graph in the real LifeOps PGLite
  test host and verifies that collaborator directly.
- Defines and tests explicit host/mobile action capability profiles.
- Uses the real, non-mutating `schedule_plan` path (`list_active`) for the hosted
  capability case instead of dispatching to an unavailable counterparty.
- Persists `report.json`, `report.md`, `cases.jsonl`, every native JSON/Markdown
  trajectory, and a SHA-256 manifest before the terminal assertion, including
  red runs.
- Uploads that bundle on every credentialed live workflow dispatch and runs the
  PR workflow against `develop`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and benchmark-evidence hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user documentation change is needed. The workflow, typed rows, report, and
manifest are the review contract for this test-only infrastructure.

- expose `WEB_FETCH` and `WEB_SEARCH` to the headless `eliza-code` coding-agent runtime
- reuse core DNS-pinned SSRF guard with HTTPS-only redirects, bounded redirects/time/body, binary rejection, and HTML-to-readable-text extraction
- normalize/cap keyless Parallel + Exa MCP search results and advertise the tools to the planner
- add focused safety and behavior tests

## Audit context
The chat-agent actions added in #9280 and fixed in #16378 are registered by `packages/agent`, but the coding-only ACP runtime loads `plugin-coding-tools` directly, so they were not reachable in its tool loop. The existing chat actions already had DNS-pinned SSRF and redirect revalidation, but returned raw HTML, accepted broad content types, used character rather than byte caps, and exposed provider text without stable coding-tool metadata. This PR keeps the same providers/architecture while hardening the coding-agent surface.

## Root causes

- Clean runners installed with `--ignore-scripts` but built a hand-maintained subset of the scenario runner's transitive workspace graph, exposing missing dist artifacts one package at a time.
- `wake-up-routine` was classified as a `simple` wellness reply at Stage 1, so explicit first-person sleep/recovery telemetry never reached `OWNER_HEALTH`.
- `app-list` selected `APP action=list`, but the live harness had no real agent API host behind the production loopback client. The tool returned `fetch failed`; retries then consumed the 120-second turn.

## Fix

- Build the complete Turbo `@elizaos/scenario-runner...` dependency closure, run all live roots with workspace source conditions, and remove build-time model secrets.
- Declare the agent server's dynamically imported `@elizaos/plugin-app-manager` dependency so Turbo includes it.
- Add a health-owned Stage-1 evaluator that routes explicit owner telemetry reads to `OWNER_HEALTH`, while excluding clinical-advice requests and staying inactive when the host action is absent.
- Start the real production agent API server on an ephemeral port in the app-list live scenario. No fetch shim or timeout increase. Assert successful structured installed/running arrays.
- Strengthen both scenarios with successful-action and selected-argument checks.

- `node packages/scripts/run-turbo.mjs run build --filter=@elizaos/scenario-runner... --concurrency=8` — 103/103 tasks passed across 108 packages, including app-manager.
- plugin-health: 180 passed, 4 skipped; typecheck passed.
- plugin-app-control: 1,488 passed.
- Workflow contract: 3 passed.
- Production-path host probe: real `startApiServer({ port: 0, runtime })` + real `AppControlClient` returned valid `installedCount: 0`, `runningCount: 0`.
- Root verify passed all ratchets and touched-package lanes; the unrelated cloud-api `wrangler deploy --dry-run` process was stopped after hanging for more than six minutes.
- Exact-head credentialed enforcing workflow: pending.

# Risks

Medium: this introduces the future stable merge-gate context. The aggregate is
base-trusted, read-only, hosted, bounded to 45 minutes, and never checks out PR
code. It binds every observed check to the GitHub Actions app, exact owning
workflow path, `pull_request` event, and current PR head SHA.

This PR does **not** mutate ruleset `18901247`. The existing nine no-bypass
required contexts remain authoritative throughout bootstrap and live canaries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one always-emitted `Develop PR Gate` check for PRs targeting `develop`.
It polls the nine existing lightweight required contexts and passes only when
all nine have a current, owner-bound terminal `success`. Missing, stale,
pending past the poll budget, cancelled, timed out, failed, neutral, action
required, and skipped results fail closed. Owner workflows may classify
non-applicable work inside an always-emitted successful job; disappearing
behind a path filter is forbidden by the static workflow contract.

The workflow uses `pull_request_target` only as a trusted orchestration
boundary: it checks out `pull_request.base.sha`, has read-only permissions, has
no secrets, and observes check metadata for `pull_request.head.sha`. The new
self-test is also wired into the already-required `Test Integrity (lane
coverage)` job so this bootstrap PR validates the proposed implementation even
though a newly-added base-trusted workflow cannot execute until it exists on
the base branch.

The workflow README now distinguishes the lightweight pre-merge surface from
the exhaustive post-merge suites and the push-only `ci-ok` roll-up.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / CI enforcement improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `.github/workflows/README.md` with the current pre-merge and post-merge
ownership model.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.self-test.mjs` — deterministic state
   transitions and trust-boundary tamper cases.
2. `.github/workflows/develop-pr-gate.yml` — stable hosted orchestration
   boundary.
3. `packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate-workflow-contract.mjs` — exact owner,
   trigger, path-filter, permissions, and checkout contracts.

## Detailed testing steps

```text
node packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.self-test.mjs
  develop-pr aggregate self-test passed

node packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate-workflow-contract.mjs
  develop-pr aggregate workflow contract passed (9 contexts)

actionlint .github/workflows/develop-pr-gate.yml .github/workflows/develop-pr.yml
  exit 0

  packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate-workflow-contract.mjs \

  {"ok":true,"failures":[]}

  exit 0

  exit 0; 8859 packages installed

  exit 0; 573/573 uncached Turbo tasks and 28/28 dist-path consumers passed

bun test packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.test.mjs --coverage
  1 pass; contract 97.54%, implementation 60.85%; enforced floor 50%
```

Deterministic terminal-state receipt at this exact head:

| Scenario | Expected aggregate | Observed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| all nine success | success | success / exit 0 |
| owner-bound context missing at deadline | failure | `missing` |
| context remains in progress at deadline | failure | `pending-timeout` |
| cancelled | failure | `terminal-cancelled` |
| timed out | failure | `terminal-timed_out` |
| failed | failure | `terminal-failure` |
| skipped/path-disappeared | failure | `skipped-forbidden` |
| wrong workflow path or wrong app | wait, then fail missing | observed |
| stale result after an event that reruns its owner | wait, then fail stale | observed |
| newer successful rerun supersedes an old failure | success | observed |

Real GitHub baseline canaries for the same nine source contexts are preserved
in #13306: [green #16321](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16321/checks)
reached 9/9 success, including the
[zero-file coverage job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29298277609/job/86976439814);
[red #16322](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16322) was blocked by the
[failed title job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29298342833/job/86976635515).

## Post-merge aggregate canaries and migration (required before ruleset mutation)

1. Dispatch `develop-pr-gate.yml` on `develop` once for each synthetic
   `success`, `missing`, `cancelled`, `timed-out`, `failed`, `skipped`, and
   `pending-timeout` scenario. Only `success` may conclude success.
2. Open aggregate-specific green and deliberately red PR canaries. Confirm the
   aggregate observes real owner checks and that the red source lane makes the
   aggregate red.
3. Add `Develop PR Gate` to ruleset `18901247` **alongside** the existing nine
   contexts. Confirm a PR is blocked while it is missing/pending and becomes
   mergeable only after both old and new gates pass.
4. Only after those receipts, replace the nine individual required contexts
   with the aggregate. At no point is the rule open or bypassable.

Rollback is the reverse, fail-closed sequence: restore the captured nine
individual contexts alongside the aggregate first, verify effective-rule
readback and a green PR, then remove the aggregate context. If the aggregate
is unhealthy, leave the old nine in force. Never delete/disable the ruleset;
its `bypass_actors: []`, PR, deletion, and non-fast-forward rules remain
unchanged.

Notification hydration now fails closed and recovers observably. A terminal inbox failure renders a distinct error card with Retry, background recovery reports through `runtime.reportError`, and a successful retry restores the persisted notification state.

# What changed

- Extends the notification lifecycle runtime contract with `reportError` and reports every J7 recovery failure with scope, attempt, and retry delay.
- Exposes an explicit hydration retry operation used by lifecycle callbacks and the UI.
- Renders loading, error, empty, and recovered inbox states distinctly; the error card does not leak raw backend details.
- Adds component and lifecycle coverage for terminal failure, owner-visible reporting, retry, and recovery.

# Background

Adds dual-contract Omi BLE reassembly for firmware that increments the notification sequence on each chunk and legacy firmware that keeps one frame id across chunks. It adds typed diagnostics, loss/cadence metrics, conservative ambiguous-tail handling, and adapts the pendant connection without changing transport selection, ASR, transcript UI, auth, billing, or provider wiring.

No default-on voice behavior changes. No physical-device completion claim is made.

Correctness fix with deterministic coverage.

b0:T10af,## What

Corrective follow-up to the prematurely merged #16293. This closes #16300 by making the Codex one-time refresh-token path fail closed from selection through the periodic account sweep.

- Codex selection reconciles every materialized token generation under the account refresh mutex before any canonical token resolution. Adoption failure propagates; neither `select()` nor the keep-alive sweep can fall through to `getAccessToken` with the consumed token.
- A published `auth.json` is thereafter Codex-owned. The bridge creates a refresh-token-addressed generation with atomic no-replace publication and never overwrites an existing generation, so an external Codex truncate/write cannot lose the only valid rotated pair in an adoption/write gap.
- Stable stat/read/stat retries cover the real truncate-then-write behavior of the external Codex process. Divergent generations require a real parseable `last_refresh`; ambiguous order fails instead of guessing.
- Initial materialization uses the canonical record's observable `updatedAt`, not materialization time, so it cannot invent a fresher OAuth generation.
- `config.toml` and the non-secret `active-home` pointer are atomically replaced and verified. Failure to pin `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` aborts the spawn.
- The benchmark adapter follows the validated active generation while preserving the pre-generation account-root layout.

## Correctness boundary

The in-process account mutex serializes reconciliation, canonical persistence, config publication, and selection. An external Codex process cannot honor that mutex, so the bridge removes the conflicting write entirely: after the initial no-replace publish, only Codex writes that generation. A later OAuth login gets a different generation path; reconciliation scans both without clobbering either.

b2:Tc96,## What & why

Follow-up to #16423. The LifeOps activity-signal capture (Capacitor mobile presence/health + desktop power/screen-time) was mounted as a React effect from `packages/app/src/main.tsx` — a 3881-line bootstrap that can't be unit-tested. This moves the capture into `@elizaos/plugin-personal-assistant` so the plugin owns its own lifecycle.

- **Imperative controller** — `src/lifeops/activity-signals-capture.ts` exports `startLifeOpsActivitySignalCapture()`: installs the browser lifecycle listeners / Capacitor mobile-signal / desktop-power pollers and returns a stop function.
- **Self-start at boot** — `src/register.ts` is a side-effect entry the renderer's `plugin-registrations` loader runs after first paint (opted in via `elizaos.appRegister: "register"`), so the capture runs for the app lifetime with **no app-shell mount**.
- **Removes the app-shell wiring** — the `main.tsx` mount + its `.d.ts` declarations, the dead `useLifeOpsActivitySignals` hook (−484), and the `LifeOpsActivitySignalsEffect` from `ui.ts`.
- **Now testable** — 17 real jsdom tests: `activity-signals-capture` **90.56%**, `register` **100%**, `ui` **100%**.

## ⚠️ Coverage + evidence gates need an admin-merge (same as #16423)

Removing the wiring is a `main.tsx` edit, so `main.tsx` re-enters two gates it structurally can't satisfy:
- **coverage-on-changed** — `main.tsx` shows MISSING (the 3881-line bootstrap has no unit test; it's only read as a string by structural tests). The other 3 changed source files are ≥50%.
- **check-pr-evidence (ocr-review)** — any `.tsx` change in `packages/app` demands OCR media, but this change has **no visual delta** (it removes an invisible headless-effect mount).

Both are the same un-testable-bootstrap limitation #16423 was admin-merged past. Everything else (typecheck, lint, unit, integration, ratchet) is green.

b3:T1058,Relates to #15347, #16194, #16079, and #16228.

## What changed

- routes every Cloudflare Worker-to-agent API, bridge, SSE, workflow, wallet, LifeOps, snapshot, and restore request through the configured control-plane origin
- carries the logical `<agent-id>.<agent-domain>` identity in trusted `X-Forwarded-Host` / `X-Forwarded-Proto` headers so the control-plane router selects the intended agent
- validates the control-plane host, base domain, agent UUID, and relative path before network I/O
- strips caller-controlled host, forwarding, and auth headers; restores the stored agent credential; and disables redirects so custom auth headers cannot follow a cross-origin redirect
- fails closed before fetch when routing configuration or the stored agent credential is missing
- preserves the existing direct tailnet/public routing behavior outside Cloudflare Workers
- declares the production default origin while retaining explicit staging and production environment bindings

## Why

The real managed-agent canary provisioned a healthy dedicated agent, but the nested same-zone Worker fetch to its UUID wildcard hostname failed at Cloudflare with HTTP 525 before nginx, the router, authentication, Headscale, or the model runtime received HTTP. Worker subrequests must target the fixed control-plane origin and pass the logical agent hostname separately.

## Validation

- exact head: `1a8277c542d5e90c58ec6ea61587f95036718a70`
- seven changed sandbox suites: 141 passed, 0 failed, 609 assertions
- `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck`
- `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/api typecheck` (includes production Worker bundle dry-run)
- explicit staging Wrangler dry-run confirms `AGENT_ROUTER_ORIGIN_HOST=eliza-staging-1.elizacloud.ai`
- production dry-run confirms `AGENT_ROUTER_ORIGIN_HOST=eliza-production-1.elizacloud.ai`
- Biome, `git diff --check`, and `bun run audit:error-policy-ratchet`

## Deployment and post-merge acceptance

The repository's canonical Cloud deployment guard intentionally rejects feature refs, so post-fix infrastructure evidence cannot be captured from this branch. After merge, deploy this exact change to staging through the canonical `develop` workflow, then run #16228's managed dedicated canary and require successful bridge, SSE, real model turn, exact cleanup, and deployed-commit provenance before production promotion.

## Risk

Medium: this centralizes all Worker-side dedicated-agent calls. Invalid configuration now fails closed instead of falling back to the broken wildcard route. No database migration or public API shape changes are included.

b4:Td10,## What & why

The shared `pr-evidence` GitHub release hit GitHub's hard **1000-assets-per-release** cap. `gh release upload pr-evidence` now returns HTTP 422, so `scripts/pr-evidence.mjs` cannot attach media and **no surface-`.tsx` PR can satisfy the `check-pr-evidence` surface-artifact gate**. This is a repo-wide CI-infra blocker for every contributor.

## Fix

- **`scripts/pr-evidence.mjs`** — when the target release has no capacity (≥ 990-asset headroom, or a 422 `file_count` on upload), roll deterministically to the next overflow release: `pr-evidence-2`, `pr-evidence-3`, … Selection (`selectPrEvidenceTarget`) picks the highest-indexed existing release with room and creates the next one only when none has room. Dedup and URL emission span the whole family, so an asset already uploaded to any release keeps its original URL. Behavior is identical when the primary has room.
- **`scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs`** — add `PR_EVIDENCE_RELEASE_RE` recognizing the whole `pr-evidence`/`pr-evidence-N` **download-URL** family as a first-class evidence host. Overflow-release media already validated via the media-extension check; this generalizes the non-media evidence-file recognition too. **Nothing looser**: a surface PR with no real media still fails, a link to the release *page* still fails, arbitrary hosts are not newly accepted.

Two invariants held and proven: (1) the gate stays exactly as strict; (2) existing `pr-evidence` links keep validating.

CI-tooling change (scripts + docs), no UI surface — visual rows are N/A. Backend-logs row points at the test/dry-run output below.

b5:T1a23,Fixes #16065. Reworks the closed #15998 per the reviewer's requests (error-classified retry + committed tests + a clean touched suite).

## Root cause

A committed `cloud-hybrid` mobile runtime re-onboarded on **every** cold launch, via two independent mechanisms:

1. **Persisted-record path** — `reconcileMobileRestoredActiveServer` (`packages/ui/src/state/startup-phase-restore.ts`) accepted the on-device active-server record only for mode `"local"`. For `cloud-hybrid` it returned `null`, which cleared the record + reset `firstRunComplete` on every boot — bouncing a returning hybrid user into onboarding.
2. **Probe path** — when no active server was persisted (e.g. a WebView-storage wipe, #11506), `detectExistingFirstRunConnection` (`packages/ui/src/state/first-run-bootstrap.ts`) gave up after a fixed **3.5s** single-shot, while the on-device agent takes **~30s** to boot. It read the still-booting agent as "no install" → onboarding.

## What the reviewer rejected on #15998 (and this reworks)

The #15998 retry swallowed **every** probe failure as "still booting" and, after up to 45s, converted it into first-run — so an auth `401`, a real `5xx`, or malformed JSON became onboarding instead of surfacing. J4 can't turn arbitrary failures into an "unavailable" result. The reviewer also required committed retry-loop tests, a symmetric Android below-floor diagnostic for cloud-hybrid, and a clean touched suite.

## The fix

- **reconcile** — accept the on-device record for **both** committed on-device modes (`local` + `cloud-hybrid`) through one shared predicate `isCommittedOnDeviceMobileRuntimeMode` (`packages/ui/src/first-run/mobile-runtime-mode.ts`); non-agent modes (`cloud`/`remote-mac`/`tunnel-to-mobile`) are still dropped as stale.
- **error-classified retry** — `detectExistingFirstRunConnection` gains `waitForBootingAgent`. It keeps retrying **only** while the agent is still booting, classified by the new exported `isBootingAgentProbeError`, which reuses the exact transient band `startup-phase-poll.ts` already trusts: `ApiError` kind `network`/`timeout`, or HTTP `502`/`503`/`504` (the iOS/Android native transports answer a not-yet-ready kernel with a structured `503`). Any **genuine** fault — auth `401/403`, a real `500`, `parse` on malformed JSON, or an unrecognized throw — is **rethrown**, never retried into first-run. The fresh-install single-shot is unchanged (instant onboarding).
- **restore boundary (J1)** — the probe call site catches a rethrown genuine fault and, because the committed install exists, routes to restore-as-detected-install so the **polling-backend phase surfaces the real error** through its designed timeout/error states, instead of masking it as onboarding.
- **Android** — `shouldAutoStart` now pre-warms the bundled agent at boot for `cloud-hybrid` (4 GB hybrid floor) as well as `local` (8 GB), reusing the existing `allowsAgentStartForRuntimeMode`; the fail-loud below-floor diagnostic now covers **both** modes symmetrically, so a below-floor cloud-hybrid is refused observably rather than leaving the renderer to wait out the 45s probe.

## Tests — real functions, injected transports (nothing under test is mocked)

- `packages/ui/src/state/first-run-bootstrap.test.ts` (new, 12 tests): classifier table (transient vs genuine vs unknown); repeated-unreachable → eventual success (no re-onboard); timeout termination → onboard; cancellation stops probing after the race settles; genuine `401`/`parse` faults **surfaced (thrown)**, not retried; fresh-install single-shot never waits and even an auth error resolves instantly.
- `packages/ui/src/state/persistence-cloud-active-server.test.ts` (+3): cloud-hybrid and local on-device records kept; non-agent modes (`cloud`/`remote-mac`) still dropped.
- `packages/app-core/.../ElizaAgentAutostartPolicyTest.java`: cloud-hybrid autostarts at the 4 GB floor, local at 8 GB, below-floor refused, fresh install never; `isOnDeviceAgentRuntimeMode` predicate.

b6:T1405,"## Problem\n\nWith per-task GROUP rooms on by default, the ACP session's `metadata.roomId` is the minted task-room UUID while the user types in the origin connector channel. Every consumer that read the raw `roomId` diverged from the router's origin ladder:\n\n- **Mid-task user follow-ups were silently never forwarded** to the running sub-agent — `boundToRoom` in `active-session-forward.ts` matched only `meta.roomId`/`meta.threadRoomId`, neither of which is the origin room once a task room is minted.\n- **Live progress (acks/heartbeats/narration) targeted the minted GROUP room**, which is not a live connector channel; sends failed and were demoted to debug after the first warn — the user often saw nothing between spawn and completion.\n- **`QUESTION_FOR_TASK_CREATOR` landed only in the task room** — a blocked sub-agent's question never directly reached the user.\n- Multi-session rooms forwarded only to the **first** bound session; inbox overflow **silently dropped** the oldest queued message; and a bind-retry-window race between `SubAgentRouter` and `SwarmCoordinatorService` could double-post or lose a completion.\n\n## Fix\n\n- New `session-room-binding.ts`: shared origin ladder (`resolveOriginRoomId`) + bound-room set (`sessionBoundRoomIds`) used by the forward handler and the progress hook, with an anti-drift contract test pinning agreement with the router's `readOrigin` across all metadata shapes.\n- Forward handler binds on the full session room set (task/origin/source/thread) and delivers to **all** bound live sessions with a per-session interruption decision.\n- Progress hook resolves emit targets through the origin ladder, so acks/heartbeats/narration reach the user's actual channel.\n- `QUESTION_FOR_TASK_CREATOR` routes to BOTH the task room (planner context) and the origin room (user-facing leg), deduped when they are equal.\n- `SubAgentInbox` overflow surfaces dropped counts via an observer wired to `logger.warn` + `runtime.reportError` — no silent drops.\n- Swarm coordinator cede decision uses a **receipt-time router-activity snapshot** instead of a post-await `isActive()` read. (Ceding to a still-binding router would lose the event outright — ACP fan-out has no replay — so the snapshot closes the double-post race in both directions; regression tests cover bind-during-gap and unbind-during-gap.)\n\n## Evidence\n\n- Targeted suites (6 files): **191/191 passed**. b7:T10aa,## Summary

- preserve every explicit `max_tokens` value as the provider-facing output/spend ceiling, including reasoning and Anthropic extended-thinking models
- retain the 4096 reasoning safety default only when callers omit `max_tokens`
- classify empty-but-billed results as `finish_reason: "length"` consistently in streaming and non-streaming responses
- add regression coverage for named, catalog-detected, and Anthropic reasoning paths
- rebase onto `origin/develop` (Cerebras `reasoning_effort` support, #16087) and fix a `mock.module` leak in `chat-completions-explicit-max-tokens-route.test.ts` that crashed the `test:cloud` batch containing it and `chat-completions-tool-choice.test.ts`

## Reproduction

Before this change, `computeEffectiveMaxTokens(10, null, "minimax/minimax-m3")` returned `4096`, and an Anthropic request with `max_tokens: 1000` plus an 8000-token thinking budget returned `12096`. The gateway therefore authorized more provider output and spend than the caller requested.

Separately, `bun run test:cloud`'s batching runs `chat-completions-explicit-max-tokens-route.test.ts` and `chat-completions-tool-choice.test.ts` in the same bun process. The former's top-level `mock.module()` calls narrowly overrode `modelUsesReasoningTokens` and `canonicalizeCerebrasModelId` instead of relying on the spread real exports, and bun's `mock.module` restore does not propagate to `route.ts`'s already-bound imports when both files load in one process — so the narrow mocks leaked into the sibling file's Cerebras reasoning assertions and crashed the batch.

## Verification

- `bun run test:cloud` (full local batch run, 10 batches) — batch 10/10 fails on `Cannot find module '@elizaos/plugin-sql'` in `packages/cloud/services/agent-server` webhook/infra/script tests unrelated to this change (pre-existing worktree module-resolution gap, not touched by this PR); the batch containing the two files this PR modifies passed cleanly.

b8:Tf46,# Relates to

Found while auditing cloud hot-path caches for the negative-caching / stale-preservation class (the pricing-catalog analog of #16162). No existing issue.

- [x] Targets `develop`, rebased on latest `origin/develop`, zero conflicts.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the change from the evidence below without reading the code.

# Risks

Low. Narrows one function's error handling: a failed catalog fetch now throws instead of returning `[]`, and its single caller catches the cold-miss case. Success path and "no provider configured" (a real empty catalog) are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`fetchBitRouterModelCatalog` swallowed a failed OpenRouter catalog fetch and returned `[]`. `getWithSWR` caches whatever the revalidate function returns (its background revalidation writes on any non-null result, and `[] !== null`), so a **transient upstream blip overwrote the cached model catalog with an empty one for the whole TTL** — model listing/routing then sees no bitrouter models until the next successful revalidation, and the last-good stale catalog is lost.

The fix lets the fetch throw on failure (invalid shape / network error), so `getWithSWR` keeps serving the last-good stale catalog instead of caching `[]` — its background revalidation only writes on a non-throwing result. `getCachedBitRouterModelCatalog` catches the cold-miss case (no stale to serve) and degrades to `[]` rather than throwing into the hot path. "No provider configured" stays a real, cacheable empty catalog.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (resilience; non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/model-catalog.ts` — `fetchBitRouterModelCatalog` now throws on failure and `getCachedBitRouterModelCatalog` catches the cold miss. Pinned by `model-catalog.test.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

None — unit tests cover it.
